## Endnotes / 尾注

===Original===

1. This title amongst the Muhammadans comprehends the concrete character of prophet, priest, and king; and is used to signify “the Vicar of God on earth.” —﻿Habesci’s *State of the Ottoman Empire*, p. 9. Herbelot, p. 985 ↩︎
2. The author of Nighiaristan hath preserved a fact that supports this account; and there is no history of Vathek, in which his “terrible eye” is not mentioned. ↩︎
3. This Caliph was eminent above all others for temperance and self-denial; insomuch, that he is believed to have been raised to Muhammad’s bosom, as a reward for his abstinence in an age of corruption. —﻿Herbelot, p. 690 ↩︎
4. A city of the Babylonian Irak, supposed to have stood on the site where Nimrod erected his tower. Khondemir relates, in his life of Motassem, that this prince, to terminate the disputes which were perpetually happening between the inhabitants of Baghdad and his Turkish slaves, withdrew from thence; and, having fixed on a situation in the plain of Catoul, there founded Samarah. He is said to have had in the stables of this city a hundred and thirty thousand pied horses; each of which carried, by his order, a sack of earth to a place he had chosen. By this accumulation, an elevation was formed that commanded a view of all Samarah, and served for the foundation of his magnificent palace. —﻿Herbelot, p. 752, 808, 985. *Anecdotes Arabes*, p. 413 ↩︎

===Chinese===

1. 这一尊号在穆罕默德信徒（Muhammadans）那里兼摄先知、祭司与君王三重身份，并用以指称「上帝在尘世的代理人」。—﻿Habesci’s *State of the Ottoman Empire*, p. 9. Herbelot, p. 985 ↩︎
2. 《尼吉阿里斯坦》（Nighiaristan）的作者保存了一则可为此说作证的史实；凡记述瓦提克（Vathek）史事的著作，没有一部不提到他的「可怖之眼」。 ↩︎
3. 这位哈里发以节制与克己著称，为历代哈里发之冠；人们甚至相信，作为他在一个腐化时代持守禁欲的报偿，他已被擢升到穆罕默德（Muhammad）的怀抱之中。—﻿Herbelot, p. 690 ↩︎
4. 这是巴比伦伊拉克（Babylonian Irak）地区的一座城市，据说坐落在宁录（Nimrod）筑塔的故址上。洪德米尔（Khondemir）在其所撰穆塔西姆（Motassem）传中记述：这位君王为平息巴格达（Baghdad）居民与他的突厥奴隶之间层出不穷的争执，撤离了该城，在卡图勒平原（the plain of Catoul）上选定一处所在，创建了萨迈拉（Samarah）。据说他在该城的马厩中养有杂色马十三万匹，每匹都奉他之命，向他择定的地方驮去一袋土。如此堆积起来，便形成一座俯瞰萨迈拉全城的高丘，充作了他那座壮丽宫殿的地基。—﻿Herbelot, p. 752, 808, 985. *Anecdotes Arabes*, p. 413 ↩︎

===Original===

5. The Virgins of Paradise, called, from their large black eyes, *Hur al oyun*. An intercourse with these, according to the institution of Muhammad, is to constitute the principal felicity of the faithful. Not formed of clay, like mortal women, they are deemed in the highest degree beautiful, and exempt from every inconvenience incident to the sex. —﻿Al Koran; passim ↩︎
6. *Genn* or *Ginn*, in the Arabic, signifies a Genius or Demon﻿—a being of a higher order, and formed of more subtle matter than man. According to Oriental mythology, the Genii governed the world long before the creation of Adam. The Muhammadans regarded them as an intermediate race between angels and men, and capable of salvation: whence Muhammad pretended a commission to convert them. Consonant to this, we read that, “When the servant of God stood up to invoke him, it wanted little but that the Genii had pressed on him in crowds, to hear him rehearse the Koran.” —﻿Herbelot, p. 357. Al Koran ch. 72 ↩︎
7. Dives of this kind are frequently mentioned by Eastern writers. Consult their tales in general, and especially those of “The Fisherman,” “Aladdin,” and “The Princess of China.” ↩︎
8. As it was the employment of the black eunuchs to wait upon, and guard the sultanas, to the general superintendence of the Harem was particularly committed to their chief. —﻿Habesci’s *State of the Ottoman Empire*, p. 155﻿–﻿6 ↩︎

===Chinese===

5. 乐园的诸处女即天女（Houris），因黑眸硕大而被称为 *Hur al oyun*（阿拉伯语，意即「黑眸佳人」）。按照穆罕默德的教制，与她们亲近将构成信士们最主要的福乐。她们不像尘世女子那样由泥土造成，故被认为美到极处，且全然免于女性与生俱来的一切不便。—﻿Al Koran; passim ↩︎
6. 阿拉伯语中的 *Genn* 或 *Ginn*，意指神灵或魔怪——一种品阶高于人类、由比人更精微的物质构成的存在。据东方神话，早在亚当（Adam）被造之前，精灵们就已统治世界。穆罕默德信徒把他们看作天使与人类之间的一个中间族类，认为他们也能得救：穆罕默德正是据此自称奉有教化他们的使命。与此相合，我们读到：「当上帝的仆人起来祈求他时，众精灵几乎要成群拥挤到他跟前，听他诵读《古兰经》。」—﻿Herbelot, p. 357. Al Koran ch. 72 ↩︎
7. 东方作家时常提到这类迪夫（Dives）。可普遍参看他们的故事，尤其是《渔夫》《阿拉丁》与《中国公主》诸篇。 ↩︎
8. 黑太监的职分既是侍奉并护卫苏丹妃嫔，后宫的总管之责便特别托付给他们的首领。—﻿Habesci’s *State of the Ottoman Empire*, p. 155﻿–﻿6 ↩︎

===Original===

9. This was both the supreme council, and court of justice, at which the Caliphs of the race of the Abassides assisted in person to redress the injuries of every appellant. —﻿Herbelot, p. 298 ↩︎
10. *Vazir*, *Vezir*, or as we express it, Vizier, literally signifies a porter; and by metaphor, the minister who bears the principal burden of the state. ↩︎
11. By this appellation was distinguished the monarch of that species of beings, whom the Arabians denominate *Gian* or *Ginn*, that is, “Genii”; and the Tarik Thabari, *Peres*, *Feez*, or “Faeries.” ↩︎
12. The stream thus denominated flows near the city of Schiraz. Its waters are uncommonly pure and limpid, and their banks swarded with the finest verdure. ↩︎

===Chinese===

9. 这既是最高议事会，又是最高法庭；阿拔斯王族（the race of the Abassides）的哈里发们亲临其间，为每一个申诉者昭雪冤屈。—﻿Herbelot, p. 298 ↩︎
10. *Vazir*、*Vezir*，或照我们的写法，维齐尔，字面意思是「挑夫」；引申为肩负国家主要重任的大臣。 ↩︎
11. 这一称呼所标别的，是那一族类之王：阿拉伯人称之为 *Gian* 或 *Ginn*，即「精灵」；而塔里克·塔巴里（Tarik Thabari）则称之为 *Peres*、*Feez*，即「仙灵」。 ↩︎
12. 得此名的那条溪在设拉子（Schiraz）城附近流过。溪水异常纯净清澈，两岸覆着最细美的绿茵。 ↩︎

===Original===

13. Those among the Muhammadans who were bred to the law had this title; and from their order the judges of cities and provinces were taken. ↩︎
14. The heinousness of Vathek’s profanation can only be judged of by an orthodox Mussulman; or one who recollects the ablution and prayer indispensably required on the exoneration of nature. —﻿Sale’s *Prelim. Disc.* p. 139. Al Koran, ch. 4. Habesci’s *State of the Ottoman Empire*, p. 93 ↩︎
15. This mountain, which in reality is no other than Caucasus, was supposed to surround the earth, like a ring encompassing a finger. The sun was believed to rise from one of its eminences (as over Octa, by the Latin poets) and to set on the opposite; whence “from Kaf to Kaf,” signified from one extremity of the earth to the other. ↩︎
16. This is that wonderful bird of the East concerning which so many marvels are told. It was not only endowed with reason, but possessed also the knowledge of every language. This creature relates of itself, that it had seen the great revolution of seven thousand years, twelve times, commence and close; and, that in its duration, the world had been seven times void of inhabitants, and as often replenished. The Simurgh is represented as a great friend to the race of Adam, and not less inimical to the Dives. ↩︎

===Chinese===

13. 穆罕默德信徒中受法学教育出身者得此头衔；城市与各省的法官即从他们这一阶层中选任。 ↩︎
14. 瓦提克这番渎圣之举何其穷凶极恶，唯有正统的穆斯林（Mussulman）、或是记得履行自然之需后必不可少的沐浴与祈祷的人，方能评判。—﻿Sale’s *Prelim. Disc.* p. 139. Al Koran, ch. 4. Habesci’s *State of the Ottoman Empire*, p. 93 ↩︎
15. 这座山其实不是别的，正是高加索（Caucasus）山；人们设想它环抱大地，好比戒指箍着手指。太阳被认为从它的一处峰峦升起——如同拉丁诗人笔下太阳升起于奥克塔山（Octa）——又落在对面的一处；因此「从卡夫到卡夫」（from Kaf to Kaf）一语，便意谓从大地的一端到另一端。 ↩︎
16. 这就是那只有无数奇谈流传的东方神鸟。它不仅禀有理性，还通晓各种语言。据这生灵自述，它曾目睹七千年一度的大轮回十二番肇始与收束；在这漫长岁月里，世界曾七度杳无人迹，又七度重新住满。西摩格（Simurgh）被描绘为亚当后裔的挚友，对迪夫的敌意也不稍减。 ↩︎

===Original===

17. These were a kind of Medusa, or Lamia, supposed to be the most terrible and cruel of all the orders of the Dives. —﻿Herbelot, p. 66 ↩︎
18. This word signifies properly a liar and imposter, but is applied by Muhammadan writers to their Antichrist. He is described as having but one eye and eyebrow, and on his forehead the radicals of *cafer*, or infidel, are said to be impressed. ↩︎
19. These were a sort of men amongst the Muhammadans who abandoned father and mother, wife and children, relations and possessions, to wander through the world, under a pretence of religion, entirely subsisting on the fortuitous bounty of those they had the address to dupe. —﻿Herbelot, Suppl. p. 204 ↩︎
20. A body of religionists who were also called *Abdals*, and pretended to be inspired with the most enthusiastic raptures of divine love. They were regarded by the vulgar as saints. —﻿Olearius, Tom. I p. 971. Herbelot, p. 5 ↩︎

===Chinese===

17. 这是一类美杜莎（Medusa）或拉弥亚（Lamia）式的魔物，被视为迪夫诸支系中最可怖、最残忍者。—﻿Herbelot, p. 66 ↩︎
18. 这个词的本义是撒谎者和骗子，但穆罕默德信徒的作家们用它来指他们的敌基督（Antichrist）。据描述，他独眼独眉；其额上据说烙着 *cafer*（即「异教徒」一词）的词根。 ↩︎
19. 这是穆罕默德信徒中的一类人：他们抛开父母、妻子儿女、亲族与财产，假宗教之名浪游天下，全凭他们有本事哄骗之人偶然的慷慨施舍度日。—﻿Herbelot, Suppl. p. 204 ↩︎
20. 一伙教徒，又称 *Abdals*（阿布达尔，意为「代行者」），自称胸中充满了对神之爱最狂热的销魂陶醉。他们被俗众奉为圣者。—﻿Olearius, Tom. I p. 971. Herbelot, p. 5 ↩︎

===Original===

21. The term *dervise* signifies a poor man, and is the general appellation by which a religious sect amongst the Muhammadans is named. ↩︎
22. These constituted the principal caste of the Indians, according to whose doctrines Brahma, from whom they are called, is the first of the three created beings by whom the world was made. This Brahma is said to have communicated to the Indians four books, in which all the sciences and ceremonies of their religion are comprised. ↩︎
23. This sect were a kind of religious anchorites, who spent their whole lives in the severest austerities and mortification. ↩︎
24. The word *Peri*, in the Persian language, signifies that beautiful race of creatures which constitutes the link between angels and men. ↩︎

===Chinese===

21. *dervise*（托钵僧）一词意为「穷人」，是穆罕默德信徒中一个教门的统称。 ↩︎
22. 他们构成印度人的首要种姓，即婆罗门；依他们的教义，其名号所自出的梵天（Brahma）是造成世界的三神之首。据说这位梵天授予印度人四部典籍，他们宗教的全部学问与仪式尽括其中。 ↩︎
23. 这一教派是一类宗教隐修士，毕生于最严酷的苦行与肉身的磨折中度过。 ↩︎
24. *Peri*（佩丽）一词在波斯语中，指那个美丽的族类——她们构成天使与人之间的链环。 ↩︎

===Original===

25. This was the Arabian name of the Queen of Sheba, who went from the South to hear the wisdom and admire the glory of Solomon. The Koran represents her as a worshipper of fire. Solomon is said not only to have entertained her with the greatest magnificence, but also to have raised her to his bed and his throne. —﻿Al Koran, ch. 27, and Sale’s notes. Herbelot, p. 182 ↩︎
26. This monster is represented as a fierce flying hydra, and belongs to the same class with the *Rakshe*, whose ordinary food was serpents and dragons; the *Soham*, which had the head of a horse, with four eyes, and the body of a flame-coloured dragon; the *Syl*, a basilisk with a face resembling the human, but so tremendous that no mortal could bear to behold it; the *Ejder*, and others. See these respective titles in *Richardson’s Dictionary*, Persian, Arabic and English. ↩︎

===Chinese===

25. 这是示巴女王（Queen of Sheba）的阿拉伯名字。她从南方前来，要听所罗门（Solomon）的智慧，瞻仰他的荣光。《古兰经》把她写成一个拜火的人。据说所罗门不仅以极尽豪奢之礼款待她，还把她扶上了自己的床榻与宝座。—﻿Al Koran, ch. 27, and Sale’s notes. Herbelot, p. 182 ↩︎
26. 这头怪物被描绘为一头凶猛善飞的海德拉（hydra），与下列诸怪同属一类：*Rakshe*（拉克什）——日常以蛇和龙为食；*Soham*（索哈姆）——马首四目，躯体是火焰色的龙；*Syl*（西尔）——一种蛇怪，面容略似人形，却狰狞得没有一个凡人敢于正视；以及 *Ejder*（埃杰德尔）等等。诸词条可分别于理查森《波斯、阿拉伯、英语词典》（*Richardson’s Dictionary*, Persian, Arabic and English）中查得。 ↩︎

## Preface / 序

===Original===

The original of the following story, with some others of a similar kind, collected in the East by a man of letters, was communicated to the editor above three years ago. The pleasure he received from the perusal of it induced him at that time to transcribe, and since to translate it. How far the copy may be a just representation it becomes not him to determine. He presumes however to hope that if the difficulty of accommodating our English idioms to the Arabic, preserving the correspondent tones of a diversified narration, and discriminating the nicer touches of character through the shades of foreign manners be duly considered, a failure in some points will not preclude him from all claim to indulgence; especially if those images, sentiments, and passions, which being independent of local peculiarities, may be expressed in every language, shall be found to retain their native energy in our own.

===Chinese===

下面这篇故事的原作，连同另外几篇同类之作，是一位文人在东方搜集所得，三年多以前送到了编者手中。编者披读之下深得其趣，当时便将它抄录下来，其后又着手翻译。这个抄本在多大程度上如实呈现了原貌，轮不到他本人来断定。不过他仍冒昧希望：倘使人们对其中种种困难有充分的体谅——既要使我们的英语习语迁就阿拉伯语，又要在变化多端的叙述中保持彼此相应的声调，还要透过异域风俗的层层阴影辨析人物刻画中更精微的笔触——那么，纵有几处闪失，也不至于使他全然无缘于读者的宽宥；尤其是，倘若那些不系于一方风土、因而可形诸任何语言的意象、情感与激情，在我们的语言中依然保有它们固有的气韵，那就更其如此了。

===Original===


Vathek, ninth Caliph1 of the race of the Abassides, was the son of Motassem, and the grandson of Haroun Al Raschid. From an early accession to the throne, and the talents he possessed to adorn it, his subjects were induced to expect that his reign would be long and happy. His figure was pleasing and majestic; but when he was angry, one of his eyes became so terrible2 that no person could bear to behold it; and the wretch upon whom it was fixed instantly fell backward, and sometimes expired. For fear, however, of depopulating his dominions, and making his palace desolate, he but rarely gave way to his anger.

Being much addicted to women, and the pleasures of the table, he sought by his affability to procure agreeable companions; and he succeeded the better, as his generosity was unbounded and his indulgences unrestrained; for he was by no means scrupulous: nor did he think, with the Caliph Omar Ben Abdalaziz,3 that it was necessary to make a hell of this world to enjoy Paradise in the next.

He surpassed in magnificence all his predecessors. The palace of Alkoremmi, which his father Motassem had erected on the hill of Pied Horses, and which commanded the whole city of Samarah,4 was in his idea far too scanty: he added, therefore, five wings, or rather other palaces, which he destined for the particular gratification of each of his senses.

===Chinese===


瓦提克（Vathek）——阿拔斯王族（Abassides）的第九代哈里发——是穆塔西姆（Motassem）之子、哈伦·阿尔·拉希德（Haroun Al Raschid）之孙。他冲龄践祚，兼有足以装点御座的才华，臣民们由是预期他的统治必将长久而昌隆。他身形俊美，气度威严；然而一旦动怒，他的一只眼睛便会变得无比可怖，无人敢于直视；被这只眼睛盯住的不幸者当即仰面栽倒，有时竟就此气绝。不过，他惟恐疆域之内人丁凋零、宫闱为之空寂，所以很少放任怒火发作。

他耽于女色，又嗜口腹之乐，便以和蔼可亲的姿态罗致称心的伴侣；他出手慷慨漫无止境，纵情享乐毫无羁束，因此收效愈发显著；因为他绝非拘谨小节之人：也不像哈里发奥马尔·本·阿卜杜勒-阿齐兹（Omar Ben Abdalaziz）那样想——以为须把今世化作地狱，方能在来世安享天堂。

他的豪奢超越历代先君。其父穆塔西姆在杂色马山（hill of Pied Horses）上营建的阿尔科雷米宫（Alkoremmi），俯瞰萨迈拉（Samarah）全城，在他看来却还远远不够宽敞：于是他又增建了五座翼殿——不如说五座宫殿——专门用来分别满足他每一种官能的享乐。

===Original===


In the first of these were tables continually covered with the most exquisite dainties, which were supplied both by night and by day according to their constant consumption; whilst the most delicious wines, and the choicest cordials, flowed forth from a hundred fountains, that were never exhausted. This palace was called “The Eternal, or Unsatiating Banquet.”

The second was styled “The Temple of Melody, or the Nectar of the Soul.” It was inhabited by the most skilful musicians and admired poets of the time, who not only displayed their talents within, but dispersing in bands without, caused every surrounding scene to reverberate their songs, which were continually varied in the most delightful succession.

The palace named “The Delight of the Eyes, or the Support of Memory,” was one entire enchantment. Rarities collected from every corner of the earth were there found in such profusion as to dazzle and confound, but for the order in which they were arranged. One gallery exhibited the pictures of the celebrated Mani; and statues that seemed to be alive. Here a well-managed perspective attracted the sight; there, the magic of optics agreeably deceived it; whilst the naturalist, on his part, exhibited in their several classes the various gifts that heaven had bestowed on our globe. In a word, Vathek omitted nothing in this particular that might gratify the curiosity of those who resorted to it, although he was not able to satisfy his own; for he was, of all men, the most curious.

“The Palace of Perfumes,” which was termed likewise, “The Incentive to Pleasure,” consisted of various halls, where the different perfumes which the earth produces were kept perpetually burning in censers of gold. Flambeaus and aromatic lamps were here lighted in open day; but the too-powerful effects of this agreeable delirium might be avoided by descending into an immense garden, where an assemblage of every fragrant flower diffused through the air the purest odours.

===Chinese===


第一座宫殿里，案上永远罗列着最精美的珍馐，昼夜随耗随补，源源不绝；最甘醇的美酒与最上等的香醴，从一百眼永不枯竭的泉中滚滚流出。这座宫殿名为「永恒盛宴宫」，又称「永不餍足之宴」。

第二座称作「妙音神殿」，又名「灵魂之甘露」。当时最娴熟的乐师与最受推崇的诗人居住其间；他们不仅在殿内施展才艺，还结成歌班散布四郊，使周围每一处景致都回荡着他们的歌声，曲调更迭不绝，愈出愈妙。

名为「悦目宫」又称「记忆之支柱」的那座宫殿，通体便是一场幻术。天下四隅搜罗来的珍奇之物汇聚于此，多到令人眼花缭乱、神魂失据，幸而陈列次序井然，方才不至如此。一座长廊展出名画家摩尼（Mani）的图画，以及栩栩如生的雕像。这里，经营巧妙的透视牵引着视线；那里，光学的幻术又愉快地欺瞒着它；而博物之士，则按门别类陈列上天赐予我辈尘寰的种种恩物。一言以蔽之，凡能餍足来访者好奇心的东西，瓦提克在这座宫中一无所缺；只是他自己的好奇心却无从满足，因为普天之下，再没有比他更好奇的人了。

「香雾宫」，又名「欢愉之煽动」，由若干殿厅组成，地上所产的各式香料，在纯金的香炉里昼夜燃着。光天化日之下也点起火把与芳香的灯盏；只是这甘美迷醉的药力太过猛烈，欲避其熏灼，可降入一座广阔的花园，那里群芳毕集，将最纯净的芬芳播散在空气之中。

===Original===


The fifth palace, denominated “The Retreat of Joy, or the Dangerous,” was frequented by troops of young females, beautiful as the Houris,5 and not less seducing, who never failed to receive with caresses all whom the Caliph allowed to approach them; for he was by no means disposed to be jealous, as his own women were secluded within the palace he inhabited himself.

Notwithstanding the sensuality in which Vathek indulged, he experienced no abatement in the love of his people, who thought that a sovereign immersed in pleasure was not less tolerable to his subjects than one that employed himself in creating them foes. But the unquiet and impetuous disposition of the Caliph would not allow him to rest there: he had studied so much for his amusement in the lifetime of his father as to acquire a great deal of knowledge, though not a sufficiency to satisfy himself; for he wished to know everything; even sciences that did not exist. He was fond of engaging in disputes with the learned, but liked them not to push their opposition with warmth. He stopped the mouths of those with presents, whose mouths could be stopped; whilst others, whom his liberality was unable to subdue, he sent to prison to cool their blood; a remedy that often succeeded.

Vathek discovered also a predilection for theological controversy; but it was not with the orthodox that he usually held. By this means he induced the zealots to oppose him, and then persecuted them in return; for he resolved, at any rate, to have reason on his side.

===Chinese===


第五座宫殿名为「欢乐幽居宫」，又称「险宫」，成群结队的年轻女子出入其间，个个美如天女（Houris），妖娆也不稍逊；凡哈里发准许亲近她们的人，无不得到她们温存的爱抚；因为他绝无善妒之心——他自己的妻妾，都深锁在他本人所居的宫中。

瓦提克尽管纵情声色，所享的爱戴却未尝稍减；臣民们以为，一位沉溺逸乐的君主，总不比一位专替他们制造仇敌的君主更难忍受。然而哈里发那躁动暴烈的天性不容他就此止步：早在父亲在世之时，他便为消遣而博览群书，获得了大量知识，却不足以餍足自身；因为他想知晓一切，连尚不存在的学问也想知晓。他喜欢与饱学之士论战，却不愿他们据理力争、言辞激切。谁的嘴可以用礼物堵住，他便用礼物堵住谁的嘴；至于那些他的慷慨无从收买的人，他便投入监狱去冷却血液——这剂药方往往奏效。

瓦提克对神学论争也别有偏爱；只是他通常不同正统派交锋。他借此诱使狂信者群起攻之，然后再反过来迫害他们；因为无论如何，他决意要让自己站在理性的一边。

===Original===


The great prophet Muhammad, whose vicars the Caliphs are, beheld with indignation from his abode in the seventh heaven the irreligious conduct of such a vicegerent.

“Let us leave him to himself,” said he to the Genii,6 who are always ready to receive his commands; “let us see to what lengths his folly and impiety will carry him; if he run into excess we shall know how to chastise him. Assist him, therefore, to complete the tower which, in imitation of Nimrod, he hath begun; not, like that great warrior, to escape being drowned, but from the insolent curiosity of penetrating the secrets of heaven: he will not divine the fate that awaits him.”

The Genii obeyed; and when the workmen had raised their structure a cubit in the day time, two cubits more were added in the night. The expedition with which the fabric arose was not a little flattering to the vanity of Vathek. He fancied that even insensible matter showed forwardness to subserve his designs; not considering that the successes of the foolish and wicked form the first rod of their chastisement.

===Chinese===


伟大的先知穆罕默德（Muhammad）——哈里发们本是他的代理人——在第七层天的居所里，目睹这位代理人如此渎神的行径，不禁义愤填膺。

「且由他自己去，」他对精灵（Genii）们说——他们总是随时恭候他的旨意；「且看他的愚妄与不敬要把他带到怎样的地步；他若肆行无度，我们自有惩戒之法。所以要帮助他把那座塔建完；他仿效宁录（Nimrod）已经起了工，却与那位大勇士不同——不是为逃避溺亡，而是出于那妄图窥探上天奥秘的狂悖好奇心：他决计料不到正等着他的命运。」

精灵们遵命而行；工匠们白天把塔身砌高一肘尺，夜里便又自行加高两肘尺。工程进展如此神速，大大慰帖了瓦提克的虚荣。他恍然以为，连无知的木石也争相效命，乐于成全他的宏图；却不曾想到，愚人与恶人的顺境，正是惩戒他们的第一根鞭笞。

===Original===


His pride arrived at its height when, having ascended, for the first time, the eleven thousand stairs of his tower, he cast his eyes below and beheld men not larger than pismires; mountains than shells; and cities than beehives. The idea which such an elevation inspired of his own grandeur completely bewildered him; he was almost ready to adore himself; till lifting his eyes upwards, he saw the stars as high above him as they appeared when he stood on the surface of the earth. He consoled himself, however, for this transient perception of his littleness with the thought of being great in the eyes of the others, and flattered himself that the light of his mind would extend beyond the reach of his sight, and transfer to the stars the decrees of his destiny.

With this view the inquisitive prince passed most of his nights on the summit of his tower, till he became an adept in the mysteries of astrology, and imagined that the planets had disclosed to him the most marvellous adventures, which were to be accomplished by an extraordinary personage, from a country altogether unknown. Prompted by motives of curiosity, he had always been courteous to strangers; but from this instant he redoubled his attention, and ordered it to be announced by sound of trumpet, through all the streets of Samarah, that no one of his subjects, on peril of his displeasure, should either lodge or detain a traveller, but forthwith bring him to the palace.

Not long after this proclamation, there arrived in his metropolis a man so hideous that the very guards who arrested him were forced to shut their eyes as they led him along. The Caliph himself appeared startled at so horrible a visage; but joy succeeded to this emotion of terror when the stranger displayed to his view such rarities as he had never before seen, and of which he had no conception.

===Chinese===


当他初次登上高塔的一万一千级台阶，俯瞰下方，只见人比蚂蚁还小，山不比贝壳大，城不过蜂巢一般，他的骄矜至此达到了顶点。这样的高度令他自觉伟大非凡，简直神魂颠倒；他几乎要膜拜起自己来；直到举目上望，才见群星依旧高悬，与他站在地面时所见一般无二。不过，对这转瞬即逝的渺小之感，他随即以「在他人眼中自己何其伟大」的念头聊以自慰，还自欺欺人地想：他心智的光芒必将超越目力所及，把命运的法度加于星辰之上。

怀着这个念头，这位好奇的君王把大部分夜晚都消磨在塔顶，终于深谙占星术的奥秘，并且想象诸行星已向他昭示种种最为奇异的际遇——这些际遇将由一位非凡的人物来完成，此人来自一个全然未知的国度。出于好奇心的驱使，他一向礼遇异乡人；从此刻起他更加倍殷勤，并下令以号角声传遍萨迈拉全城大街小巷：他的任何臣民，若敢触怒天威，不得收留或滞留任何旅客，而应即刻将其护送入宫。

诏令颁布后不久，京城来了一个人，相貌奇丑无比，连奉命拿他的卫士一路上都不得不闭着眼睛走。哈里发本人乍见这张狰狞的面孔，也不免骇然失色；但当陌生人向他展示那些前所未见、连想都想不到的珍奇之物时，惊惧之情随即化作了狂喜。

===Original===


In reality, nothing was ever so extraordinary as the merchandise this stranger produced. Most of his curiosities, which were not less admirable for their workmanship than their splendour, had besides, their several virtues described on a parchment fastened to each. There were slippers which enabled the feet to walk; knives that cut without the motion of a hand; sabres which dealt the blow at the person they were wished to strike; and the whole enriched with gems that were hitherto unknown.

The sabres, whose blades emitted a dazzling radiance, fixed more than all the Caliph’s attention, who promised himself to decipher at his leisure the uncouth characters engraven on their sides. Without, therefore, demanding their price, he ordered all the coined gold to be brought from his treasury, and commanded the merchant to take what he pleased. The stranger complied with modesty and silence.

Vathek, imagining that the merchant’s taciturnity was occasioned by the awe which his presence inspired, encouraged him to advance, and asked him, with an air of condescension, “Who he was? whence he came? and where he obtained such beautiful commodities?”

===Chinese===


的确，这陌生人的货色，真是天下未有的奇珍。他的大部分玩物，做工之精妙不亚于光彩之夺目，件件还附有一张羊皮纸，上面写明各自的妙用。有能叫双脚自己行走的拖鞋；有不必动手便能切割的刀子；有能替人挥击、指哪打哪的弯刀；件件都镶嵌着世间未见的宝石。

那几柄弯刀，刀锋射出耀眼的光华，比什么都更攫住了哈里发的心思；他打定主意，要从容破解刀身上镌刻的那些古怪文字。因此他并不问价，只命人把库中铸造的金币尽数搬来，任凭商贩随意取用。那陌生人谦卑而沉默地照办了。

瓦提克以为，商贩的缄默是因为慑于他的威仪，便和颜悦色地请他上前，屈尊问道：「你是何人？从何处来？又在何处得来这些美物？」

===Original===


The man, or rather monster, instead of making a reply, thrice rubbed his forehead, which, as well as his body, was blacker than ebony; four times clapped his paunch, the projection of which was enormous; opened wide his huge eyes, which glowed like firebrands; began to laugh with a hideous noise, and discovered his long amber coloured teeth bestreaked with green.

The Caliph, though a little startled, renewed his enquiries, but without being able to procure a reply. At which, beginning to be ruffled, he exclaimed, “knowest thou, varlet, who I am? and at whom thou art aiming thy gibes?” Then addressing his guards, “have ye heard him speak? is he dumb?”

“He hath spoken,” they replied, “though but little.”

===Chinese===


那人——或者说那怪物——非但不答，反而三次摩挲自己的额头（他的额头同他的身子一样，黑得赛过乌木），四次拍打他那凸出得惊人的大肚皮，瞪圆一双火把般灼灼放光的巨眼，发出一阵凄厉的怪笑，龇出一口琥珀色的长牙，牙上斑斑驳驳尽是绿痕。

哈里发虽微感错愕，仍再三相询，却始终讨不到一句回答。他不由得恼将起来，喝道：「你这厮，可知道我是谁？可知道你在讥诮何人？」又转向卫士们：「你们可曾听见他开口？他莫非是个哑巴？」

「他说过话，」卫士们答道，「只是说得极少。」

===Original===


“Let him speak then again,” said Vathek, “and tell me who he is, from whence he came, and where he procured these singular curiosities, or I swear, by the ass of Balaam, that I will make him rue his pertinacity.”

This menace was accompanied by the Caliph with one of his angry and perilous glances, which the stranger sustained without the slightest emotion, although his eyes were fixed on the terrible eye of the prince.

No words can describe the amazement of the courtiers, when they beheld this rude merchant withstand the encounter unshocked. They all fell prostrate with their faces on the ground, to avoid the risk of their lives, and continued in the same abject posture till the Caliph exclaimed in a furious tone:

“Up, cowards! seize the miscreant! see that he be committed to prison, and guarded by the best of my soldiers! Let him, however, retain the money I gave him; it is not my intent to take from him his property, I only want him to speak.”

===Chinese===


「那就让他说，」瓦提克道，「告诉我他是何人，从何处来，这些古怪的珍玩又是从哪里弄到的；否则我以巴兰的驴起誓，定叫他为他的顽固不化后悔莫及。」

哈里发发出这番威吓时，向他投去盛怒之下那致命的一瞥；陌生人坦然受之，纹丝不动，一双眼睛竟直直迎着君王那只可怖的眼睛。

廷臣们眼见这个粗鄙的商贩经受此劫竟面不改色，那份惊骇实非言语所能形容。为保性命，他们个个俯伏在地，脸贴尘土，一直保持着这般狼狈的姿势，直到哈里发怒气冲冲地大喝：

「起来，胆小鬼们！给我拿下这恶棍！把他打入大牢，派我最精锐的士兵看守！不过，我给他的钱仍归他持有；我无意夺他财物，我只要他开口说话。」

===Original===


No sooner had he uttered these words than the stranger was surrounded, pinioned with strong fetters, and hurried away to the prison of the great tower, which was encompassed by seven impalements of iron bars, and armed with spikes in every direction, longer and sharper than spits.

The Caliph, nevertheless, remained in the most violent agitation. He sat down indeed to eat, but of the three hundred covers that were daily placed before him, could taste of no more than thirty-two.

A diet to which he had been so little accustomed, was sufficient of itself to prevent him from sleeping, what then must be its effect when joined to the anxiety that prayed upon his spirits? At the first glimpse of dawn he hastened to the prison, again to importune this intractable stranger; but the rage of Vathek exceeded all bounds on finding the prison empty, the gates burst asunder, and his guards lying lifeless around him. In the paroxysm of his passion he fell furiously on the poor carcases, and kicked them till evening without intermission. His courtiers and viziers exerted their efforts to soothe his extravagance, but finding every expedient ineffectual, they all united in one vociferation:

===Chinese===


话音方落，陌生人便被团团围住，戴上了沉重的镣铐，押往巨塔的监狱；那监狱外围着七道铁栅尖障，四面八方布满铁刺，比烤肉的铁钎更长更尖。

然而哈里发依旧心潮翻涌，激荡难平。他固然坐下进膳，但每日照例摆在他面前的三百道菜，他统共只尝得下三十二道。

这样清减的饮食他素来不惯，单是这一点便足以叫他无法安眠，何况又添上啃噬心神的焦灼，其后果更当如何？天方破晓，他便赶往监狱，再去纠缠那个桀骜不驯的陌生人；不料一见狱中空空如也，大门崩裂，卫士们横尸四周，瓦提克的怒火顿时倾泻而出，一发不可收拾。他在狂怒的发作中凶猛地扑向那些可怜的尸首，拳脚交加，一直踢到天黑，不曾间断。廷臣与维齐尔（vizier）们百般劝解他的癫狂，却发现一切办法尽归无效，于是齐声高呼：

===Original===


“The Caliph is gone mad! the Caliph is out of his senses!”

This outcry, which was soon resounded through the streets of Samarah, at length reached the ears of Carathis, his mother: she flew in the utmost consternation to try her ascendency on the mind of her son. Her tears and caresses called off his attention; and he was prevailed upon by her entreaties to be brought back to the palace.

Carathis, apprehensive of leaving Vathek to himself, caused him to be put to bed; and seating herself by him, endeavoured by her conversation to heal and compose him. Nor could anyone have attempted it with better success; for the Caliph not only loved her as a mother but respected her as a person of superior genius. It was she who had induced him, being a Greek herself, to adopt all the sciences and systems of her country, which good Mussulmans hold in such thorough abhorrence.

===Chinese===


「哈里发疯了！哈里发神志不清了！」

这喊声不久便传遍萨迈拉的大街小巷，终于惊动了他的母亲卡拉希丝（Carathis）；她大惊失色，飞奔而来，要在儿子心上试一试她的威权。她的眼泪与抚爱转移了他的注意；经她恳求，他终于同意被送回宫中。

卡拉希丝不敢把瓦提克单独撇下，命人扶他就寝；自己坐在床边，想凭一番话语抚慰他、使他镇定。这差事也再没有第二个人比她办得更出色；因为哈里发不仅把她当作母亲来爱戴，更把她视为天纵之才来敬重。正是她——她本是希腊（Greek）人——劝他采纳了故国的一切学术与体系，那些虔诚的穆斯林（Mussulman）对之深恶痛绝的学问。

===Original===


Judicial astrology was one of those systems in which Carathis was a perfect adept. She began, therefore, with reminding her son of the promise which the stars had made him; and intimated an intention of consulting them again.

“Alas!” sighed the Caliph, as soon at he could speak, “what a fool have I been! not for the kicks bestowed on my guards, who so tamely submitted to death, but for never considering that this extraordinary man was the same the planets had foretold; whom, instead of ill-treating, I should have conciliated by all the arts of persuasion.”

“The past,” said Carathis, “cannot be recalled; but it behoves us to think of the future: perhaps you may again see the object you so much regret: it is possible the inscriptions on the sabres will afford information. Eat, therefore, and take thy repose, my dear son. We will consider, tomorrow, in what manner to act.”

===Chinese===


推命占星术正是卡拉希丝精通至极的一门学问。于是她先提醒儿子，群星曾对他许下怎样的诺言；并示意自己有意再去请教它们。

「唉！」哈里发一能开口便叹息道，「我真是昏了头！倒不在于踢了那些卫士——他们那样驯顺地就死了——而在于我竟从未想到，这个非凡之人正是行星预言过的那一个；我不该虐待他，而该用尽一切劝说之术去笼络他。」

「往者不可谏，」卡拉希丝说，「但我们必须为将来打算：你或许还能见到你如此追悔的那个人；刀上的铭文也许会透露消息。所以，我亲爱的儿子，吃点东西，好生歇息。明天我们再商量如何行事。」

===Original===


Vathek yielded to her counsel as well as he could, and arose in the morning with a mind more at ease. The sabres he commanded to be instantly brought; and poring upon them through a green glass, that their glittering might not dazzle, he set himself in earnest to decipher the inscriptions; but his reiterated attempts were all of them nugatory: in vain did he beat his head and bite his nails; not a letter of the whole was he able to ascertain. So unlucky a disappointment would have undone him again, had not Carathis, by good fortune, entered the apartment.

“Have patience, son!” said she. “You certainly are possessed of every important science, but the knowledge of languages is a trifle, at best; and the accomplishment of none but a pedant. Issue forth a proclamation that you will confer such rewards as become your greatness upon anyone that shall interpret what you do not understand, and what it is beneath you to learn. You will soon find your curiosity gratified.”

“That may be,” said the Caliph; “but in the meantime I shall be horribly disgusted by a crowd of smatterers, who will come to the trial as much for the pleasure of retailing their jargon as from the hope of gaining the reward. To avoid this evil, it will be proper to add that I will put every candidate to death who shall fail to give satisfaction; for, thank heaven, I have skill enough to distinguish between one that translates and one that invents.”

===Chinese===


瓦提克竭力依从了她的劝告，清晨起身时心境平和了许多。他命人即刻取来那几柄弯刀；隔着一片绿玻璃凝神细看，免得刀光刺目，专心致志地破解铭文；然而屡试屡败，尽属徒劳：他捶头咬指甲也是枉然，通篇竟辨认不出一个字母。这般倒霉的挫折险些又把他击垮，幸而卡拉希丝及时走进房来。

「要有耐心，我的儿！」她说，「你的确掌握了一切重要的学问，可语言之类，充其量不过是雕虫小技；除书呆子外，无人引以为能事。你且颁一道诏令：凡能解读你不解、又不屑于躬亲去学的那类文字者，你将赐予与你的尊贵相称的厚赏。你的好奇心很快便能得到满足。」

「那也未必不行，」哈里发道，「可这期间，我会被一群一知半解之徒恶心坏了；他们前来应征，一半是指望得赏，一半也为过过兜售滥调的瘾。为杜绝此弊，不妨再加一句：凡应试而不能令朕满意者，一律处死；因为，感谢上天，朕的眼力还足以分辨谁是翻译、谁是瞎编。」

===Original===


“Of that I have no doubt,” replied Carathis, “but to put the ignorant to death is somewhat severe, and may be productive of dangerous effects. Content yourself with commanding their beards to be burnt: beards, in a state, are not quite so essential as men.”

The Caliph submitted to the reasons of his mother, and sending for Morakanabad, his prime vizier, said:

“Let the common criers proclaim, not only in Samarah, but throughout every city in my empire, that whosoever will repair hither, and decipher certain characters which appear to be inexplicable, shall experience the liberality for which I am renowned; but that all who fail upon trial shall have their beards burnt off to the last hair. Let them add also, that I will bestow fifty beautiful slaves, and as many jars of apricots from the isle of Kirmith, upon any man that shall bring me intelligence of the stranger.”

===Chinese===


「这一点我不怀疑，」卡拉希丝答道，「但把无知者处死未免过苛，恐怕招致危险的后果。你只需下令烧掉他们的胡须，也便心满意足了：就国家而言，胡须总不像人命那样要紧。」

哈里发依从了母亲的道理，召来首相莫拉卡纳巴德（Morakanabad），说道：

「传令街头宣令官，不仅在萨迈拉，还要遍及朕国中每一座城池昭告：凡愿前来破译某等看似无法解读之文字者，都将领受朕素以闻名的慷慨；但凡应试失败者，胡须将烧得一根不剩。还要加上一条：凡能给朕带来那陌生人消息的人，朕将赐他五十名美貌奴隶，以及同样数目的基尔米斯岛（Kirmith）杏子。」

===Original===


The subjects of the Caliph, like their sovereign, being great admirers of women, and apricots from Kirmith, felt their mouths water at these promises, but were totally unable to gratify their hankering, for no one knew which way the stranger had gone.

As to the Caliph’s other requisition the result was different: the learned, the half-learned, and those who were neither, but fancied themselves equal to both, came boldly to hazard their beards, and all shamefully lost them.

The exaction of these forfeitures, which found sufficient employment for the eunuchs, gave them such a smell of singed hair as greatly to disgust the ladies of the seraglio, and make it necessary that this new occupation of their guardians should be transferred into other hands.

===Chinese===


哈里发的臣民同他们的君王一样，既热衷女色，又酷嗜基尔米斯的杏子，听了这些许诺不禁垂涎欲滴；无奈却全然无从餍足这份馋望，因为谁也不知那陌生人去向何方。

至于哈里发的另一项要求，结果便不同了：饱学的、半瓶醋的，还有两者皆非却自以为不输于前两者的，都壮着胆子来拿胡须冒险，结果无不颜面扫地，尽数输光。

收取这些被罚没的胡须，倒让宦官们有够多的差事可做；只是那股烧焦毛发的气味熏得后宫（seraglio）的女眷们大为作呕，以致他们这些监护人的新差事不得不移交他人之手。

===Original===


At length, however, an old man presented himself, whose beard was a cubit-and-a-half longer than any that had appeared before him. The officers of the palace whispered to each other, as they ushered him in:

“What a pity such a beard should be burnt!”

Even the Caliph, when he saw it, concurred with them in opinion; but his concern was entirely needless. This venerable personage read the characters with facility, and explained them verbatim, as follows:

===Chinese===


然而终于有一位老人前来应征，他的胡须比此前所有来者的都长出一肘尺半。宫中的官员引他进来时，彼此窃窃私语：

「这样一部胡须竟要被烧掉，多可惜啊！」

连哈里发见了，也与他们所见略同；不过他的担心纯属多余。这位可敬的老人毫不费力地读出了那些文字，逐字解释如下：

===Original===


“We were made where everything good is made; we are the least of the wonders of a place where all is wonderful; and deserving the sight of the first potentate on earth.”

“You translate admirably!” cried Vathek. “I know to what these marvellous characters allude. Let him receive as many robes of honour, and thousands of sequins of gold, as he hath spoken words. I am in some measure relieved from the perplexity that embarrassed me!”

Vathek invited the old man to dine, and even to remain some days in the palace. Unluckily for him, he accepted the offer; for the Caliph having ordered him next morning to be called, said:

===Chinese===


「我们诞生于一切美好事物诞生之处；在一处万物皆奇的地方，我们是其中最不足称道的奇观；我们理应受天下第一君主的御览。」

「你译得妙极了！」瓦提克呼道，「我已明白这些奇妙文字所指为何。他每说出一个字，便赐他荣袍一袭、金塞昆（sequin）千枚。方才窘迫着我的难题，总算纾解了几分！」

瓦提克邀老人共膳，甚至请他在宫中盘桓几日。他真不该应承下来；因为次日清晨，哈里发便命人唤他前来，说道：

===Original===


“Read again to me what you have read already; I cannot hear too often the promise that is made me, the completion of which I languish to obtain.”

The old man forthwith put on his green spectacles; but they instantly dropped from his nose, on perceiving that the characters he had read the day preceding, had given place to others of different import.

“What ails you?” asked the Caliph; “and why these symptoms of wonder?”

===Chinese===


「把你昨日读过的再读一遍给我听；许给我的这个诺言，听多少遍也不嫌多；它的兑现，我正望眼欲穿。」

老人当即戴上他的绿眼镜；可是一看之下，眼镜顿时从鼻梁上滑落——他前一日读过的那些文字，已然换成了含义全然不同的字句。

「你怎么了？」哈里发问，「何以这般惊愕？」

===Original===


“Sovereign of the world,” replied the old man, “these sabres hold another language today, from that they yesterday held.”

“How say you?” returned Vathek. “But it matters not! tell me, if you can, what they mean.”

“It is this, my lord,” rejoined the old man: “ ‘Woe to the rash mortal who seeks to know that of which he should remain ignorant and to undertake that which surpasseth his power!’ ”

“And woe to thee!” cried the Caliph, in a burst of indignation: “today thou art void of understanding: begone from my presence, they shall burn but the half of thy beard, because thou wert yesterday fortunate in guessing. My gifts I never resume.”

===Chinese===


「天下的主宰啊，」老人答道，「这些弯刀今日说的是另一番话，与昨天所说的不同。」

「你说什么？」瓦提克应道，「不过也无妨！只要你能，就告诉我它们说的是什么意思。」

「是这样的，陛下，」老人答道：「『祸哉，那鲁莽的凡人，竟妄求知他本该懵然之事、妄为其力不能及之事！』」

「祸哉的该是你！」哈里发勃然怒喝，「今天你全然没了悟性：从朕面前滚出去；他们只烧你半部胡须，因为你昨天侥幸猜中。朕赐出的东西，从不收回。」

===Original===


The old man, wise enough to perceive he had luckily escaped, considering the folly of disclosing so disgusting a truth, immediately withdrew, and appeared not again.

But it was not long before Vathek discovered abundant reason to regret his precipitation; for though he could not decipher the characters himself, yet, by constantly poring upon them, he plainly perceived that they every day changed; and unfortunately no other candidate offered to explain them. This perplexing occupation inflamed his blood, dazzled his sight, and brought on a giddiness and debility that he could not support. He failed not, however, though in so reduced a condition, to be often carried to his tower, as he flattered himself that he might there read in the stars, which he went to consult, something more congruous to his wishes. But in this his hopes were deluded; for his eyes, dimmed by the vapours of his head, began to subserve his curiosity so ill, that he beheld nothing but a thick dun cloud, which he took for the most direful of omens.

Agitated with so much anxiety, Vathek entirely lost all firmness; a fever seized him and his appetite failed. Instead of being one of the greatest eaters, he became as distinguished for drinking. So insatiable was the thirst which tormented him, that his mouth, like a funnel, was always open to receive the various liquors that might be poured into it; and especially cold water, which calmed him more than every other.

===Chinese===


老人够明智，想到自己道破了这样一桩令人厌恶的真相实属愚行，自觉侥幸脱身，当即告退，从此再未露面。

但没过多久，瓦提克便发现，他有充分的理由懊悔自己的操切；因为虽说他自己解不开那些文字，可经他日夜凝视，他分明看出它们天天都在变；不幸又再无应试者前来解读。这桩恼人的营生使他热血奔涌、两眼昏花，招来一阵他支撑不住的眩晕与虚弱。然而纵然虚弱到这般田地，他仍不忘频频被人抬上高塔，还自欺欺人地以为，在他前去请教的星辰之中，或许读得出更称心的消息。但这一指望终归落空；因为他那双被脑中雾气翳住的眼睛，已委实无从为他的好奇心效劳，他望见的只是一团浓黑的浊云，他把这当作最凶不过的预兆。

焦灼交攻之下，瓦提克全然失去了刚强的定力；热症攫住了他，食欲也衰败了。他本是天字第一号的大肚汉，如今却以豪饮著称。折磨着他的干渴如此难餍，以致他的嘴像一只漏斗，永远张着，承接人们灌进去的种种饮品；尤其是冷水，比什么都能叫他安定。

===Original===


This unhappy prince being thus incapacitated for the enjoyment of any pleasure, commanded the palaces of the five senses to be shut up; forebore to appear in public, either to display his magnificence or administer justice; and retired to the inmost apartment of his harem. As he had ever been an indulgent husband, his wives, overwhelmed with grief at his deplorable situation, incessantly offered their prayers for his health, and unremittingly supplied him with water.

In the meantime, the Princess Carathis, whose affliction no words can describe, instead of restraining herself to sobbing and tears, was closeted daily with the Vizier Morakanabad, to find out some cure or mitigation of the Caliph’s disease. Under the persuasion that it was caused by enchantment, they turned over together leaf by leaf, all the books of magic that might point out a remedy; and caused the horrible stranger, whom they accused as the enchanter, to be everywhere sought for with the strictest diligence.

At the distance of a few miles from Samarah stood a high mountain, whose sides were swarded with wild thyme and basil, and its summit overspread with so delightful a plain that it might be taken for the Paradise destined for the faithful. Upon it grew a hundred thickets of eglantine and other fragrant shrubs; a hundred arbours of roses, jessamine, and honeysuckle; as many clumps of orange trees, cedar, and citron; whose branches, interwoven with the palm, the pomegranate, and the vine, presented every luxury that could regale the eye or the taste. The ground was strewed with violets, harebells, and pansies; in the midst of which sprung forth tufts of jonquils, hyacinths, and carnations, with every other perfume that impregnates the air. Four fountains, not less clear than deep, and so abundant as to slake the thirst of ten armies, seemed purposely placed here to make the scene more resemble the garden of Eden, which was watered by the four sacred rivers. Here the nightingale sang the birth of the rose, her well-beloved, and at the same time lamented its short-lived beauty; whilst the turtle deplored the loss of more substantial pleasures and the wakeful lark hailed the rising light that reanimates the whole creation. Here, more than anywhere, the mingled melodies of birds expressed the various passions they inspired; as if the exquisite fruits, which they pecked at pleasure, had given them a double energy.

===Chinese===


这位不幸的君王既然再无从享受任何欢愉，便下令关闭那五座感官之宫；也不再公开露面——既不炫耀他的豪奢，也不主持审判；径自退入后宫（harem）最深的内室。他向来是位宽厚的夫君，妻妾们眼见他境况凄惨，悲痛欲绝，不住地为他的康复祈祷，也不停歇地给他递水。

与此同时，公主卡拉希丝——她的悲恸非言语所能形容——却不肯自限于啜泣与眼泪，而是日日与维齐尔莫拉卡纳巴德密室相商，要为哈里发的痼疾寻一个疗法或缓解之方。他们认定此病系妖术所致，便一页一页翻遍所有可能指出疗方的魔法书；又指认那可怕的陌生人便是施妖术者，派人以最严密的勤勉四处搜捕。

离萨迈拉数里之外，耸立着一座高山，山坡覆满野百里香与罗勒，山顶铺展着一片如此赏心悦目的平野，简直会被当作专为虔信者预备的天堂。其上生着百丛野蔷薇及其他芬芳灌木，百架玫瑰、素馨与忍冬编成的凉棚，还有同样多的橘树、雪松与香橼成林；它们的枝条与棕榈、石榴、葡萄藤交织缠绕，呈现出一应足以娱目适口的种种奢华。地上遍缀紫罗兰、圆叶风铃草与三色堇；其间又丛生出簇簇水仙、风信子与康乃馨，以及一切能使空气馥郁的芬芳。四眼清泉，其澄澈不亚于其深邃，水量之丰足以解十支大军的干渴，仿佛有意设在此处，使这景致更像伊甸园——那座由四条圣河浇灌的园囿。这里，夜莺歌咏她的挚爱玫瑰的诞生，同时又哀叹其红颜易逝；斑鸠悲叹更切实的欢爱之失落；不眠的百灵鸟则迎接那使万物复苏的曙光。在这里，甚于任何别处，百鸟的鸣啭交织，倾诉着它们所引发的种种情热；仿佛它们随意啄食的甘美果实，赋予了它们双倍的活力。

===Original===


To this mountain Vathek was sometimes brought, for the sake of breathing a purer air; and especially, to drink at will of the four fountains, which were reputed in the highest degree salubrious, and sacred to himself. His attendants were his mother, his wives, and some eunuchs, who assiduously employed themselves in filling capacious bowls of rock crystal, and emulously presenting them to him. But it frequently happened that his avidity exceeded their zeal; insomuch that he would prostrate himself upon the ground to lap up the water, of which he could never have enough.

One day when this unhappy prince had been long lying in so debasing a posture, a voice, hoarse but strong, thus addressed him:

“Why assumest thou the function of a dog, oh Caliph, so proud of thy dignity and power?”

===Chinese===


为了呼吸较为纯净的空气，瓦提克时常被抬到这座山上来；尤其是来随意畅饮那四眼清泉——泉水号称最益康健，且是奉献给他本人的圣泉。随侍的是他的母亲、妻妾和几名宦官，他们殷勤不迭地斟满宽大的水晶盏，争先恐后地捧到他面前。但他的贪渴常常盖过他们的热忱，以致他会扑倒在地，像牲口一般舐饮那永远喝不够的水。

一日，这位不幸的君王正以这般卑贱的姿势久卧在地，一个嘶哑而洪亮的声音向他如是说道：

「啊，哈里发，你这般夸耀自己的尊贵与威权，为何却干起狗的营生来了？」

===Original===


At this apostrophe he raised up his head and beheld the stranger that had caused him so much affliction. Inflamed with anger at the sight, he exclaimed:

“Accursed Giaour!7 what comest thou hither to do? is it not enough to have transformed a prince, remarkable for his agility, into one of those leather barrels which the Bedouin Arabs carry on their camels when they traverse the deserts? Perceivest thou not that I may perish by drinking to excess, no less than by a total abstinence?”

“Drink then this draught,” said the stranger, as he presented to him a phial of a red and yellow mixture; “and to satiate the thirst of thy soul as well as of thy body, know that I am an Indian, but from a region of India which is wholly unknown.”

===Chinese===


听了这一声呵斥，他抬起头来，看见的正是那个害他吃了这许多苦头的陌生人。一见此形，他怒火中烧，喝道：

「该咒的吉奥尔（the Giaour）！你到这里来做什么？把一位素以矫健著称的君王，变成贝都因阿拉伯人横穿沙漠时驮在骆驼上的那种皮革水囊，难道还不够吗？你难道看不出，我纵饮无度会送命，滴酒不沾也同样会送命？」

「那么就把这剂药喝下去，」陌生人说着，递给他一小瓶红黄相间的药剂，「既要解你肉体之渴，也要解你灵魂之渴，且知道我是个印度人（Indian），来自印度一个全然无人知晓的地域。」

===Original===


The Caliph, delighted to see his desires accomplished in part, and flattering himself with the hope of obtaining their entire fulfilment, without a moment’s hesitation swallowed the potion, and instantaneously found his health restored, his thirst appeased, and his limbs as agile as ever.

In the transports of his joy, Vathek leaped upon the neck of the frightful Indian, and kissed his horrid mouth and hollow cheeks, as though they had been the coral lips, and the lilies and roses of his most beautiful wives; whilst they, less terrified than jealous at the sight, dropped their veils to hide the blush of mortification that suffused their foreheads.

Nor would the scene have closed here, had not Carathis, with all the art of insinuation, a little repressed the raptures of her son. Having prevailed upon him to return to Samarah, she caused a herald to precede him, whom she commanded to proclaim as loudly as possible:

===Chinese===


哈里发眼见自己的心愿得偿其一，又自期其余也将尽数兑现，便毫不迟疑地一口吞下药水，顿时觉得健康复原、烦渴尽消，四肢矫健一如往昔。

狂喜之下，瓦提克纵身扑上那可怕印度人的脖颈，亲吻他那骇人的嘴和凹陷的双颊，仿佛那是珊瑚般的朱唇、他最美妻妾颊上的百合与玫瑰；而她们见此情景，与其说惊恐，不如说妒恨，纷纷放下面纱，遮住涨满前额的羞恼之红。

若非卡拉希丝使尽婉转的手段，稍稍遏住她儿子的狂态，这场面原不会就此收场。她劝得他回驾萨迈拉，又命一名传令官走在銮驾之前，吩咐他尽量高声宣告：

===Original===


“The wonderful stranger hath appeared again; he hath healed the Caliph; he hath spoken! he hath spoken!”

Forthwith all the inhabitants of this vast city quitted their habitations, and ran together in crowds to see the procession of Vathek and the Indian, whom they now blessed as much as they had before execrated, incessantly shouting,

“He hath healed our sovereign; he hath spoken! he hath spoken!”

===Chinese===


「神奇的陌生人又出现了；他治好了哈里发；他开口说话了！他开口说话了！」

霎时间，这座大城的所有居民都撇下家宅，成群结队奔来观看瓦提克与印度人的仪仗；他们先前怎样咒骂此人，此刻便怎样祝福他，不住地高呼：

「他治好了我们的君王；他开口说话了！他开口说话了！」

===Original===


Nor were these words forgotten in the public festivals, which were celebrated the same evening to testify the general joy, for the poets applied them as a chorus to all the songs they composed.

The Caliph, in the mean while caused the palaces of the senses to be again set open, and as he found himself prompted to visit that of taste, in preference to the rest, immediately ordered a splendid entertainment, to which his great officers and favourite courtiers were all invited. The Indian, who was placed near the prince, seemed to think that as a proper acknowledgment of so distinguished a privilege, he could neither eat, drink, nor talk too much. The various dainties were no sooner served up than they vanished, to the great mortification of Vathek, who piqued himself on being the greatest eater alive, and at this time in particular had an excellent appetite.

The rest of the company looked round at each other in amazement, but the Indian without appearing to observe it, quaffed large bumpers to the health of each of them: sung in a style altogether extravagant; related stories at which he laughed immoderately; and poured forth extemporaneous verses which would not have been thought bad, but for the strange grimaces with which they were uttered. In a word, his loquacity was equal to that of a hundred astrologers; he ate as much as a hundred porters, and caroused in proportion.

===Chinese===


当晚为昭示举城之欢而举办的公共庆典里，这些话也不曾被遗忘；诗人们把它们用作副歌，缀于他们所写的所有歌谣之中。

与此同时，哈里发下令重开那几座感官之宫；他自觉比起其余几座，更想去味觉之宫走走，便即刻吩咐设一席盛筵，遍邀重臣与宠幸的廷臣。印度人被安排在君王近侧；他似乎认为，既蒙这般殊恩，便理当吃个不停、喝个不停、说个不停，方为相称。各色佳肴方一端上便踪影全无，令瓦提克大为屈辱——他素来自负为当世第一饕客，而此际偏偏胃口极佳。

席间余人都惊愕地面面相觑；印度人却浑若不觉，举着大杯为每个人的健康干杯：唱起歌来腔调全然癫狂；讲起故事，又自个儿纵声狂笑；还即兴吟出连篇的诗句，若非吟诵时那副古怪的鬼脸，那些诗本也算不得坏。一言以蔽之，他的饶舌抵得上一百个占星家，饭量抵得上一百个脚夫，酒量亦与之相称。

===Original===


The Caliph, notwithstanding the table had been thirty times covered, found himself incommoded by the voraciousness of his guest, who was now considerably declined in the prince’s esteem. Vathek, however, being unwilling to betray the chagrin he could hardly disguise, said in a whisper to Bababalouk,8 the chief of his eunuchs:

“You see how enormous his performances in every way are; what would be the consequence should he get at my wives? Go! redouble your vigilance, and be sure look well to my Circassians, who would be more to his taste than all of the rest.”

The bird of the morning had thrice renewed his song, when the hour of the divan9 sounded. Vathek, in gratitude to his subjects, having promised to attend, immediately arose from table and repaired thither leaning upon his vizier, who could scarcely support him, so disordered was the poor prince by the wine he had drank, and still more by the extravagant vagaries of his boisterous guest.

===Chinese===


席面虽已撤换过三十次，哈里发仍被这客人的饕餮搅得不堪其扰，此人在君王心中的分量已一落千丈。然而瓦提克不愿泄露他那几乎掩饰不住的愠怒，便低声对宦官之首巴巴巴鲁克（Bababalouk）说道：

「你瞧他方方面面的能耐何等惊人；倘若他挨近了我的妻妾，后果将何如？去吧！加倍的警醒着，务必看牢我的切尔克斯（Circassian）女奴，她们会比其余人更合他的胃口。」

晨鸟已三度重展歌喉，御前会议（divan）的时刻到了。瓦提克为酬答臣民，早已许诺出席，当即离席前往，倚在维齐尔身上——那可怜的君王已被饮下的酒力弄得东倒西歪，更被那位喧闹客人层出不穷的狂态搅得神魂失据，维齐尔几乎扶持不住。

===Original===


The viziers, the officers of the crown, and of the law, arranged themselves in a semicircle about their sovereign, and preserved a respectful silence, whilst the Indian, who looked as cool as if come from a fast, sat down without ceremony on a step of the throne, laughing in his sleeve at the indignation with which his temerity had filled the spectators.

The Caliph, however, whose ideas were confused and his head embarrassed, went on administering justice at haphazard, till at length the prime vizier10 perceiving his situation, hit upon a sudden expedient to interrupt the audience, and rescue the honour of his master, to whom he said in a whisper:

“My lord, the princess Carathis, who hath passed the night in consulting the planets, informs you that they portend you evil; and the danger is urgent. Beware, lest this stranger whom you have so lavishly recompensed for his magical gewgaws, should make some attempt on your life: his liquor, which at first had the appearance of effecting your cure, may be no more than a poison of a sudden operation. Slight not this surmise; ask him, at least, of what it was compounded; whence he procured it; and mention the sabres, which you seem to have forgotten.”

===Chinese===


维齐尔们、朝廷与法司的官员们，在君王面前排成半圆，恭敬地屏息静默；那印度人却面不改色，清凉自若，仿佛刚守完斋一般，毫不客气地在御座的台阶上坐下，眼见自己的孟浪激起众怒，暗自窃笑。

然而哈里发思绪纷乱、头脑昏沉，竟胡乱断起案来；终于，首相见他这般光景，急中生智，想法子中断了听政，保全主上的体面。他俯耳低声道：

「主上，卡拉希丝公主彻夜观测星辰，托臣禀告：星象预示大凶，祸在眉睫。千万提防这陌生人——陛下为他的魔法小玩意儿赏赐得那样阔绰——恐怕他要谋害圣躬：他那药水起初像是治好了陛下，说不定只是一剂发作极快的毒药。切勿轻忽这层疑心；至少也要问问他，那药是用什么配制的、又是从何处得来；还要提起那几柄弯刀——陛下似乎已经把它们忘了。」

===Original===


Vathek, to whom the insolent airs of the stranger became every moment less supportable, intimated to his vizier by a wink of acquiescence, that he would adopt his advice, and at once turning towards the Indian, said:

“Get up and declare in full divan of what drugs the liquor was compounded you enjoined me to take, for it is suspected to be poison; add also the explanation I have so earnestly desired concerning the sabres you sold me, and thus show your gratitude for the favours heaped on you.”

Having pronounced these words in as moderate a tone as a Caliph well could, he waited in silent expectation for an answer; but the Indian, still keeping his seat, began to renew his loud shouts of laughter, and exhibit the same horrid grimaces he had shown them before, without vouchsafing a word in reply. Vathek, no longer able to brook such insolence, immediately kicked him from the steps, instantly descending repeated his blow, and persisted with such assiduity, as incited all who were present to follow his example. Every foot was aimed at the Indian, and no sooner had any one given him a kick than he felt himself constrained to reiterate the stroke.

===Chinese===


那陌生人傲慢无礼的姿态，瓦提克一刻比一刻难以容忍；他向维齐尔眨了眨眼，示意采纳其言，随即转向印度人说道：

「起来，当着满朝御前会议说明：你劝朕饮下的药水究竟用何药配成，因为有人疑它是毒药；再把你卖给朕的那几柄弯刀解释清楚——这是朕悬切以求的；如此也算报答朕堆在你身上的恩宠。」

这几句话，哈里发已尽量用一位哈里发所能有的温和口吻说出，随后便默默静候答复；不料那印度人依旧安坐不动，又放开嗓门狂笑起来，重演他先前亮过的那副狰狞鬼脸，却不屑吐出一个字。瓦提克再也无法容忍这般狂妄，当即把他从台阶上一脚踹下，随即奔下阶去连连补脚，而且踢得那样起劲不懈，惹得在场众人无不效尤。只只脚都朝印度人身上招呼，而且谁只要踢了他一脚，便觉身不由己，非要再踢不可。

===Original===


The stranger afforded them no small entertainment; for being both short and plump, he collected himself into a ball and rolled round on all sides at the blows of his assailants, who pressed after him wherever he turned, with an eagerness beyond conception, whilst their numbers were every moment increasing. The ball, indeed, in passing from one apartment to another, drew every person after it that came in its way, insomuch that the whole palace was thrown into confusion, and resounded with a tremendous clamour. The women of the harem, amazed at the uproar, flew to their blinds to discover the cause, but no sooner did they catch a glimpse of the ball than feeling themselves unable to refrain, they broke from the clutches of their eunuchs, who to stop their flight pinched them till they bled, but in vain; whilst themselves, though trembling with terror at the escape of their charge, were as incapable of resisting the attraction.

The Indian, after having traversed the halls, galleries, chambers, kitchens, gardens, and stables of the palace, at last took his course through the courts, whilst the Caliph, pursuing him closer than the rest, bestowed as many kicks as he possibly could, yet not without receiving now and then one, which his competitors, in their eagerness, designed for the ball.

Carathis, Morakanabad, and two or three old viziers whose wisdom had hitherto withstood the attraction, wishing to prevent Vathek from exposing himself in the presence of his subjects, fell down in his way to impede the pursuit, but he, regardless of their obstruction, leaped over their heads, and went on as before. They then ordered the muezzins to call the people to prayers, both for the sake of getting them out of the way, and of endeavouring by their petitions to avert the calamity; but neither of these expedients was a whit more successful. The sight of this fatal ball was alone sufficient to draw after it every beholder. The muezzins themselves, though they saw it but at a distance, hastened down from their minarets and mixed with the crowd, which continued to increase in so surprising a manner, that scarce an inhabitant was left in Samarah, except the aged, the sick confined to their beds, and infants at the breast, whose nurses could run more nimbly without them. Even Carathis, Morakanabad, and the rest, were all become of the party.

===Chinese===


这陌生人给他们添的乐子委实不小；他生得又矮又肥，索性缩成一团肉球，任凭袭击者的脚踢得四处乱滚；他滚到哪里，人们便发疯般追到哪里，那份热切非意想所能及，人数还在时时刻刻增多。这颗肉球从一间厅堂滚到另一间厅堂，一路把挡道的每一个人都卷进追逐，以致整座宫殿大乱，鼎沸的喧嚷震彻四壁。后宫的女眷们听得喧嚣，惊疑地扑到帘边想看个究竟，可肉球才一入眼，便觉欲罢不能，纷纷挣脱宦官的钳制；宦官们为拦阻她们，掐得她们出了血，也是枉然；而他们自己，虽眼看守护之人逃脱、吓得发抖，却同样抗拒不了那股吸力。

那印度人穿过了宫殿的厅堂、回廊、内室、厨房、花园与马厩，最后一路滚过各重庭院；哈里发追得比谁都紧，拼命地连踢带踹，却也不免间或挨上一脚——那是他那些竞争者情急之下本要赏给肉球的。

卡拉希丝、莫拉卡纳巴德，还有两三位智慧素来足以抵御那股吸力的老维齐尔，为免瓦提克当着臣民之面失态，索性横身扑在他的去路上阻拦追逐；他却不理会他们的拦阻，从他们头顶一跃而过，照旧追赶不误。他们又传宣礼员（muezzin）们召唤百姓礼拜，一来好叫众人让开道路，二来指望借祷告禳解这场灾殃；然而两条计策全都无济于事。只要望见这颗夺命的肉球，便足以叫每一个目击者尾随而去。连宣礼员们自己，虽然只是远远瞥见，也从宣礼塔上匆匆奔下，汇入人潮；人潮膨胀之速令人骇异，萨迈拉城中几乎不留一个居民，只剩垂暮的老人、卧床不起的病者，和怀中的婴儿——奶妈们撇下婴孩，跑起来才更轻快。到后来，连卡拉希丝、莫拉卡纳巴德与其余诸人，也都入了伙。

===Original===


The shrill screams of the females who had broken from their apartments, and were unable to extricate themselves from the pressure of the crowd, together with those of the eunuchs jostling after them, terrified lest their charge should escape from their sight, increased by the execrations of husbands urging forward and menacing both, kicks given and received, stumblings and overthrows at every step, in a word, the confusion that universally prevailed, rendered Samarah like a city taken by storm, and devoted to absolute plunder.

At last the cursed Indian, who still preserved his rotundity of figure, after passing through all the streets and public places, and leaving them empty, rolled onwards to the plain of Catoul, and traversed the valley at the foot of the mountain of the four fountains.

As a continual fall of water had excavated an immense gulf in the valley, whose opposite side was closed in by a steep acclivity, the Caliph and his attendants were apprehensive lest the ball should bound into the chasm, and to prevent it, redoubled their efforts, but in vain. The Indian persevered in his onward direction, and as had been apprehended, glancing from the precipice with the rapidity of lightning, was lost in the gulf below.

===Chinese===


冲出内室的女眷们挤在人潮中脱身不得，发出尖锐的尖叫；宦官们在后推搡追赶，唯恐看守之人逃出视线，也跟着狂呼；丈夫们催赶向前，对两下里恫吓咒骂，更添其乱；加上你踢我一脚、我还你一脚，步步有人绊倒跌翻——一言以蔽之，这场无处不然的大混乱，使萨迈拉俨然一座被强攻攻陷、听凭肆意劫掠的城池。

最后，那该咒的印度人依旧滚圆如初，穿尽所有街衢与公共场所，把它们撇得空空荡荡，一路滚向卡图勒平原（plain of Catoul），穿过了四泉山脚下的山谷。

由于流水经年不息的倾泻，山谷里已冲刷出一道深不可测的巨壑，对岸又被一道陡峭的斜壁封住；哈里发与随众惟恐肉球蹦进深渊，为阻拦它愈发拼命追赶，终究徒劳。那印度人一味朝前直滚，果然不出所惧，从崖边疾如闪电地一弹，便没入了下方的深渊。

===Original===


Vathek would have followed the perfidious Giaour, had not an invisible agency arrested his progress. The multitude that pressed after him were at once checked in the same manner, and a calm instantaneously ensued. They all gazed at each other with an air of astonishment; and notwithstanding that the loss of veils and turbans, together with torn habits, and dust blended with sweat, presented a most laughable spectacle, there was not one smile to be seen; on the contrary, all with looks of confusion and sadness returned in silence to Samarah, and retired to their inmost apartments, without ever reflecting that they had been impelled by an invisible power into the extravagance for which they reproached themselves: for it is but just, that men who so often arrogate to their own merit the good of which they are but instruments, should attribute to themselves the absurdities which they could not prevent.

The Caliph was the only person that refused to leave the valley. He commanded his tents to be pitched there, and stationed himself on the very edge of the precipice, in spite of the representations of Carathis and Morakanabad, who pointed out the hazard of its brink giving way, and the vicinity to the magician that had so severely tormented him. Vathek derided all their remonstrances; and having ordered a thousand flambeaus to be lighted, and directed his attendants to proceed in lighting more, lay down on the slippery margin, and attempted, by the help of this artificial splendour, to look through that gloom which all the fires of the empyrean had been insufficient to pervade. One while he fancied to himself voices arising from the depth of the gulf, at another he seemed to distinguish the accents of the Indian, but all was no more than the hollow murmur of waters, and the din of the cataracts that rushed from steep to steep, down the sides of the mountain.

Having passed the night in this cruel perturbation, the Caliph at daybreak retired to his tent, where, without taking the least sustenance, he continued to doze till the dusk of evening began to come on; he then resumed his vigils as before, and persevered in observing them for many nights together. At length, fatigued with so successless an employment, he sought relief from change. To this end he sometimes paced with hasty strides across the plain; and as he wildly gazed at the stars, reproached them with having deceived him; but lo! on a sudden the clear blue sky appeared streaked over with streams of blood, which reached from the valley even to the city of Samarah. As this awful phenomenon seemed to touch his tower, Vathek at first thought of repairing thither to view it more distinctly, but feeling himself unable to advance, and being overcome with apprehension, he muffled up his face in his robe.

===Chinese===


若非一股无形的力量拽住了他，瓦提克本会跟着那奸诈的吉奥尔一同跳下去。紧紧追在他身后的乌合之众，也顿时被同样地止住，霎时间万籁俱寂。众人面面相觑，一脸愕然；尽管丢了面纱与头巾、衣衫扯裂、尘土和着汗水，那副模样实在滑稽透顶，却见不到一丝笑容；恰恰相反，人人都带着惶惑悲戚之色，默然返回萨迈拉，退回自己最深的内室，却绝想不到：他们是被一种无形之力推入这场连自己也懊悔不迭的荒唐行径的。因为这也算得公道——世人既惯于把自己不过是工具的善功僭据为一己之美德，也就该把无从自主的荒唐归到自家账上。

唯独哈里发一人不肯离开山谷。他命人在那里扎下营帐，自己驻在悬崖的最边缘，全不顾卡拉希丝与莫拉卡纳巴德的谏劝——他们指陈崖畔随时可能崩塌，又离那狠狠折磨过他的魔法师太近。瓦提克对这一切谏言嗤之以鼻；他吩咐点起一千支火把，又命随从继续添点，然后伏在湿滑的崖沿上，想借这人造的煌煌光焰，看透那连九天之火也无法照彻的幽冥。一时他恍惚觉得深渊底下升起阵阵人语，一时又仿佛辨出印度人的口音，可这一切无非是空洞的水声呜咽，和瀑布沿山壁自陡崖奔向陡崖、层层跌落的轰鸣。

在这般残酷的煎熬中过了一夜，哈里发破晓时分回帐歇息；他粒米未进，昏昏沉沉直睡到暮色四合；随后又照旧守夜，一连守了许多个夜晚。终于，这毫无所获的营生使他厌倦，他指望换换环境来解闷。为此，他有时急匆匆地大步穿过平原；仰望星辰出神，责怪它们欺骗了他；可是看哪！霎时间，晴朗的碧空布满一道道血流，从山谷一直延伸到萨迈拉城。这可畏的异象似乎直抵他的高塔，瓦提克起初想登上塔去看个真切，却觉举步维艰，惊惧交加，只得把脸裹进袍子里。

===Original===


Terrifying as these prodigies were, this impression upon him was no more than momentary, and served only to stimulate his love of the marvellous. Instead, therefore, of returning to his palace, he persisted in the resolution of abiding where the Indian vanished from his view. One night, however, while he was walking as usual on the plain, the moon and the stars at once were eclipsed, and a total darkness ensued. The earth trembled beneath him, and a voice came forth, the voice of the Giaour, who in accents more sonorous than thunder, thus addressed him:

“Would’st thou devote thyself to me? adore then the terrestrial influences, and abjure Muhammad. On these conditions I will bring thee to the palace of subterranean fire: there shalt thou behold, in immense depositories, the treasures which the stars have promised thee, and which will be conferred by those intelligences whom thou shalt thus render propitious. It was from thence I brought my sabres; and it is there that Soliman Ben Daoud reposes, surrounded by the talismans that control the world.”

The astonished Caliph trembled as he answered, yet in a style that showed him to be no novice in preternatural adventures:

===Chinese===


这些异兆虽然可怖，在他心上留下的印象却不过一瞬，反而只会煽起他嗜奇好异的天性。因此他不但不回宫，反倒执意留在那印度人从他眼前消失的地方。然而一夜，他正照例在平原上散步，月亮与群星顿时一齐失明，天地间陷入一片漆黑。大地在他脚下震颤，一个声音迸发出来——是吉奥尔的声音，用比雷霆更洪亮的腔调向他如是说道：

「你可愿把自己奉献给我？那就崇奉地上的神力，弃绝穆罕默德吧。依了这些条件，我便带你前往地下火焰之宫：在那里，你将亲眼看见浩大的宝库中收藏的珍宝——那是群星许诺给你的，并将由你如此讨好而变得亲善的那些神明亲自授予。我的弯刀正是从那里带来的；也正是那里，苏莱曼·本·达乌德（Soliman Ben Daoud）安卧其间，四周围着统御世界的种种符箓。」

惊愕的哈里发战栗着作答，那口吻却显出他在超自然的奇遇中并非生手：

===Original===


“Where art thou? Be present to my eyes; dissipate the gloom that perplexes me, and of which I deem thee the cause. After the many flambeaus I have burnt to discover thee, thou mayest at least grant a glimpse of thy horrible visage.”

“Abjure then Muhammad,” replied the Indian, “and promise me full proofs of thy sincerity; otherwise thou shalt never behold me again.”

The unhappy Caliph, instigated by insatiable curiosity, lavished his promises in the utmost profusion. The sky immediately brightened; and by the light of the planets, which seemed almost to blaze, Vathek beheld the earth open, and at the extremity of a vast black chasm a portal of ebony, before which stood the Indian, still blacker, holding in his hand a golden key, that caused the lock to resound.

===Chinese===


「你在哪里？显现在朕眼前吧；驱散这团搅得朕心烦意乱的阴翳——朕认定你就是它的根由。朕为寻你烧掉了这许多火把，你总该让朕瞻仰一眼你那张可怖的面孔。」

「那就弃绝穆罕默德，」印度人答道，「并向我许下充分证明你诚意的凭据；否则你永远休想再见到我。」

那不幸的哈里发受着难餍好奇心的怂恿，把诺言撒漫天许了个十足。天空随即豁然明亮；借着几欲燃烧的行星之光，瓦提克眼见大地裂开，在巨大黑色深渊的尽头露出一扇乌木之门；门前立着那印度人，黑得愈发深黑，手中拿着一把金钥匙，震得锁具铮铮作响。

===Original===


“How,” cried Vathek, “can I descend to thee, without the certainty of breaking my neck? Come take me, and instantly open the portal.”

“Not so fast,” replied the Indian, “impatient Caliph! Know that I am parched with thirst, and cannot open this door till my thirst be thoroughly appeased. I require the blood of fifty of the most beautiful sons of thy viziers and great men, or neither can my thirst nor thy curiosity be satisfied. Return to Samarah; procure for me this necessary libation; come back hither; throw it thyself into this chasm; and then shalt thou see!”

Having thus spoken, the Indian turned his back on the Caliph, who, incited by the suggestion of demons, resolved on the direful sacrifice. He now pretended to have regained his tranquillity, and set out for Samarah amidst the acclamations of a people who still loved him, and forbore not to rejoice when they believed him to have recovered his reason. So successfully did he conceal the emotion of his heart, that even Carathis and Morakanabad were equally deceived with the rest. Nothing was heard of but festivals and rejoicings. The ball, which no tongue had hitherto ventured to mention, was again brought on the tapis. A general laugh went round; though many, still smarting under the hands of the surgeon, from the hurts received in that memorable adventure, had no great reason for mirth.

===Chinese===


「叫我怎么下去见你，」瓦提克喊道，「不落个颈断骨折的下场？来，带上我，立刻把门打开。」

「休要性急，」印度人答道，「急躁的哈里发！须知我干渴欲焚，不解透此渴，便开不得这扇门。我要你维齐尔与显贵们五十个最美貌的儿子的血；否则我的渴解不了，你的好奇心也满足不了。回萨迈拉去；为我把这必不可少的奠祭备来；再回到这里；亲手把它抛进这道深渊；那时你自会看见！」

言毕，印度人便把背转向哈里发；哈里发受群魔的撺掇，决意行这凶残的祭献。他此刻假作心神复宁，在仍然爱戴他的百姓的欢呼中起驾回萨迈拉；人们相信他神志已复，无不欢喜不置。他把心底的情绪掩饰得那样成功，连卡拉希丝与莫拉卡纳巴德也同众人一样受了欺瞒。举城上下但闻宴乐欢庆之声。那颗至今无人敢挂在嘴边的肉球，又被重提为话头。满座哄然一笑；虽说许多人尚在郎中手里忍痛疗伤——皆因那场难忘的奇遇里所受的创伤——实在没什么可笑的理由。

===Original===


The prevalence of this gay humour was not a little grateful to Vathek, as perceiving how much it conduced to his project. He put on the appearance of affability to everyone; but especially to his viziers, and the grandees of his court, whom he failed not to regale with a sumptuous banquet, during which he insensibly inclined the conversation to the children of his guests. Having asked, with a good-natured air, who of them were blessed with the handsomest boys, every father at once asserted the pretensions of his own; and the contest imperceptibly grew so warm, that nothing could have withholden them from coming to blows but their profound reverence for the person of the Caliph. Under the pretence, therefore, of reconciling the disputants, Vathek took upon him to decide; and with this view commanded the boys to be brought.

It was not long before a troop of these poor children made their appearance, all equipped by their fond mothers with such ornaments as might give the greatest relief to their beauty, or most advantageously display the graces of their age. But whilst this brilliant assemblage attracted the eyes and hearts of everyone besides, the Caliph scrutinized each in his turn with a malignant avidity that passed for attention, and selected from their number the fifty whom he judged the Giaour would prefer.

With an equal show of kindness as before, he proposed to celebrate a festival on the plain, for the entertainment of his young favourites, who he said ought to rejoice still more than all at the restoration of his health, on account of the favours he intended for them.

The Caliph’s proposal was received with the greatest delight, and soon published through Samarah. Litters, camels, and horses were prepared. Women and children, old men and young﻿—everyone placed himself in the station he chose. The cavalcade set forward, attended by all the confectioners in the city and its precincts. The populace, following on foot, composed an amazing crowd, and occasioned no little noise. All was joy; nor did anyone call to mind what most of them had suffered when they first travelled the road they were now passing so gaily.

===Chinese===


这片欢洽气象正中瓦提克下怀，他看得出这于他的图谋大有裨益。他对人人都摆出一副和蔼的面孔；对维齐尔与朝中显贵尤其如此，还设下盛筵款待他们；筵席间，他不露声色地把话头引向宾客们的子嗣。他装出好性气的模样，问他们中间谁家蒙上天眷顾，生有最俊美的男孩；做父亲的一下子个个都称起自家孩子的长来；这场争执不知不觉愈演愈烈，若不是对哈里发御体怀着深深的敬畏，他们简直要挥拳相向了。于是瓦提克借口调停争执，主动担当裁决；并为此传令把男孩们都带来。

不多时，这群可怜的孩子便成群出现了，个个都被慈爱的母亲装扮得花团锦簇，或最能衬出他们的美貌，或最能显出他们年龄的天骄。这一队光彩夺目的小人儿吸引住了旁人的眼睛与心，唯独哈里发逐一打量他们，那副恶毒的贪婪倒被当作了垂顾；他从中间挑出了五十个——他料想吉奥尔最中意的五十个。

他又摆出与前一般无二的慈和面孔，提议在平原上办一场庆典，娱悦他这些年轻的宠儿；他说，想到他将赐予他们的恩惠，他们理应比众人都更为他康复而欢欣。

哈里发的提议获得了最大的欢忭，消息很快传遍萨迈拉。肩舆、骆驼与马匹一一备齐。妇孺老幼——人人都拣了自己中意的位置坐定。马队浩浩荡荡地开拔了，城中及四郊的糖果点心师傅悉数随行。百姓徒步相随，汇成惊人的人海，喧闹非凡。人人尽欢；却没有一个人想起，当初头一回走这条如今走得这般欢畅的路时，他们大多数人吃过怎样的苦头。

===Original===


The evening was serene, the air refreshing, the sky clear, and the flowers exhaled their fragrance. The beams of the declining sun, whose mild splendour reposed on the summit of the mountain, shed a glow of ruddy light over its green declivity, and the white flocks sporting upon it. No sounds were audible, save the murmurs of the four fountains, and the reeds and voices of shepherds, calling to each other from different eminences.

The lovely innocents, proceeding to the destined sacrifice, added not a little to the hilarity of the scene. They approached the plain full of sportiveness; some coursing butterflies, others culling flowers, or picking up the shining little pebbles that attracted their notice. At intervals, they nimbly started from each other, for the sake of being caught again, and mutually imparting a thousand caresses.

The dreadful chasm, at whose bottom the portal of ebony was placed, began to appear at a distance. It looked like a black streak that divided the plain. Morakanabad and his companions took it for some work which the Caliph had ordered. Unhappy men! little did they surmise for what it was destined.

Vathek, not liking that they should examine it too nearly, stopped the procession, and ordered a spacious circle to be formed on this side, at some distance from the accursed chasm. The bodyguard of eunuchs was detached, to measure out the lists intended for the games, and prepare ringles for the lines to keep off the crowd. The fifty competitors were soon stripped, and presented to the admiration of the spectators the suppleness and grace of their delicate limbs. Their eyes sparkled with a joy which those of their fond parents reflected. Every one offered wishes for the little candidate nearest his heart, and doubted not of his being victorious. A breathless suspense awaited the contest of these amiable and innocent victims.

===Chinese===


夜色澄宁，空气清爽，天空明净，百花吐露芬芳。西沉的太阳把柔和的光辉安放在山巅，给青翠的山坡和坡上嬉戏的白色羊群洒下一抹红润的光泽。除了四座泉眼的淙淙低语，和牧人们从各处高地遥相呼应的芦笛与人声，再听不见别的声响。

这些可爱的孩子正走向注定的祭途，却为这幅景象平添了不小的欢愉。他们满身嬉态地向平原走来；有的追逐蝴蝶，有的采撷花朵，或者拾捡那些引人注目的闪亮小卵石。他们不时轻捷地从彼此身边蹿开，只为再度被捉住，相互间千般亲昵。

那可怕的裂谷——谷底安置着乌木门户——已开始远远显出形迹。它看上去像一道把平原分开的黑线。摩拉卡纳巴德和他的同伴们还只当那是哈里发下令兴修的什么工程。不幸的人啊！他们万万料不到这裂谷注定派何用场。

瓦提克不愿让他们近前细看，便叫停了队伍，命人在这一侧、离那可诅咒的裂谷稍远之处，围出一个宽阔的圆场。太监卫队被差遣出去，量出赛技用的场地，钉好系绳的环子，拉起围绳以隔开人群。五十名参赛的孩子很快脱去了衣裳，将纤巧四肢的柔韧与优美呈现在观者的赞叹之前。他们的眼中闪烁着欢欣，慈爱的父母眼中也映出同样的光芒。人人都在为自己最疼爱的孩子许愿，毫不怀疑他会得胜。一场屏息的紧张期待，等候着这些可爱而无辜的祭品的角逐。

===Original===


The Caliph, availing himself of the first moment to retire from the crowd, advanced towards the chasm, and there heard, yet not without shuddering, the voice of the Indian; who, gnashing his teeth, eagerly demanded:

“Where are they? Where are they? perceivest thou not how my mouth waters?”

“Relentless Giaour!” answered Vathek, with emotion, “can nothing content thee but the massacre of these lovely victims? Ah! wert thou to behold their beauty, it must certainly move thy compassion.”

“Perdition on thy compassion, babbler!” cried the Indian. “Give them me! instantly give them, or my portal shall be closed against thee forever!”

“Not so loudly,” replied the Caliph, blushing.

“I understand thee,” returned the Giaour, with the grin of an ogre: “thou wantest to summon up more presence of mind. I will for a moment forbear.”

===Chinese===


瓦提克趁着最先的一个空当退出人群，朝裂谷走去，在那里听见了那印度人的声音——他听时仍不免战栗；那印度人咬着牙，急切地索讨：

“他们在哪儿？他们在哪儿？你没瞧见我口水都要流出来了么？”

“狠心的吉奥尔！”瓦提克激动地答道，“难道除了屠戮这些可爱的牺牲品，就没有什么能满足你了吗？啊！你若亲眼见到他们的美貌，必定会动恻隐之心的。”

“让你的恻隐之心见鬼去吧，碎嘴的家伙！”印度人嚷道，“把他们给我！立刻给我，不然我的门户将对你永远关闭！”

“别这么大声，”哈里发红着脸回答。

“我明白你的意思，”吉奥尔咧开食人魔般的狞笑答道，“你想再攒起几分镇定。我就暂且忍一忍。”

===Original===


During this exquisite dialogue the games went forward with all alacrity, and at length concluded, just as the twilight began to overcast the mountains. Vathek, who was still standing on the edge of the chasm, called out with all his might:

“Let my fifty little favourites approach me, separately; and let them come in the order of their success. To the first I will give my diamond bracelet; to the second my collar of emeralds; to the third my aigrette of rubies; to the fourth my girdle of topazes; and to the rest, each a part of my dress, even down to my slippers.”

This declaration was received with reiterated acclamations; and all extolled the liberality of a prince who would thus strip himself for the amusement of his subjects and the encouragement of the rising generation.

The Caliph in the mean while undressed himself by degrees; and raising his arm as high as he was able, made each of the prizes glitter in the air; but, whilst he delivered it with one hand to the child, who sprang forward to receive it, he with the other pushed the poor innocent into the gulf, where the Giaour, with a sullen muttering, incessantly repeated “More! more!”

===Chinese===


在这场妙不可言的对话进行之际，竞技也在卖力推进，终于在暮色开始笼罩群山时收了场。瓦提克仍站在裂谷边上，放开嗓子高声喊道：

“让我那五十个心肝宝贝挨个儿上前，按得胜的名次顺序来领赏。头一名，我赏他我的钻石手镯；第二名，赏我的祖母绿项圈；第三名，赏我的红宝石羽饰；第四名，赏我的黄玉腰带；其余的，每人赏我身上的一件穿戴，连我的拖鞋也在内。”

这番宣告赢得了一浪高过一浪的欢呼；人人都称颂这位君王的慷慨大度——为了娱悦臣民、激励年青一代，竟肯把自己剥得精光。

与此同时，哈里发一件件脱下衣物，把手臂尽量举高，让每件奖品在空中熠熠生辉；可是，当他一手把奖品递给欢跳着上前领取的孩子时，另一只手却把那可怜的无辜者推下深渊；深渊里的吉奥尔一边阴沉地咕哝，一边不停地喊：“再来！再来！”

===Original===


This dreadful device was executed with so much dexterity, that the boy who was approaching him remained unconscious of the fate of his forerunner; and as to the spectators, the shades of evening, together with their distance, precluded them from perceiving any object distinctly. Vathek, having in this manner thrown in the last of the fifty, and expecting that the Giaour on receiving him would have presented the key, already fancied himself as great as Soliman, and consequently above being amenable for what he had done; when, to his utter amazement, the chasm closed, and the ground became as entire as the rest of the plain.

No language could express his rage and despair. He execrated the perfidy of the Indian; loaded him with the most infamous invectives; and stamped with his foot as resolving to be heard. He persisted in this demeanour till his strength failed him, and then fell on the earth like one void of sense. His viziers and grandees, who were nearer than the rest, supposed him at first to be sitting on the grass at play with their amiable children; but at length, prompted by doubt, they advanced towards the spot, and found the Caliph alone, who wildly demanded what they wanted.

“Our children! our children!” cried they.

“It is assuredly pleasant,” said he, “to make me accountable for accidents. Your children, while at play, fell from the precipice that was here; and I should have experienced their fate had I not been saved by a sudden start back.”

===Chinese===


这骇人的勾当干得极其娴熟，走向他的孩子对前一个的遭遇毫无知觉；至于看客们，暮色加之距离，使他们无法看清任何东西。瓦提克就这样把第五十个孩子也扔了进去，满以为吉奥尔收下他后便会交出钥匙，已经自忖伟大得如同苏莱曼，所做的一切再也无须答问；不料裂谷竟然合拢，地面变得与平原其余部分一样平整，令他惊愕万分。

任何语言都无法形容他的狂怒与绝望。他咒骂印度人的奸诈，用最恶毒的话痛骂不绝，顿着脚，打定主意非要人听见不可。他就这样闹到力气耗尽，才像失了知觉一般瘫倒在地。他的维齐尔和贵胄们离得比旁人近些，起初还以为他正坐在草地上同他们可爱的孩子玩耍；但疑心终于驱使他们走向前去，却只见哈里发独自一人，狂乱地质问他们要干什么。

“我们的孩子！我们的孩子！”他们哭喊着。

“把这飞来横祸都算在我头上，可真是妙事，”他说，“你们的孩子玩耍时从原先这儿的悬崖上跌了下去；若不是我猛地向后一跳捡回一条命，也早遭了他们的下场。

===Original===


At these words, the fathers of the fifty boys cried out aloud: the mothers repeated their exclamations an octave higher; whilst the rest, without knowing the cause, soon drowned the voices of both, with still louder lamentations of their own.

“Our Caliph,” said they, and the report soon circulated, “Our Caliph has played us this trick, to gratify his accursed Giaour. Let us punish him for his perfidy! let us avenge ourselves! let us avenge the blood of the innocent! let us throw this cruel Prince into the gulf that is near, and let his name be mentioned no more!”

At this rumour, and these menaces, Carathis, full of consternation, hastened to Morakanabad, and said:

===Chinese===


这些话一出，五十个男孩的父亲们放声大哭；母亲们又高了八度重复着他们的哀号；其余的人虽不知缘故，也随即用更响的恸哭把两边的声音都淹没了。

“咱们的哈里发，”他们说道，这风声很快传扬开去，“咱们的哈里发使这一手来捉弄我们，好讨他那可诅咒的吉奥尔欢心。惩罚他的背信弃义吧！我们要报仇！为无辜者的血报仇！把这个残忍的君王扔进近旁的深渊里去，让他的名字从此无人再提！”

听到这风声和这些威吓，卡拉希丝大为惊骇，急忙去找摩拉卡纳巴德，说道：

===Original===


“Vizier, you have lost two beautiful boys, and must necessarily be the most afflicted of fathers; but you are virtuous; save your master!”

“I will brave every hazard,” replied the Vizier, “to rescue him from his present danger; but afterwards will abandon him to his fate. Bababalouk,” continued he, “put yourself at the head of your eunuchs, disperse the mob, and if possible bring back this unhappy Prince to his palace.”

Bababalouk and his fraternity, felicitating each other in a low voice on their disability of ever being fathers, obeyed the mandate of the Vizier; who, seconding their exertions to the utmost of his power, at length accomplished his generous enterprise, and retired, as he resolved, to lament at his leisure.

===Chinese===


“维齐尔，你失去了两个俊美的孩子，必定是众父亲中最为伤痛的；可你是贤德之人；救救你的君上吧！”

“我甘冒一切风险，”维齐尔答道，“把他从眼前的危难中救出来；但事后便任他听天由命。巴巴巴鲁克，”他接着说，“你带上你的太监们，驱散暴民，要是可能，把这位不幸的君王送回宫里去。”

巴巴巴鲁克和他那一帮人低声相互庆贺自己天生做不了父亲，便遵了维齐尔的令；维齐尔竭尽全力从旁协助，终于成就了这桩仗义之举，随后依着他先前的打算退下去，好从容地悲悼一番。

===Original===


No sooner had the Caliph reentered his palace, than Carathis commanded the doors to be fastened; but perceiving the tumult to be still violent, and hearing the imprecations which resounded from all quarters, she said to her son:

“Whether the populace be right or wrong, it behoves you to provide for your safety: let us retire to your own apartment, and from thence, through the subterranean passage known only to ourselves, into your tower; there, with the assistance of the mutes who never leave it, we may be able to make some resistance. Bababalouk, supposing us to be still in the palace, will guard its avenues for his own sake; and we shall soon find, without the counsels of that blubberer Morakanabad, what expedient may be the best to adopt.”

Vathek, without making the least reply, acquiesced in his mother’s proposal, and repeated as he went:

“Nefarious Giaour! where art thou? hast thou not yet devoured those poor children? where are thy sabres? thy golden key? thy talismans?”

===Chinese===


哈里发刚重回宫中，卡拉希丝便命人把门户闩死；但见骚乱依然凶猛，又听得咒骂声四下震响，她对儿子说：

“不管百姓有理没理，你总得为自身的安全作打算：我们先退到你自己寝宫去，再从那里经过只有我们知道的地下通道进你的塔楼；在那里，靠着从不离开塔楼的哑仆们相助，我们兴许还能抵挡一阵。巴巴巴鲁克只当我们还在宫里，为了他自己也会把守各处路口；至于那个哭包摩拉卡纳巴德，用不着他的主意，我们很快就能想出最妥当的对策。”

瓦提克一言不发地依了母亲的提议，一路走一路念叨：

“恶毒的吉奥尔！你在哪儿？你还没把那些可怜的孩子吞下肚么？你的宝剑呢？你的金钥匙呢？你的护符呢？”

===Original===


Carathis, who guessed from these interrogations a part of the truth, had no difficulty to apprehend in getting at the whole, as soon as he should be a little composed in his tower. This Princess was so far from being influenced by scruples that she was as wicked as woman could be, which is not saying a little, for the sex pique themselves on their superiority in every competition. The recital of the Caliph therefore occasioned neither terror nor surprise to his mother; she felt no emotion but from the promises of the Giaour; and said to her son:

“This Giaour, it must be confessed, is somewhat sanguinary in his taste, but the terrestrial powers are always terrible: nevertheless, what the one has promised and the others can confer, will prove a sufficient indemnification. No crimes should be thought too dear for such a reward. Forbear then to revile the Indian: you have not fulfilled the conditions to which his services are annexed. For instance, is not a sacrifice to the subterranean Genii required? and should we not be prepared to offer it as soon as the tumult is subsided? This charge I will take on myself, and have no doubt of succeeding by means of your treasures; which, as there are now so many others in store, may without fear be exhausted.”

Accordingly, the Princess, who possessed the most consummate skill in the art of persuasion, went immediately back through the subterranean passage, and presenting herself to the populace from a window of the palace, began to harangue them with all the address of which she was mistress, whilst Bababalouk showered money from both hands amongst the crowd, who by these united means were soon appeased. Every person retired to his home, and Carathis returned to the tower.

===Chinese===


卡拉希丝从这些问话里猜出了部分真相，只等他在塔楼里稍稍定下神来，便不难问出全部。这位公主非但不受良心的半点拘束，简直坏到了女人所能坏的极处——这可不是小话，因为女性向来自负无论何事都要赛过旁人。因此哈里发的讲述没让他的母亲生出半点惊骇或诧异；唯有吉奥尔的许诺才叫她心旌摇动；她对儿子说：

“这个吉奥尔，不得不承认，口味是有点血腥，可尘世间的权势者向来都是可怕的：不过，他许下的、他们能给的一切，足以抵偿。为了这样的报偿，再大的罪孽也不嫌昂贵。所以别再咒骂那印度人了：他效力所附带的条件，你还没有履行呢。比如说，不是还得向地下神灵献祭么？骚乱一平息，我们岂不该立刻备办起来？这件事包在我身上，靠你库里的财宝，没有办不成的；如今既另有那许多宝物在望，这些家私尽可放心大胆地花。”

于是，这位深谙劝服之术、技艺炉火纯青的公主，立刻从地下通道折回去，在宫殿一扇窗前现身于百姓面前，拿出她全部的本领向他们长篇演说，巴巴巴鲁克则双手往人群里大把撒钱；双管齐下，众人很快便平息了。人人各自归家，卡拉希丝也回了塔楼。

===Original===


Prayer at break of day was announced, when Carathis and Vathek ascended the steps which led to the summit of the tower, where they remained for some time, though the weather was lowering and wet.
