This impending gloom corresponded with their malignant dispositions; but when the sun began to break through the clouds, they ordered a pavilion to be raised as a screen from the intrusion of his beams. The Caliph, overcome with fatigue, sought refreshment from repose, at the same time hoping that significant dreams might attend on his slumbers; whilst the indefatigable Carathis, followed by a party of her mutes, descended to prepare whatever she judged proper for the oblation of the approaching night.

By secret stairs, known only to herself and her son, she first repaired to the mysterious recesses in which were deposited the mummies that had been brought from the catacombs of the ancient Pharaohs. Of these she ordered several to be taken. From thence she resorted to a gallery, where, under the guard of fifty female negroes, mute, and blind of the right eye, were preserved the oil of the most venomous serpents, rhinoceros’ horns, and woods of a subtle and penetrating odour, procured from the interior of the Indies, together with a thousand other horrible rarieties. This collection had been formed for a purpose like the present, by Carathis herself, from a presentiment that she might one day enjoy some intercourse with the infernal powers, to whom she had ever been passionately attached, and to whose taste she was no stranger.

To familiarize herself the better with the horrors in view, the Princess remained in the company of her negresses, who squinted in the most amiable manner from the only eye they had, and leered with exquisite delight at the skulls and skeletons which Carathis had drawn forth from her cabinets, whose key she entrusted to no one; all of them making contortions, and uttering a frightful jargon, but very amusing to the Princess till at last, being stunned by their gibbering, and suffocated by the potency of their exhalations, she was forced to quit the gallery, after stripping it of a part of its treasures.

===Chinese===


破晓的祷时已到，卡拉希丝和瓦提克登上通往塔顶的阶梯，在顶上盘桓了好一阵，尽管天色阴沉欲雨。这迫近的昏暗倒正合他们恶毒的心性；待到太阳开始钻出云层，他们便吩咐支起一座凉棚，挡住那不速而至的光线。哈里发困乏已极，寻个安歇来养养精神，一面还盼着有深意的梦境伴随他的睡眠；不知疲倦的卡拉希丝则带着一队哑仆下了塔，去备办她认为合宜的东西，以供即将来临的黑夜献祭之用。

她循着只有她和儿子知道的秘梯，先去了那些神秘的幽窟——里面存放着从古法老墓穴里运来的木乃伊。她吩咐取下其中几具。随后她来到一条廊庑，那里在五十名黑人女子的看守下——个个喑哑，右眼皆盲——保存着最毒的蛇的油、犀牛角，以及从印度内陆弄来的气味幽微而穿透力极强的香木，还有一千样别的可怖珍奇。这批收藏是卡拉希丝专为今日这般用途而亲自搜罗的，出于一种预感：她有朝一日得以同冥府诸权贵交通——她向来对它们一往情深，对它们的口味也并不生疏。

为了让自己更好地习惯即将面对的种种恐怖，公主留在她的黑人女仆们中间；她们用仅存的那只眼睛极和蔼地斜睨着，喜滋滋地打量卡拉希丝从柜子里取出的骷髅和骸骨——那柜子的钥匙她从不交给任何人；她们个个挤眉弄眼，发出一种吓人的呓语，公主起初只觉有趣，直到后来被她们叽里咕噜吵得发懵，又被她们身上散发的浓烈气息呛得透不过气，只得把廊庑里的珍奇劫掠了一部分，离开了那里。

===Original===


Whilst she was thus occupied, the Caliph, who instead of the visions he expected, had acquired in these insubstantial regions a voracious appetite, was greatly provoked at the negresses: for, having totally forgotten their deafness, he had impatiently asked them for food; and seeing them regardless of his demand, he began to cuff, pinch, and push them, till Carathis arrived to terminate a scene so indecent, to the great content of these miserable creatures, who having been brought up by her, understood all her signs, and communicated in the same way their thoughts in return.

“Son! what means all this?” said she, panting for breath. “I thought I heard as I came up, the shrieks of a thousand bats, tearing from their crannies in the recesses of a cavern, and it was the outcry only of these poor mutes, whom you were so unmercifully abusing. In truth you but ill deserve the admirable provision I have brought you.”

“Give it me instantly!” exclaimed the Caliph: “I am perishing for hunger!”

===Chinese===


她忙着这些的时候，哈里发非但没盼来期想中的幻象，反而在这些虚无缥缈之境里落下了一副饿痨般的胃口，对那些黑人女仆大为光火：他把她们失聪的事忘了个干净，不耐烦地向她们要吃的；见她们置之不理，他便动手又掴又拧又推，直到卡拉希丝赶来终止了这场大不体面的闹剧——这让那些苦命的人大为快慰，她们是她一手调教出来的，看得懂她的全部手势，也用同样的手势回禀她们的心思。

“儿子！这是怎么回事？”她喘着气说。“我上来时还当听见一千只蝙蝠从岩洞深处的窟窿里撕抓着窜出的尖啸，原来不过是这些可怜的哑仆被你这样无情作践时的喊叫。说实在的，我给你备下的这份美馔，你真不配享用。”

“快给我拿来！”哈里发喊道，“我快饿死啦！”

===Original===


“As to that,” answered she, “you must have an excellent stomach if it can digest what I have been preparing.”

“Be quick,” replied the Caliph. “But oh, heavens! what horrors! What do you intend?”

“Come, come,” returned Carathis, “be not so squeamish, but help me to arrange everything properly, and you shall see that what you reject with such symptoms of disgust will soon complete your felicity. Let us get ready the pile for the sacrifice of tonight, and think not of eating till that is performed. Know you not that all solemn rites are preceded by a rigorous abstinence?”

===Chinese===


“说到这个，”她答道，“你的胃可得足够强健，才消化得了我备下的东西。”

“快点吧，”哈里发应道，“可是，天哪！好吓人！你要干什么？”

“来吧，来吧，”卡拉希丝答道，“别这么娇气，帮我样样布置妥当，你就会看到，你如此作呕嫌弃的东西，转眼便能成全你的洪福。且把今夜祭仪的柴堆备好，这事不了，休想用饭。你难道不知一切庄严的祭仪之前，都须严格斋戒么？”

===Original===


The Caliph, not daring to object, abandoned himself to grief, and the wind that ravaged his entrails, whilst his mother went forward with the requisite operations. Phials of serpents’ oil, mummies, and bones, were soon set in order on the balustrade of the tower. The pile began to rise; and in three hours was as many cubits high. At length, darkness approached, and Carathis having stripped herself to her inmost garment, clapped her hands in an impulse of ecstasy, and struck light with all her force. The mutes followed her example: but Vathek, extenuated with hunger and impatience, was unable to support himself, and fell down in a swoon. The sparks had already kindled the dry wood; the venomous oil burst into a thousand blue flames; the mummies, dissolving, emitted a thick dun vapour; and the rhinoceros’ horns beginning to consume; all together diffused such a stench, that the Caliph, recovering, started from his trance and gazed wildly on the scene in full blaze around him. The oil gushed forth in a plentitude of streams; and the negresses, who supplied it without intermission, united their cries to those of the Princess. At last the fire became so violent, and the flames reflected from the polished marble so dazzling, that the Caliph, unable to withstand the heat and the blaze, effected his escape, and clambered up the imperial standard.

In the meantime, the inhabitants of Samarah, scared at the light which shone over the city, arose in haste, ascended their roofs, beheld the tower on fire, and hurried half-naked to the square. Their love to their sovereign immediately awoke; and apprehending him in danger of perishing in his tower, their whole thoughts were occupied with the means of his safety. Morakanabad flew from his retirement, wiped away his tears, and cried out for water like the rest. Bababalouk, whose olfactory nerves were more familiarized to magical odours, readily conjecturing that Carathis was engaged in her favourite amusements, strenuously exhorted them not to be alarmed. Him, however, they treated as an old poltroon; and forbore not to style him a rascally traitor. The camels and dromedaries were advancing with water, but no one knew by which way to enter the tower. Whilst the populace was obstinate in forcing the doors, a violent east wind drove such a volume of flame against them, as at first forced them off; but afterwards, rekindled their zeal. At the same time, the stench of the horns and mummies increasing, most of the crowd fell backward in a state of suffocation. Those that kept their feet mutually wondered at the cause of the smell, and admonished each other to retire. Morakanabad, more sick than the rest, remained in a piteous condition. Holding his nose with one hand, he persisted in his efforts with the other to burst open the doors, and obtain admission. A hundred and forty of the strongest and most resolute at length accomplished their purpose. Having gained the staircase by their violent exertions, they attained a great height in a quarter of an hour.

===Chinese===


哈里发不敢违拗，只得听凭悲伤和那绞肠刮肚的饥风的摆布，由他母亲去进行必要的一应操作。一瓶瓶蛇油、一具具木乃伊和枯骨，很快在塔楼的栏杆上依次排开。柴堆开始升高；三个钟头里就垒起了三肘之高。终于，夜幕降临，卡拉希丝脱得只剩贴身里衣，狂喜之下拍起手来，使出浑身力气击出火光。哑仆们如法炮制；瓦提克却已饿得形销骨立、心焦难耐，支撑不住，晕倒在地。火星早已点燃干柴；毒油迸出千百道蓝色的火焰；木乃伊渐渐消融，喷出浓浊的暗褐烟气；犀牛角也开始燃烧；种种气味混作一股恶臭，哈里发悠悠转醒，从昏迷中惊起，狂乱地瞪视着四周烈焰熊熊的景象。油像丰沛的溪流一般涌出；那些不停添油的黑人女仆，把她们的喊声与公主的喊声汇作一处。火势终于猛烈到极点，磨光的大理石映出的火焰晃得人睁不开眼，哈里发受不住那热浪与强光，脱身逃开，攀上了帝国的御旗。

与此同时，萨迈拉的居民们望见城市上空的光亮，惊惶而起，爬上屋顶，看见塔楼起火，便半裸着身子奔向广场。他们对君王的爱戴当即醒了过来；唯恐他葬身塔中，满脑子想的都是救他脱险的法子。摩拉卡纳巴德从退隐之所飞奔而来，抹去眼泪，像旁人一样高喊要水。巴巴巴鲁克的嗅觉神经对魔法气味早已习以为常，一猜便中，知道卡拉希丝正忙着她心爱的玩意儿，便竭力劝大家不要惊慌。可众人只把他当作一个老懦夫，毫不客气地骂他是个无赖的奸贼。骆驼和单峰驼驮着水一路赶来，却没人知道该从哪条道进塔。百姓们执意撞门之际，一阵猛烈的东风把大片火焰压向他们，先把他们逼退；过后却又重新点燃了他们的热忱。与此同时，犀牛角和木乃伊的恶臭愈来愈重，人群大半被熏得向后栽倒，几近窒息。站得住的彼此惊疑这气味的来历，相互劝告退避。摩拉卡纳巴德比别人更难受，处境煞是可怜。他一手捏着鼻子，另一只手仍不肯歇地撞门，要闯进去。一百四十个最壮健最果决的人终于如愿。他们奋力抢上楼梯，一刻钟内便攀到了很高的地方。

===Original===


Carathis, alarmed at the signs of her mutes, advanced to the staircase, went down a few steps, and heard several voices calling out from below:

“You shall in a moment have water!”

Being rather alert, considering her age, she presently regained the top of the tower, and bade her son suspend the sacrifice for some minutes, adding:

“We shall soon be enabled to render it more grateful. Certain dolts of your subjects, imagining, no doubt, that we were on fire, have been rash enough to break through those doors, which had hitherto remained inviolate, for the sake of bringing up water. They are very kind, you must allow, so soon to forget the wrongs you have done them: but that is of little moment. Let us offer them to the Giaour. Let them come up: our mutes, who neither want strength nor experience, will soon despatch them, exhausted as they are with fatigue.”

===Chinese===


卡拉希丝见哑仆们比划示警，便走向楼梯，下了几级，听见下面好几个人声在喊：

“水马上就到！”

论年纪，她算是利落的了，转眼便重上塔顶，吩咐儿子把祭仪暂停片刻，又补上一句：

“转眼我们就能把祭品办得更丰盛。你的臣民里有几个蠢货，想必以为咱们着了火，竟敢鲁莽地撞破了那几道门——那可是至今从未遭人亵渎过的——就为送水上来。你可得承认，他们实在好心，这么快就忘了你对不起他们的地方：不过这无关紧要。把他们献给吉奥尔就是了。让他们上来：咱们的哑仆有的是力气和经验，他们又已累得半死，转眼就能打发掉。”

===Original===


“Be it so,” answered the Caliph, “provided we finish, and I dine.”

In fact, these good people, out of breath from ascending eleven thousand stairs in such haste, and chagrined at having spilt, by the way, the water they had taken, were no sooner arrived at the top than the blaze of the flames and the fumes of the mummies at once overpowered their senses. It was a pity! for they beheld not the agreeable smile with which the mutes and the negresses adjusted the cord to their necks: these amiable personages rejoiced, however, no less at the scene. Never before had the ceremony of strangling been performed with so much facility. They all fell without the least resistance or struggle; so that Vathek, in the space of a few moments, found himself surrounded by the dead bodies of his most faithful subjects, all of which were thrown on the top of the pile.

Carathis, whose presence of mind never forsook her, perceiving that she had carcases sufficient to complete her oblation, commanded the chains to be stretched across the staircase, and the iron doors barricaded, that no more might come up.

No sooner were these orders obeyed, than the tower shook; the dead bodies vanished in the flames; which at once changed from a swarthy crimson to a bright rose colour. An ambient vapour emitted the most exquisite fragrance; the marble columns rang with harmonious sounds, and the liquefied horns diffused a delicious perfume. Carathis, in transports, anticipated the success of her enterprise; whilst the mutes and negresses, to whom these sweets had given the cholic, retired to their cells grumbling.

===Chinese===


“就这么办，”哈里发答道，“只要能办完，好让我用饭。”

其实，这些好心人慌里慌张地爬完一万一千级楼梯，早已气喘吁吁，又懊恼一路把带来的水洒了个精光，刚到塔顶，火焰的强光和木乃伊的烟气便顿时熏得他们神志全失。可惜呀！他们竟没能看见哑仆和黑人女仆替他们把绳圈套上脖颈时那和悦的笑容：不过这几位仁厚的角色见了这场面也一样欢欣。行刑勒毙的仪式从来还没有办得这般顺当过。他们一个个毫无挣扎反抗便倒了下去；不出片刻，瓦提克便发现自己已被最忠实的臣民的尸首团团围住，这些尸首统统被抛上了柴堆的顶上。

卡拉希丝向来临危不乱，见尸首已足以成全她的祭礼，便吩咐把铁链横过楼梯拉起，再把铁门堵死，免得再有人上来。

这些吩咐一一照办，塔楼便震颤起来；尸首在火焰中消失了；火焰顿时由黝暗的猩红转为明亮的玫瑰色。四周弥漫的水汽散出至精至美的芬芳；大理石柱铮铮作响，音调和谐；熔化的犀角漾开美妙的香气。卡拉希丝欣喜若狂，已然预见到她这桩事业的成功；哑仆和黑人女仆却被这甜香激得腹中绞痛，一路抱怨着退回各自的小屋里去了。

===Original===


Scarcely were they gone, when, instead of the pile, horns, mummies, and ashes, the Caliph both saw and felt, with a degree of pleasure which he could not express, a table, covered with the most magnificent repast: flagons of wine, and vases of exquisite sherbet, floating on snow. He availed himself, without scruple, of such an entertainment; and had already laid hands on a lamb stuffed with pistachios, whilst Carathis was privately drawing from a fillagreen urn, a parchment that seemed to be endless; and which had escaped the notice of her son. Totally occupied, in gratifying an importunate appetite, he left her to peruse it, without interruption; which having finished, she said to him, in an authoritative tone,

“Put an end to your gluttony, and hear the splendid promises with which you are favoured!” She then read, as follows:

“Vathek, my well-beloved, thou hast surpassed my hopes: my nostrils have been regaled by the savour of thy mummies, thy horns; and, still more, by the lives devoted on the pile. At the full of the moon, cause the bands of thy musicians, and thy tymbals, to be heard; depart from thy palace surrounded by all the pageants of majesty; thy most faithful slaves, thy best beloved wives; thy most magnificent litters; thy richest loaden camels; and set forward on thy way to Istakar. There await I thy coming. That is the region of wonders. There shalt thou receive the diadem of Gian Ben Gian,11 the talismans of Soliman, and the treasures of the preadamite Sultans: there shalt thou be solaced with all kinds of delight. But, beware how thou enterest any dwelling on thy route, or thou shalt feel the effects of my anger.”

===Chinese===


她们刚退下，柴堆、犀角、木乃伊和灰烬便都不见了；哈里发怀着难以言表的欢愉，看见并摸到了一张桌子，上面摆着最丰盛华美的肴馔：一坛坛美酒，一瓶瓶上乘的果子露，浮在雪上。他毫不客气地享用了这份款待；手上已经抓住一只填了开心果的烤羊羔，卡拉希丝却在私下里从一只金银丝细工的瓮中抽出一卷仿佛没有尽头的羊皮纸来；这事躲过了她儿子的眼睛。他一心一意满足那纠缠不休的口腹之欲，任凭她不受打扰地读下去；她读完了，便用一种专断的口气对他说：

“别再贪嘴了，听听人家许给你的锦绣前程吧！”她随即念道：

“瓦提克，我的心肝，你超出我的期望了：你的木乃伊、你的犀角，香气滋养了我的鼻观；堆上焚献的那些性命，更叫我受用。月圆之时，传你的乐班和铜鼓齐鸣；带上君王的全部仪仗离开你的宫殿：你最忠实的奴仆，你最宠爱的妻妾，你最华美的步辇，你驮载最丰的骆驼；动身往伊斯特卡尔（Istakar）去。我在那里等你来。那才是奇境之邦。在那里，你将领受吉安·本·吉安（Gian Ben Gian）11 的王冠、苏莱曼的诸般护符，以及亚当之前的众苏丹的宝藏：在那里，百般欢娱都将抚慰你。但你务必小心，途中不得踏入任何居所一步，否则便叫你尝尝我动怒的滋味。”

===Original===


The Caliph, who, notwithstanding his habitual luxury, had never before dined with so much satisfaction, gave full scope to the joy of these golden tidings, and betook himself to drinking anew. Carathis, whose antipathy to wine was by no means insuperable, failed not to supply a reason for every bumper, which they ironically quaffed to the health of Muhammad. This infernal liquor completed their impious temerity, and prompted them to utter a profusion of blasphemies. They gave a loose to their wit, at the expense of the ass of Balaam, the dog of the seven sleepers, and the other animals admitted into the paradise of Muhammad. In this sprightly humour they descended the eleven thousand stairs, diverting themselves as they went at the anxious faces they saw on the square, through the oilets of the tower, and at length arrived at the royal apartments by the subterranean passage. Bababalouk was parading to and fro, and issuing his mandates with great pomp to the eunuchs, who were snuffing the lights and painting the eyes of the Circassians. No sooner did he catch sight of the Caliph and his mother than he exclaimed,

“Hah! you have then, I perceive, escaped from the flames; I was not, however, altogether out of doubt.”

“Of what moment is it to us what you thought or think?” cried Carathis: “go, speed, tell Morakanabad that we immediately want him; and take care how you stop by the way to make your insipid reflections.”

===Chinese===


哈里发虽惯于奢华，却从未吃得这般称心，此刻任由这天大的喜讯带来的欢愉尽情流淌，又重新开怀畅饮起来。卡拉希丝对酒的厌恶本不算根深蒂固，每一杯都不愁找不出由头，母子俩冷嘲热讽地把酒浇给穆罕默德（Muhammad）的健康。这地狱的酒浆把他们肆无忌惮的渎神气焰推到了极处，教他们说出满嘴亵渎的话。他们恣意卖弄俏皮，拿巴兰的驴、七睡者的狗，以及蒙准进入穆罕默德天堂的其余畜生打趣。他们就带着这般轻快的心情走下一万一千级楼梯，一路从塔身的射孔里眺望广场上一张张焦虑的脸，取笑一番，最后由地下通道回到了王室的寝宫。巴巴巴鲁克正来回踱着步，煞有介事地向太监们发号施令——太监们有的剪着灯花，有的给切尔克斯（Circassian）美人画着眼线。他一看见哈里发母子便嚷起来：

“哈！这么说，我看你们是从火里逃出来了；不过我先前可没少犯嘀咕。”

“你先前想什么、如今想什么，与我们何干？”卡拉希丝喝道，“去，快去，告诉摩拉卡纳巴德我们立刻要见他；小心别在路上停下来发你那些乏味的高论。”

===Original===


Morakanabad delayed not to obey the summons, and was received by Vathek and his mother with great solemnity. They told him with an air of composure and commiseration that the fire at the top of the tower was extinguished, but that it had cost the lives of the brave people who sought to assist them.

“Still more misfortunes!” cried Morakanabad with a sigh. “Ah, commander of the faithful, our holy prophet is certainly irritated against us! it behoves you to appease him.”

“We will appease him hereafter,” replied the Caliph, with a smile that augured nothing of good. “You will have leisure sufficient for your supplications during my absence; for this country is the bane of my health. I am disgusted with the mountain of the Four Fountains, and am resolved to go and drink of the stream of Rocnabad.12 I long to refresh myself in the delightful valleys which it waters. Do you, with the advice of my mother, govern my dominions; and take care to supply whatever her experiments may demand; for you well know that our tower abounds in materials for the advancement of science.”

The tower but ill suited Morakanabad’s taste. Immense treasures had been lavished upon it, and nothing had he ever seen carried thither but female negroes, mutes, and abominable drugs. Nor did he know well what to think of Carathis, who like a chamelion could assume all possible colours. Her cursed eloquence had often driven the poor Mussulman to his last shifts. He considered, however, that if she possessed but few good qualities, her son had still fewer, and that the alternative, on the whole, would be in her favour. Consoled, therefore, with this reflection, he went in good spirits to soothe the populace, and make the proper arrangements for his master’s journey.

===Chinese===


摩拉卡纳巴德即刻应召而来，瓦提克母子极为郑重地接见了他。他们带着镇定而悲悯的神情告诉他，塔顶的火已经灭了，只是搭上了那些前来救应的勇敢人手的性命。

“又是灾祸！”摩拉卡纳巴德叹息道，“啊，信士们的长官，我们的圣先知定是对我们动怒了！你该去平息他的怒气才是。”

“往后再平息吧，”哈里发答道，脸上那笑容可不预示着什么好事。“我不在的时候，你有的是工夫去祈祷；因为这地方毁我的身子。我厌倦了四泉山，决意去喝罗克纳巴德（Rocnabad）12 的溪水。我渴望到它滋润的那些赏心悦目的山谷里爽快爽快。你呢，依着我母亲的指点，替我掌管我的疆土；她那些实验要什么，你都得留心供应；你满知道，咱们这座塔楼里，推进学术的材料可多得很。”

这座塔楼实在不合摩拉卡纳巴德的口味。泼在上面的钱财不计其数，可他眼见运进去的，从来只有黑人女子、哑仆和种种可憎的药物。他也说不清该把卡拉希丝看作什么人——她像一条变色龙，什么颜色都变得出来。她那可诅咒的口才，常逼得这可怜的穆斯林走投无路。不过他转念一想，她纵然好品质无多，她儿子的好品质更少，两害相权，终归还是她强些。他这样一想便得了安慰，兴致很好地去安抚百姓，替君王的行程做妥当的安排去了。

===Original===


Vathek, to conciliate the spirits of the subterranean palace, resolved that his expedition should be uncommonly splendid. With this view he confiscated on all sides the property of his subjects, whilst his worthy mother stripped the seraglios she visited of the gems they contained. She collected all the sempstresses and embroiderers of Samarah, and other cities, to the distance of sixty leagues, to prepare pavilions, palanquins, sofas, canopies, and litters, for the train of the monarch. There was not left in Masulipatan a single piece of chintz; and so much muslin had been bought up to dress out Bababalouk and the other black eunuchs, that there remained not an ell in the whole Irak of Babylon.

During these preparations, Carathis, who never lost sight of her great object, which was to obtain favour with the powers of darkness, made select parties of the fairest and most delicate ladies of the city; but in the midst of their gaiety she contrived to introduce serpents amongst them, and to break pots of scorpions under the table. They all bit to a wonder, and Carathis would have left them to bite, were it not that to fill up the time, she now and then amused herself in curing their wounds with an excellent anodyne of her own invention; for this good princess abhorred being indolent.

===Chinese===


瓦提克为了讨好地下宫殿的诸灵，决意把这一趟出行办得盛况空前。为此他四处抄没臣民的财产，他那贤德的母亲则把所到之内院闺房的宝石搜刮一空。她把萨迈拉和方圆六十里格内各城的缝衣妇、绣娘统统征来，为君王的随从队伍赶制帷殿、肩舆、长榻、华盖和步辇。马苏利帕塔姆（Masulipatan）连一匹印花布都没剩下；为了装扮巴巴巴鲁克和其余的黑太监，细平布也买去那么多，整个巴比伦的伊拉克（Irak of Babylon）连一厄尔的布头都不剩了。

备办期间，卡拉希丝始终没忘记她的宏图大旨——讨得黑暗诸势力的欢心。她精心张罗了一场场聚会，请的都是城里最美貌娇贵的夫人小姐；可正当她们行乐之际，她设法把毒蛇混进人丛，又把一罐罐蝎子在桌下摔破。她们个个被咬，咬得出奇地齐整；卡拉希丝本想由着她们咬去，只是闲着也是闲着，她不时拿自己发明的一种上好的止痛药给她们治伤，聊以自娱；因为这位好心的公主最恨无所事事。

===Original===


Vathek, who was not altogether so active as his mother, devoted his time to the sole gratification of his senses, in the palaces which were severally dedicated to them. He disgusted himself no more with the divan or the mosque. One half of Samarah followed his example, whilst the other lamented the progress of corruption.

In the midst of these transactions, the embassy returned which had been sent in pious times to Mecca. It consisted of the most reverend moullahs,13 who had fulfilled their commission, and brought back one of those precious besoms which are used to sweep the sacred kaaba; a present truly worthy of the greatest potentate on earth!

The Caliph happened at this instant to be engaged in an apartment by no means adapted to the reception of embassies, though adorned with a certain magnificence, not only to render it agreeable, but also because he resorted to it frequently, and stayed a considerable time together. Whilst occupied in this retreat, he heard the voice of Bababalouk calling out from between the door and the tapestry that hung before it:

===Chinese===


瓦提克不及母亲那般勤快，便把光阴尽数用于声色之娱，消磨在分门别类侍奉五官的诸座宫殿里。他再也不肯为御前会议和清真寺败自己的兴。萨迈拉半城人效法他的榜样，另外半城则为世风日下而痛心。

正当诸事纷纭之际，先前在虔诚年月里派往麦加（Mecca）的使团回来了。团中皆是极可敬的毛拉（moullahs）13，他们已办完了差事，带回一把用以清扫圣殿克尔白（kaaba）的宝帚；这份礼物，当真配得上地上最伟大的君主！

不巧的是，哈里发此时正在一间绝不适合接见使团的屋子里忙着，虽说屋里也装点得颇有几分富丽——既为的是住着惬意，也因为他常来此地，一待便是好半晌。他正隐在这里忙活，忽听巴巴巴鲁克在门与门帘之间高声禀报：

===Original===


“Here are the excellent Muhammad Ebn Edris al Shafei, and the seraphic Al Mouhadethin, who have brought the besom from Mecca, and with tears of joy entreat they may present it to your majesty in person.”

“Let them bring the besom hither, it may be of use,” said Vathek, who was still employed, not having quite racked off his wine.

“How!” answered Bababalouk, half aloud and amazed.

“Obey,” replied the Caliph, “for it is my sovereign will; go instantly! vanish! for here will I receive the good folk who have thus filled thee with joy.”

The eunuch departed muttering, and bade the venerable train attend him. A sacred rapture was diffused amongst these reverend old men. Though fatigued with the length of their expedition, they followed Bababalouk with an alertness almost miraculous, and felt themselves highly flattered as they swept along the stately porticos, that the Caliph would not receive them like ambassadors in ordinary, in his hall of audience. Soon reaching the interior of the harem (where, through blinds of persian they perceived large soft eyes, dark and blue, that went and came like lightning) penetrated with respect and wonder, and full of their celestial mission, they advanced in procession towards the small corridors that appeared to terminate in nothing, but nevertheless led to the cell where the Caliph expected their coming.

===Chinese===


“尊贵的穆罕默德·伊本·伊德里斯·沙菲（Muhammad Ebn Edris al Shafei）和超凡入圣的阿尔·穆哈德辛（Al Mouhadethin）到了，他们从麦加捧来了宝帚，喜泪盈眶，恳求亲自呈交陛下御前。”

“叫他们把宝帚拿到这儿来，说不定有用处，”瓦提克说——他还忙着呢，杯中之物尚未滗到尽兴。

“这怎么成！”巴巴巴鲁克半出声地应道，惊愕不已。

“照办，”哈里发答道，“这是朕的圣意；快去！走开！这几位好人既然叫你欢喜成这样，朕就在这儿接见他们。”

太监咕哝着退了出去，请那可敬的一行人跟上。一种神圣的狂喜在这些可敬的老者中间弥漫开来。他们虽已因长途跋涉而疲惫，跟随巴巴巴鲁克时却敏捷得近乎奇迹；一路行过巍峨的柱廊，他们还暗自得意，觉得哈里发不肯像接见寻常使臣那样在正殿受礼，实在抬举了他们。不多时他们便到了内宫深处（在那里，透过波斯细帘，他们瞥见一只只大而温柔的眼睛，黑的蓝的，如闪电般来去），他们满怀敬畏与惊叹，一心记着上天的使命，列队走向那些看似没有尽头的小廊——廊子却通向那间小室，哈里发正在那里等他们到来。

===Original===


“What! is the commander of the faithful sick?” said Ebn Edris al Shafei, in a low voice to his companion.

“I rather think he is in his oratory,” answered Al Mouhadethin.

Vathek, who heard the dialogue, cried out, “What imports it you how I am employed? approach without delay.”

They advanced, and Bababalouk almost sunk with confusion,14 whilst the Caliph, without showing himself, put forth his hand from behind the tapestry that hung before the door, and demanded of them the besom.

===Chinese===


“怎么！信士们的长官病了么？”伊本·伊德里斯·沙菲低声问同伴。

“我看他是在小礼拜堂里，”阿尔·穆哈德辛答道。

瓦提克听见了这段对话，喊道：“朕怎么忙着，与你们何干？还不快上前。”

他们上前去，巴巴巴鲁克窘得几乎要钻进地缝 14；哈里发却不露面，只从门帘后伸出手来，向他们索要宝帚。

===Original===


Having prostrated themselves as well as the corridor would permit, and even in a tolerable semicircle, the venerable Al Shafei, drawing forth the besom from the embroidered and perfumed scarfs in which it had been enveloped, and secured from the profane gaze of vulgar eyes, arose from his associates and advanced with an air of the most awful solemnity towards the supposed oratory; but with what astonishment! with what horror was he seized!

Vathek, bursting out into a villainous laugh, snatched the besom from his trembling hand, and fixing upon it some cobwebs that hung suspended from the ceiling, gravely brushed away till not a single one remained.

The old men, overpowered with amazement, were unable to lift their beards from the ground; for as Vathek had carelessly left the tapestry between them half drawn, they were witnesses to the whole transaction. Their tears gushed forth on the marble. Al Mouhadethin swooned through mortification and fatigue, whilst the Caliph, throwing himself backward on his seat, shouted and clapped his hands without mercy. At last, addressing himself to Bababalouk:

“My dear black,” said he, “go, regale these pious poor souls with my good wine from Shiraz; and as they can boast of having seen more of my palace than anyone besides, let them also visit my office courts, and lead them out by the back steps that go to my stables.” Having said this, he threw the besom in their face, and went to enjoy the laugh with Carathis.

===Chinese===


众人在廊中尽可能地面伏于地，居然还围成一个像样的半圆；尊贵的沙菲老人从层层绣香帕巾的包裹里——那是为防俗眼亵渎的目光——取出宝帚，从同伴中间站起，带着最肃穆可畏的神气朝那想必是礼拜堂的地方走去；可是何等的惊愕！何等的恐怖攫住了他！

瓦提克爆出一阵恶棍似的狂笑，从他颤抖的手中夺过宝帚，对准悬在顶棚上的蛛网，一本正经地扫除起来，直到一根蛛丝也不剩。

老人们惊得瘫软，胡须抵着地面抬不起头来；原来瓦提克漫不经心，他们之间的门帘只拉上了一半，这一幕勾当他们看了个通通透透。他们的泪水在大理石上迸流。阿尔·穆哈德辛又羞又乏，晕了过去；哈里发却仰倒在座位上，毫不留情地又是叫嚷又是拍手。末了，他向巴巴巴鲁克说道：

“我亲爱的黑家伙，”他说，“去，拿我设拉子（Shiraz）的好酒款待这些虔诚的可怜人；他们既能夸口说比我宫里任何人都多看了些地方，那就也让他们观光观光我的办事庭院，再从通马厩的后台阶把他们送出去。”说罢，他把宝帚掷到他们脸上，自去和卡拉希丝共享这场大笑去了。

===Original===


Bababalouk did all in his power to console the ambassadors, but the two most infirm expired on the spot; the rest were carried to their beds, from whence, being heartbroken with sorrow and shame, they never arose.

The succeeding night, Vathek, attended by his mother, ascended the tower to see if everything were ready for his journey, for he had great faith in the influence of the stars. The planets appeared in their most favourable aspects. The Caliph, to enjoy so flattering a sight, supped gaily on the roof, and fancied that he heard, during his repast, loud shouts of laughter resound through the sky, in a manner that inspired the fullest assurance.

All was in motion at the palace; lights were kept burning through the whole of the night; the sound of implements, and of artisans finishing their work; the voices of women and their guardians who sung at their embroidery; all conspired to interrupt the stillness of nature, and infinitely delight the heart of Vathek, who imagined himself going in triumph to sit upon the throne of Soliman.

===Chinese===


巴巴巴鲁克使出浑身解数宽慰使臣们，无奈两位最衰弱的当场咽了气；其余的被抬回床上，从此怀着悲伤和羞愧断了肝肠，再没有起来。

第二天夜里，瓦提克由母亲陪着登上塔楼，看看行装可曾齐备——他对星象的影响力向来笃信不疑。诸行星正显出最吉利的方位。哈里发为饱览这般可喜的景象，在屋顶上快活地用了晚餐，席间还仿佛听见响亮的笑声震荡天空，那声势给了他十足的信心。

宫里处处都在动；灯火彻夜不熄；器具的声响，工匠们赶完活计的声响；妇人和监守她们的人一面刺绣一面歌唱的歌声；这一切交织成一片，搅扰了天地的宁静，也叫瓦提克的心欢喜无限——他想象着自己正凯旋而去，坐上苏莱曼的宝座。

===Original===


The people were not less satisfied than himself; all assisted to accelerate the moment which should rescue them from the wayward caprices of so extravagant a master.

The day preceding the departure of this infatuated prince was employed by Carathis in repeating to him the decrees of the mysterious parchment, which she had thoroughly gotten by heart; and in recommending him not to enter the habitation of anyone by the way; “for well thou knowest,” added she, “how liquorish thy taste is after good dishes and young damsels; let me therefore enjoin thee to be content with thy old cooks, who are the best in the world; and not to forget that in thy ambulatory seraglio there are three dozen pretty faces, which Bababalouk hath not yet unveiled. I, myself, have a great desire to watch over thy conduct, and visit the subterranean palace, which no doubt contains whatever can interest persons like us. There is nothing so pleasing as retiring to caverns; my taste for dead bodies and everything like mummy is decided; and I am confident thou wilt see the most exquisite of their kind. Forget me not then, but the moment thou art in possession of the talismans which are to open to thee the mineral kingdoms, and the centre of the earth itself, fail not to dispatch some trusty genius to take me and my cabinet, for the oil of the serpents I have pinched to death will be a pretty present to the Giaour, who cannot but be charmed with such dainties.”

Scarcely had Carathis ended this edifying discourse, when the sun, setting behind the mountain of the Four Fountains, gave place to the rising moon. This planet being that evening at full, appeared of unusual beauty and magnitude in the eyes of the women, the eunuchs, and the pages, who were all impatient to set forward. The city reechoed with shouts of joy and flourishing of trumpets. Nothing was visible but plumes nodding on pavilions, and aigrettes shining in the mild lustre of the moon. The spacious square resembled an immense parterre, variegated with the most stately tulips of the East.

===Chinese===


百姓们也同他一样心满意足；人人都帮着催促那个时辰快些到来，好把他们从这般骄纵无常的主子手里解脱出去。

这位执迷不悟的君王启程的前一日，卡拉希丝把那卷神秘羊皮纸上的谕令背得烂熟，一遍遍念给他听，又叮嘱他沿途切莫踏进任何人的居所；“因为你满知道，”她补充道，“你这张嘴见了美馔和妙龄女子有多馋；所以我劝你知足，守着你那些天下第一的老厨子；也别忘了，你那座行走的后宫里还有三十六张俏脸蛋，巴巴巴鲁克还没揭开面纱呢。我自己么，很想照管着你的行事，也去看看那座地下宫殿——里头无疑尽是合我们这类人口味的东西。再没有什么比退居岩穴更惬意的了；我对死尸和一切木乃伊之类的东西，爱好是拿定了的；我准知道你将见到其中最精妙的藏品。那便别忘了我：你一得到那些护符——它们将为你开启诸矿王国乃至大地的中心——即刻打发一个可靠的精灵来接我和我的橱柜，我掐死的那些蛇熬出的油，正是给吉奥尔的一件漂亮礼物，这样的美馔他岂有不着迷的。”

卡拉希丝这篇教诲的话音刚落，太阳便落到四泉山后，让位给东升的月亮。当晚正值满月，在妇人、太监和侍童们眼里显得格外壮丽硕大——他们个个急不可耐，只盼启程。全城回荡着欢呼与嘹亮的号角。放眼望去，只见羽饰在帷殿顶上摇曳，鹭羽在温柔的月华里闪烁。宽阔的广场宛如一座巨大的花坛，其间装点着东方最雍容的郁金香。

===Original===


Arrayed in the robes which were only worn at the most distinguished ceremonials, and supported by his vizier and Bababalouk, the Caliph descended the grand staircase of the tower in the sight of all his people. He could not forbear pausing at intervals to admire the superb appearance which everywhere courted his view, whilst the whole multitude, even to the camels with their sumptuous burdens, knelt down before him. For some time a general stillness prevailed, which nothing happened to disturb, but the shrill screams of some eunuchs in the rear. These vigilant guards having remarked certain cages of the ladies swagging somewhat awry, and discovered that a few adventurous gallants had contrived to get in, soon dislodged the enraptured culprits, and consigned them with good commendations, to the surgeons of the serail. The majesty of so magnificent a spectacle was not, however, violated by incidents like these. Vathek, meanwhile, saluted the moon with an idolatrous air, that neither pleased Morakanabad nor the doctors of the law, any more than the viziers and grandees of his court, who were all assembled to enjoy the last view of their sovereign.

At length the clarions and trumpets from the top of the tower announced the prelude of departure. Though the instruments were in unison with each other, yet a singular dissonance was blended with their sounds. This proceeded from Carathis, who was singing her direful orisons to the Giaour, whilst the negresses and mutes supplied thorough bass without articulating a word. The good Mussulmans fancied that they heard the sullen hum of those nocturnal insects which presage evil, and importuned Vathek to beware how he ventured his sacred person.

===Chinese===


哈里发身披唯有最隆重的典礼才穿的名贵朝服，由维齐尔和巴巴巴鲁克左右搀扶，在全城百姓的注视下走下塔楼的大阶梯。他一路不时驻足，忍不住欣赏处处争相入眼的壮丽景象；与此同时，全体民众，连驮着华贵重物的骆驼在内，都跪拜在他面前。有一阵子，四下里一片肃静，唯有队尾几个太监的尖叫声来搅扰。这些警觉的守卫发现某些女眷的笼轿歪得有点不对，又查出几个胆大包天的风流客设法钻了进去，很快便把那班心醉神迷的罪人揪了出来，连同几句夸赞，送交后宫的外科医生发落。这般小事自不敢有损这宏大场面的威严。瓦提克此时正以偶像崇拜者的姿态向月亮致意，这副做派既不入摩拉卡纳巴德和法博士们的眼，也不合他宫廷里维齐尔和贵胄们的心——他们都聚在此处，想最后再瞻仰一眼他们的君王。

终于，塔顶上号角与喇叭齐鸣，奏起了启程的序曲。乐器的音调彼此和谐，声响里却掺着一缕古怪的不谐之音。那是卡拉希丝发出来的——她正对吉奥尔唱着凶险的祷词，黑人女仆和哑仆们则不发一言地替她配着低音。善良的穆斯林们只觉得自己听见了报凶的夜虫那阴沉的嗡鸣，便苦苦劝瓦提克珍重他神圣的御体，切莫轻身涉险。

===Original===


On a given signal the great standard of the Caliphate was displayed; twenty thousand lances shone around it; and the Caliph, treading royally on the cloth of gold which had been spread for his feet, ascended his litter amidst the general awe that possessed his subjects.

The expedition commenced with the utmost order, and so entire a silence, that even the locusts were heard from the thickets on the plain of Catoul. Gaiety and good humour prevailing, six good leagues were past before the dawn; and the morning star was still glittering in the firmament when the whole of this numerous train had halted on the banks of the Tigris, where they encamped to repose for the rest of the day.

The three days that followed were spent in the same manner, but on the fourth the heavens looked angry, lightnings broke forth in frequent flashes, reechoing peals of thunder succeeded, and the trembling Circassians clung with all their might to their ugly guardians. The Caliph himself was greatly inclined to take shelter in the large town of Gulchissar, the governor of which came forth to meet him, and tendered every kind of refreshment the place could supply. But having examined his tablets, he suffered the rain to soak him almost to the bone, notwithstanding the importunity of his first favourites. Though he began to regret the palace of the senses, yet he lost not sight of his enterprise, and his sanguine expectations confirmed his resolution. His geographers were ordered to attend him, but the weather proved so terrible, that these poor people exhibited a lamentable appearance; and as no long journeys had been undertaken since the time of Haroun al Raschid, their maps of the different countries were in a still worse plight than themselves. Everyone was ignorant which way to turn; for Vathek, though well versed in the course of the heavens, no longer knew his situation on earth. He thundered even louder than the elements, and muttered forth certain hints of the bowstring which were not very soothing to literary ears. Disgusted at the toilsome weariness of the way, he determined to cross over the craggy heights, and follow the guidance of a peasant, who undertook to bring him, in four days, to Rocnabad. Remonstrances were all to no purpose, his resolution was fixed, and an invasion commenced on the province of the goats, who sped away in large troops before them. It was curious to view on these half-calcined rocks camels richly caparisoned, and pavilions of gold and silk waving on their summits, which till then had never been covered, but with sapless thistles and fern.

===Chinese===


号令一下，哈里发帝国的大纛高高扬起；两万支长枪在四周闪光；哈里发踏着为他双脚铺开的金锦地毯，帝王气派地登上了步辇，臣民们无不肃然敬畏。

大军启程，秩序井然，一片整肃的寂静，连卡图勒（Catoul）平原灌木丛里的蝗虫声都听得见。一路上欢声笑语，天未破晓便走足了六里格；启明星还在天际闪烁，这一大队人马已停在底格里斯河（Tigris）岸边，就地扎营，歇息这一日的剩余时光。

此后三日皆是如此行进，到了第四天，天色震怒，电光频闪，雷声隆隆相续，瑟瑟发抖的切尔克斯美人们死命搂住她们那些丑陋的守护人。哈里发本很想躲进大城古尔奇萨尔（Gulchissar）避一避，城主也出城迎驾，献上本地能办到的一切供应。可他查了查随身的书板，便任凭雨水把全身浸得几乎透骨，任凭心腹宠臣们苦劝也不理会。他虽已开始怀念那座感官之宫，却没有忘记自己的大业，一腔乐观的指望反而愈发坚定了他的决心。他传地理学者们前来伺候，无奈天气坏到极处，这些可怜人狼狈得叫人不忍卒睹；又因自哈伦·阿尔·拉希德（Haroun al Raschid）以来再没有人远行过，他们那些各国地图比他们本人还要凄惨。人人不知该往哪里拐才好；原来瓦提克虽熟谙天象的运行，却认不得自己在地上的方位了。他发起雷霆来比风雷还响，还咕哝着抛出几句弓弦绞刑的暗示，这话落在读书人耳朵里可不太受用。他厌透了这一路的辛苦劳顿，决意翻越嶙峋的群山，跟着一个农夫的指引走——那农夫夸口四天之内便能带他到罗克纳巴德。众人的谏阻全然无用，他主意已定，于是一场入侵便在山羊的国土上开始了，成群的山羊在他们前面飞奔而逃。这些半煅烧的岩石上，只见鞍辔华丽的骆驼逶迤而行，金丝绸缎的帷殿在峰顶上招展——那些峰顶此前除了枯萎的蓟草和蕨草，还从未被别的东西覆盖过，这景象煞是好看。

===Original===


The females and eunuchs uttered shrill wailings at the sight of the precipices below them, and the dreary prospects that opened in the vast gorges of the mountains. Before they could reach the ascent of the steepest rock night overtook them, and a boisterous tempest arose, which having rent the awnings of the palanquins and cages, exposed to the raw gusts the poor ladies within, who had never before felt so piercing a cold. The dark clouds that overcast the face of the sky deepened the horrors of this disastrous night, insomuch that nothing could be heard distinctly but the mewling of pages, and lamentations of sultanas.

To increase the general misfortune, the frightful uproar of wild beasts resounded at a distance, and there were soon perceived in the forest they were skirting the glaring of eyes which could belong only to devils or tigers. The pioneers, who as well as they could, had marked out a track, and a part of the advanced guard were devoured before they had been in the least apprised of their danger. The confusion that prevailed was extreme. Wolves, tigers, and other carnivorous animals, invited by the howling of their companions, flocked together from every quarter. The crushing of bones was heard on all sides, and a fearful rush of wings over head, for now vultures also began to be of the party.

The terror at length reached the main body of the troops which surrounded the monarch and his harem, at the distance of two leagues from the scene. Vathek (voluptuously reposed in his capacious litter upon cushions of silk, with two little pages beside him, of complexions more fair than the enamel of Franguestan, who were occupied in keeping off flies) was soundly asleep, and contemplating in his dreams the treasures of Soliman. The shrieks, however, of his wives awoke him with a start, and instead of the Giaour with his key of gold, he beheld Bababalouk full of consternation.

===Chinese===


妇人和太监们望见脚下的绝壁和大山峡谷间豁然洞开的荒凉景象，发出尖利的哀号。他们还没来得及登上最陡的那座岩石，夜色便追了上来，又起了一场狂暴的风暴，把肩舆和笼轿的帷盖撕成碎片，害得轿中可怜的夫人们赤裸裸暴露在阴冷的疾风里——她们生平从未感受过这般刺骨的寒气。满天乌云遮蔽了天空的面容，加深了这灾祸之夜的恐怖，以致什么都听不真切，唯有侍童们的低泣和苏丹女眷们的哀叹。

雪上加霜的是，野兽的骇人吼声又在远处响起，不多时，他们擦边而过的那片森林里便有一双双灼灼的目光闪动，那绝不是别的，只能是魔鬼或老虎的眼睛。工兵们勉勉强强标出了一条小径，前哨的一部分人还没来得及察觉半点危险，便已葬身兽腹。四下一片大乱。狼、虎和别的食肉野兽听见同伴的嗥叫，从四面八方成群赶来。四面八方都听得见啃碎骨头的声响，头顶上还有一阵阵可怕的鼓翅之声——兀鹰如今也来入伙了。

这场惊恐终于传到了两里格之外、环卫着君王及其后宫的大队人马那里。瓦提克正舒舒服服躺在他那宽敞的步辇里，倚着锦缎软垫酣睡，身旁侍立着两个小侍童，肤色比法兰吉斯坦（Franguestan）的珐琅还要白净，正忙着替他驱赶苍蝇——他在梦里还端详着苏莱曼的宝藏。然而妻妾们的尖叫惊得他猛醒过来，眼前没有捧着金钥匙的吉奥尔，只有吓破了胆的巴巴巴鲁克。

===Original===


“Sire,” exclaimed this good servant of the most potent of monarchs, “misfortune has arrived at its height; wild beasts, who entertain no more reverence for your sacred person than for that of a dead ass, have beset your camels and their drivers: thirty of the richest laden are already become their prey, as well as all your confectioners, your cooks, and purveyors, and unless our holy prophet should protect us, we shall have all eaten our last meal.”

At the mention of eating, the Caliph lost all patience. He began to bellow, and even beat himself, for there was no seeing in the dark. The rumour every instant increased, and Bababalouk finding no good could be done with his master stopped both his ears against the hurly-burly of the harem, and called out aloud:

“Come, ladies and brothers! all hands to work! strike light in a moment! never shall it be said that the commander of the faithful served to regale these infidel brutes.”

===Chinese===


“陛下，”这位万王之王的忠实仆人嚷道，“大祸临头了；野兽们对您神圣御体的敬意，比对一头死驴的多不了半分，它们已经围住了您的骆驼和赶驼人：驮载最丰的三十峰已经落入它们的口中，还有您全部的糖点师傅、厨子和采买；若不是我们神圣的先知保佑，咱们这顿就要成最后的晚餐了。”

一提到吃，哈里发便再也按捺不住。他吼叫起来，甚至动手捶打自己——黑灯瞎火里什么也看不见。喊杀声一浪高过一浪，巴巴巴鲁克见在主子身上再讨不出好来，便堵上双耳不听后宫那边的吵嚷，高声喊道：

“来呀，夫人们，弟兄们！大家一齐动手！立刻打着火！绝不能让后人传说，信士们的长官竟是给这班异教畜生上菜的。”

===Original===


Though there wanted not in this bevy of beauties a sufficient number of capricious and wayward, yet, on the present occasion they were all compliance. Fires were visible in a twinkling in all their cages. Ten thousand torches were lighted at once. The Caliph himself seized a large one of wax; every person followed his example; and by kindling ropes ends dipped in oil and fastened on poles, an amazing blaze was spread. The rocks were covered with the splendour of sunshine. The trails of sparks wafted by the wind, communicated to the dry fern, of which there was plenty. Serpents were observed to crawl forth from their retreats with amazement and hissings, whilst the horses snorted, stamped the ground, tossed their noses in the air, and plunged about without mercy.

One of the forests of cedar that bordered their way took fire, and the branches that overhung the path extending their flames to the muslins and chintzes which covered the cages of the ladies, obliged them to jump out at the peril of their necks. Vathek, who vented on the occasion a thousand blasphemies, was himself compelled to touch with his sacred feet the naked earth.

Never had such an incident happened before. Full of mortification, shame and despondence, and not knowing how to walk, the ladies fell into the dirt.

===Chinese===


这一群美人里任性使气的本不在少数，可眼下这个当口，个个都百依百顺。转眼之间，她们各自的笼轿里都亮起了火。一万支火把同时点起。哈里发亲手抓过一支大蜡烛火把；人人跟着效仿；又把浸了油、绑在长竿上的绳头一一点燃，一片惊人的大火顿时铺展开来。岩石笼罩在艳阳般的光辉里。火星随风飘散，落到遍地都是的干蕨上，也烧了起来。只见群蛇又惊又嘶，从洞穴里爬将出来；马匹打着响鼻，刨着地面，扬起鼻子，没命地腾跃打转。

路旁的一片雪松林着了火，低垂在道上的枝桠把火焰伸向女眷笼轿上的细平布和印花布帷，逼得她们冒着跌断脖子的危险跳了出来。瓦提克一迭声骂了一千句渎神的话，自己也被迫让神圣的双足沾了沾裸露的土地。

这等变故真是旷古未闻。夫人们又羞又恼又绝望，加上不知道该怎么走路，纷纷跌进泥里。

===Original===


“Must I go on foot,” said one.

“Must I wet my feet,” cried another.

“Must I soil my dress,” asked a third.

“Execrable Bababalouk,” exclaimed all; “Outcast of hell! what hadst thou to do with torches? Better were it to be eaten by tigers than to fall into our present condition; we are forever undone. Not a porter is there in the army, nor a currier of camels but hath seen some part of our bodies, and what is worse, our very faces!”

===Chinese===


“难道要我步行么？”一位说。

“难道要我这双脚沾水么？”另一位嚷道。

“难道要我把衣裙弄脏么？”第三位问。

“该死的巴巴巴鲁克，”众人齐声嚷道，“地狱里撵出来的孽种！要火把做什么？给老虎吃掉也比落到这步田地强；我们算是永世不得翻身了。军中连一个挑夫、一个鞣驼皮的皮匠都没有漏掉，个个都看了我们身上的某处，更糟的是，连我们的脸都看了去！”

===Original===


On saying this, the most bashful amongst them hid their foreheads on the ground, whilst such as had more boldness flew at Bababalouk, but he, well apprised of their humour, and not wanting in shrewdness, betook himself to his heels along with his comrades, all dropping their torches and striking their tymbals.

It was not less light than in the brightest of the dog-days, and the weather was hot in proportion; but how degrading was the spectacle, to behold the Caliph bespattered like an ordinary mortal! As the exercise of his faculties seemed to be suspended, one of his Ethiopian wives (for he delighted in variety) clasped him in her arms, threw him upon her shoulder like a sack of dates, and finding that the fire was hemming them in, set off with no small expedition, considering the weight of her burden. The other ladies who had just learned the use of their feet followed her; their guards galloped after; and the camel drivers brought up the rear as fast as their charge would permit.

===Chinese===


话音未落，她们中最娇羞的把额头贴到了地上，胆子大些的便朝巴巴巴鲁克扑去；他深谙她们的心性，又伶俐得紧，早同他的弟兄们撒腿跑了，火把丢了一地，一路还敲着铜鼓。

天光亮得不亚于三伏天最晴的日子，天气也热得与之相称；可是这光景何其丢份——眼见哈里发竟像个凡夫俗子一样浑身泥污！他的神智官能似乎一时全停了摆，他的一位埃塞俄比亚（Ethiopian）妻子（他素来喜欢花样繁多）便把他搂进怀里，像扛一袋枣子似的往肩上一甩，见火势已经围拢过来，便迈开大步飞奔——念着她肩上这副担子的分量，这脚程实在算得上神速。其余刚刚学会用脚的夫人们跟在她后面；护卫们纵马紧随；赶驼人押着后队，在驼队脚力许可的范围内尽快跟上。

===Original===


They soon reached the spot where the wild beasts had commenced the carnage, and which they had too much spirit to leave, notwithstanding the approaching tumult, and the luxurious supper they had made. Bababalouk nevertheless seized on a few of the plumpest, which were unable to budge from the place, and began to flay them with admirable adroitness. The cavalcade being got so far from the conflagration as that the heat felt rather grateful than violent, it was immediately resolved on to halt. The tattered chintzes were picked up; the scraps left by the wolves and tigers interred; and vengeance was taken on some dozens of vultures that were too much glutted to rise on the wing. The camels which had been left unmolested to make sal-ammoniac being numbered, and the ladies once more enclosed in their cages, the imperial tent was pitched on the levellest ground they could find.

===Chinese===


众人不久便行至野兽们开始屠戮的地方——这些野兽尽管听得大队人马渐渐逼近，又都饱啖了一顿奢侈的盛餐，却兀自意气昂扬，不肯舍之而去。巴巴巴鲁克还是擒住了几只吃得最肥、再挪不动步的，以令人叹服的娴熟手法动手剥皮。此时大队人马已离火场颇远，热力非但不觉灼人，反倒令人受用，于是当即议定驻扎下来。撕破的印花布帷一一拾起；狼和虎吃剩的残骸就地掩埋；几十只饱嗉过度、再也振翅不起的秃鹫，也被拿住泄了愤。点过那些未受惊扰、仍旧照旧产制硇砂（sal-ammoniac）的骆驼数目，把夫人们重新关进笼轿之后，御帐便扎在了所能寻到的最平坦的地面上。

===Original===


Vathek, reposing upon a matress of down, and tolerably recovered from the jolting of the Ethiopian, who, to his feelings seemed the roughest trotting jade he had hitherto mounted, called out for something to eat; but alas! those delicate cakes which had been baked in silver ovens for his royal mouth, those rich manchets, amber comfits, flagons of Shiraz wine, porcelain vases of snow, and grapes from the banks of the Tigris, were all irremediably lost; and nothing had Bababalouk to present in their stead, but a roasted wolf, vultures *à la daube*, aromatic herbs of the most acrid poignancy, rotten truffles, boiled thistles, and such other wild plants as must ulcerate the throat and parch up the tongue. Nor was he better provided in the article of drink, for he could procure nothing to accompany these irritating viands but a few phials of abominable brandy, which had been secreted by the scullions in their slippers.

===Chinese===


瓦提克倚在一张羽绒床垫上，经那位埃塞俄比亚妇人的颠簸之后，总算缓过几分劲儿来——在他感觉里，那简直是他此生骑过的一匹最粗劣的颠蹄牝马——便开口传膳；可是天哪！那些在银炉中为他御口烤制的精巧糕饼、丰腴的白面包卷、琥珀色的蜜饯糖果、一瓶瓶设拉子葡萄酒、盛着冰雪的瓷瓶、底格里斯河岸的葡萄，全都无可挽回地失散了；巴巴巴鲁克拿不出别的东西来顶替，只有一只烤狼、几只按炖肉法（à la daube）煨制的秃鹫、辛辣刺鼻之极的香草、腐烂的块菌、煮熟的蓟草，以及其他必然叫人喉咙溃烂、舌头焦干的野生植物。饮品一项他也没有更好的筹措：除了几小瓶恶劣得可怕的白兰地——那是厨役们藏在拖鞋里私带出来的——再无别物，来送这些剌嗓子的吃食。

===Original===


Vathek made wry faces at so savage a repast, and Bababalouk answered them with shrugs and contortions. The Caliph however ate with tolerable appetite, and fell into a nap that lasted six hours. The splendour of the sun, reflected from the white cliffs of the mountains in spite of the curtains that enclosed him, at length disturbed his repose. He awoke terrified, and stung to the quick by those wormwood-coloured flies which emit from their wings a suffocating stench. The miserable monarch was perplexed how to act, though his wits were not idle in seeking expedients, whilst Bababalouk lay snoring amidst a swarm of those insects, that busily thronged to pay court to his nose. The little pages, famished with hunger, had dropped their fans on the ground, and exerted their dying voices in bitter reproaches on the Caliph, who now for the first time heard the language of truth.

===Chinese===


面对这样一顿野蛮的饭食，瓦提克直做苦脸，巴巴巴鲁克便以耸肩扭身相应。不过哈里发仍旧吃得颇有胃口，饭后一觉睡了六个钟头。山崖上白色的岩壁把太阳的光华反射进帐来，任凭层层帏幔围裹也遮拦不住，终于搅扰了他的清梦。他惊醒过来，又被那些苦艾色的苍蝇叮得刺痛难当——这种苍蝇双翅间会散发出令人窒息的恶臭。这可怜的君主一时进退失据，虽然脑子也没有闲着，一个劲儿搜寻对策；巴巴巴鲁克却躺在一大群苍蝇中间鼾声大作，那些苍蝇正忙着蜂拥而上，向他的鼻子献殷勤。小侍童们饿得发慌，扇子丢在地上，奄奄一息地扯着嗓子苦苦埋怨哈里发——他这还是有生以来头一遭，听见真话。

===Original===


Thus stimulated, he renewed his imprecations against the Giaour, and bestowed upon Muhammad some soothing expressions.

===Chinese===


受了这般刺激，他重新把吉奥尔咒骂了一遍，又对穆罕默德说了几句安抚告罪的话。

===Original===


“Where am I?” cried he; “What are these dreadful rocks; these valleys of darkness? Are we arrived at the horrible Kaf?15 Is the Simurgh16 coming to pluck out my eyes as a punishment for undertaking this impious enterprise?”

Having said this, he bellowed like a calf, and turned himself towards an outlet in the side of his pavilion. But alas! what objects occurred to his view! on one side a plain of black sand that appeared to be unbounded, and on the other perpendicular crags bristled over with those abominable thistles which had so severely lacerated his tongue. He fancied, however, that he perceived amongst the brambles and briars some gigantic flowers, but was mistaken, for these were only the dangling palampores and variegated tatters of his gay retinue. As there were several clefts in the rock from whence water seemed to have flowed, Vathek applied his ear with the hope of catching the sound of some latent runnel, but could only distinguish the low murmurs of his people, who were repining at their journey, and complaining for the want of water.

===Chinese===


“我在什么地方？”他喊道，“这些可怕的岩石是什么？这些黑暗的山谷又是什么？我们难道已经到了那可怖的卡夫山（Kaf）15？西摩格（Simurgh）16 莫非要来啄出我的双眼，惩罚我担当这桩渎神的营生？”

话音一落，他便像小牛犊一般吼叫起来，转身朝御帐侧面的一处出口走去。可是天哪！映入他眼帘的是怎样一番景象！一侧是一片望不到边际的黑沙平原，另一侧是壁立的危岩，上面密密麻麻生满那些可憎的蓟草——正是它们方才把他的舌头割得稀烂。他还以为自己望见荆棘榛莽之间生着几朵硕大无朋的花，却是看错了：那不过是随行的艳服队伍里垂挂下来的印花绸布和斑斓的破布条罢了。岩石上有几道裂罅，似乎曾有水流从那里淌过，瓦提克便把耳朵贴上去，指望听见什么暗泉的声响，却只辨得出臣仆们的低声抱怨——他们正怨叹这趟行程，叫苦没有水喝。

===Original===


“To what purpose,” asked they, “have we been brought hither? Hath our Caliph another tower to build? or have the relentless Afreets17 whom Carathis so much loves, fixed in this place their abode?”

At the name of Carathis, Vathek recollected the tablets he had received from his mother, who assured him they were fraught with preternatural qualities, and advised him to consult them as emergencies might require. Whilst he was engaged in turning them over, he heard a shout of joy, and a loud clapping of hands. The curtains of his pavilion were soon drawn back, and he beheld Bababalouk, followed by a troop of his favourites, conducting two dwarfs, each a cubit high, who brought between them a large basket of melons, oranges, and pomegranites. They were singing in the sweetest tones the words that follow:

===Chinese===


“把我们带到这儿来，究竟是为了什么？”他们问道，“咱们的哈里发还有一座塔要造么？还是说，卡拉希丝那般宠爱的那些无情的阿弗里特（Afreets）17，把居处安在了此地？”

听到卡拉希丝的名字，瓦提克想起母亲给他的那几块书板——她曾向他担保，这些书板蕴含超自然的威力，并嘱他遇有急难，便取来请教。他正翻检之间，忽听得一阵欢呼，还有响亮的击掌声。御帐的帏幔随即掀开，只见巴巴巴鲁克领着一队亲随，引着两个侏儒进来——每个身高一肘，两人合抬着一大筐甜瓜、香橙和石榴。他们正用最清甜的嗓音，唱着下面这些话：

===Original===


“We dwell on the top of these rocks, in a cabin of rushes and canes; the eagles envy us our nest; a small spring supplies us with abdest, and we daily repeat prayers which the prophet approves. We love you, O commander of the faithful! our master, the good emir Fakreddin, loves you also; he reveres in your person the vicegerent of Muhammad. Little as we are, in us he confides; he knows our hearts to be good, as our bodies are contemptible, and hath placed us here to aid those who are bewildered on these dreary mountains.

===Chinese===


“我们栖身在这些岩顶之上，住一间灯心草和藤条搭成的小屋；苍鹰都艳羡我们的巢；一眼小泉供给我们净礼用的净水（abdest），我们每日诵念先知嘉许的祷文。我们爱您，信士们的长官（commander of the faithful）！我们的主人、善良的埃米尔法克雷丁（Fakreddin）也爱您；他在您身上尊奉着穆罕默德的代权者。我们虽然渺小，他却信托我们；他知道我们的心是好的，正如我们的身体是可鄙的，所以把我们安置在此，救助那些在这凄凉群山中迷路的人。

===Original===


Last night, whilst we were occupied within our cell in reading the holy Koran, a sudden hurricane blew out our lights and rocked our habitation. For two whole hours a palpable darkness prevailed: but we heard sounds at a distance which we conjectured to proceed from the bells of a cafila, passing over the rocks. Our ears were soon filled with deplorable shrieks, frightful roarings, and the sound of tymbals. Chilled with terror, we concluded that the Deggial18 with his exterminating angels had sent forth their plagues on the earth. In the midst of these melancholy reflections, we perceived flames of the deepest red glow in the horizon, and found ourselves in a few moments covered with flakes of fire. Amazed at so strange an appearance, we took up the volume dictated by the blessed intelligence, and kneeling by the light of the fire that surrounded us, we recited the verse which says: ‘Put no trust in anything but the mercy of heaven; there is no help save in the holy prophet; the mountain of Kaf itself may tremble; it is the power of Alla only that cannot be moved.’ After having pronounced these words, we felt consolation, and our minds were hushed into a sacred repose. Silence ensued, and our ears clearly distinguished a voice in the air, saying: ‘Servants of my faithful servant, go down to the happy valley of Fakreddin; tell him that an illustrious opportunity now offers to satiate the thirst of his hospitable heart. The commander of true believers is this day bewildered amongst these mountains, and stands in need of thy aid.’ We obeyed with joy the angelic mission, and our master, filled with pious zeal, hath culled with his own hands these melons, oranges, and pomegranites. He is following us with a hundred dromedaries laden with the purest waters of his fountains, and is coming to kiss the fringe of your consecrated robe, and implore you to enter his humble habitation, which, placed amidst these barren wilds, resembles an emerald set in lead.”

===Chinese===


昨夜，我们正在小屋里诵读神圣的《古兰经》（Koran），一阵骤起的飓风吹灭了我们的灯火，摇撼着我们的居所。足足两个钟头，天地间一片浓得化不开的黑暗：但我们听见远处有声响，猜想是一支商队（cafila）的驼铃，正从岩石上经过。不多时，我们的耳朵便灌满了凄惨的尖号、骇人的咆哮和铜鼓之声。我们吓得浑身冰凉，认定是德贾尔（Deggial）18 率着他那些灭绝天使，把瘟疫放到了人间。正凄惶寻思间，我们望见天际烧起最深沉的红焰，转眼之间，火片便落满了我们全身。面对这般奇异的景象，我们惊愕不已，捧起那部由蒙福的睿智所口授的经典，跪在四围火光的照耀下，诵念那节经文：‘万事皆不可信赖，惟靠上天的仁慈；除神圣的先知外，别无救助；卡夫山自身也会震颤；惟有安拉（Alla）的大能永不动摇。’诵毕这些话，我们便觉宽慰，心境归于一种神圣的安宁。随后万籁俱寂，我们的耳朵清清楚楚分辨出空中有个声音说道：‘我忠实仆人的仆人们啊，下去到法克雷丁的欢乐谷吧；告诉他，一个显赫的机会如今送上门来，正好滋润他好客心肠中的渴念。信士们的长官今日在群山中迷了路，正需要你的援手。’我们欢欢喜喜地领了这天使的使命，我们的主人满怀虔诚的热忱，亲手采选了这些甜瓜、香橙和石榴。他正率领一百头驮着他泉中最纯净之水的单峰驼随后赶来；他要来亲吻您圣袍的流苏，恳请您光临他卑微的居所——那居所坐落在这些荒瘠的旷野之间，恰似镶在铅底上的一块翡翠。”

===Original===


The dwarfs having ended their address, remained still standing, and with hands crossed upon their bosoms, preserved a respectful silence.

Vathek, in the midst of this curious harangue seized the basket, and long before it was finished, the fruits had dissolved in his mouth. As he continued to eat, his piety increased, and in the same breath which recited his prayers, he called for the Koran and sugar.

Such was the state of his mind when the tablets, which were thrown by at the approach of the dwarfs, again attracted his eye. He took them up, but was ready to drop on the ground when he beheld, in large red characters, these words inscribed by Carathis, which were indeed enough to make him tremble.

===Chinese===


两个侏儒致完了辞，仍旧肃立不动，双手交叠在胸前，恭恭敬敬地保持着沉默。

这篇稀奇的演讲才到中途，瓦提克早已把筐子抢到手里，话还没说完，那些果子已在他口中化尽。他愈吃愈发虔诚，诵经祈祷的那一口气里，就便吩咐取《古兰经》和糖来。

他的心绪正处在这般光景，先前侏儒进帐时被随手丢开的那几块书板，又映入了他的眼帘。他把书板拾起，一看之下险些跌坐在地——上面以粗大的红字刻着卡拉希丝的手笔，那字句委实足以叫他发抖。

===Original===


“Beware of thy old doctors, and their puny messengers of but one cubit high; distrust their pious frauds; and instead of eating their melons, impale on a spit the bearers of them. Shouldst thou be such a fool as to visit them, the portal of the subterranean palace will be shut in thy face, and with such force as shall shake thee asunder; thy body shall be spit upon, and bats will engender in thy belly.”

“To what tends this ominous rhapsody?” cries the Caliph; “and must I then perish in these deserts with thirst, whilst I may refresh myself in the valley of melons and cucumbers? Accursed be the Giaour with his portal of ebony! he hath made me dance attendance too long already. Besides, who shall prescribe laws to me? I, forsooth, must not enter anyone’s habitation! Be it so, but what one can I enter that is not my own.”

===Chinese===


“当心那些老学究，还有那些身高不过一肘的孱弱信使；莫信他们虔诚的骗局；他们的甜瓜吃不得，倒该把送瓜的人串上烤叉。你若蠢到登门造访，地下宫殿的大门便会当面砸在你脸上，力道之猛，要把你震得散了架；你的尸身要受人唾弃，蝙蝠会在你肚子里做窝。”

“这段不祥的疯话到底用意何在？”哈里发嚷道，“难道要我渴死在这些荒漠里，而甜瓜黄瓜之谷明明可以让我一解暑热？该咒的吉奥尔和他的乌木之门！他叫我空等得太久了。再说，谁配给我立规矩？我，偏偏就不许进别人的家门！好啊，可除了我自己的门，我还能进哪一扇？”

===Original===


Bababalouk, who lost not a syllable of this soliloquy, applauded it with all his heart; and the ladies, for the first time, agreed with him in opinion. The dwarfs were entertained, caressed, and seated with great ceremony on little cushions of satin. The symmetry of their persons was the subject of criticism; not an inch of them was suffered to pass unexamined. Knick-knacks and dainties were offered in profusion, but all were declined with respectful gravity. They clambered up the sides of the Caliph’s seat, and placing themselves each on one of his shoulders, began to whisper prayers in his ears. Their tongues quivered like the leaves of a poplar, and the patience of Vathek was almost exhausted, when the acclamations of the troops announced the approach of Fakreddin, who was come with a hundred old greybeards, and as many korans and dromedaries. They instantly set about their ablutions, and began to repeat the Bismillah. Vathek, to get rid of these officious monitors, followed their example, for his hands were burning.

The good Emir, who was punctiliously religious, and likewise a great dealer in compliments, made an harangue five times more prolix and insipid than his harbingers had already delivered. The Caliph, unable any longer to refrain, exclaimed:

“For the love of Muhammad, my dear Fakreddin, have done! let us proceed to your valley, and enjoy the fruits that heaven hath vouchsafed you.” The hint of proceeding put all into motion. The venerable attendants of the emir set forward somewhat slowly, but Vathek having ordered his little pages, in private, to goad on the dromedaries, loud fits of laughter broke forth from the cages, for the unwieldy curvetting of these poor beasts, and the ridiculous distress of their superannuated riders afforded the ladies no small entertainment.

===Chinese===


这篇独白，巴巴巴鲁克一字不落全听在耳里，打心眼里喝彩；夫人们也破天荒头一遭与他所见相同。两个侏儒受到款待和爱抚，还被郑重其事地安置在缎面小坐垫上。他们身形的匀称成了众人评头论足的题目，一寸也没放过。小玩意儿和精美吃食流水般送上，都被他们端重地一一谢绝。他们攀上哈里发御座的两侧，一左一右在他肩头坐定，对着他的耳朵低声祷告起来。两条舌头抖得像白杨的叶子，瓦提克的耐性几乎就要耗尽，恰在此时，军中的欢呼声传告法克雷丁驾临——他带着一百位白胡子老人前来，随行的还有数目相当的《古兰经》和单峰驼。众人当即着手行小净，开始诵念泰斯米（Bismillah）。瓦提克为了摆脱这些殷勤的说教者，也依样行了个礼，因为他双手正烧得慌。

这位善良的埃米尔一丝不苟地虔敬，又极讲究客套，发表了一篇演说，比他那些先行官已经献上的还要冗长乏味五倍。哈里发再也无法忍耐，喊道：

“看在穆罕默德份上，我亲爱的法克雷丁，够了吧！让我们这就去你的谷地，享用上天赐给你的果物。”这一动身的暗示使全体行动起来。埃米尔那些可敬的随员们上路时步子略嫌迟缓，但瓦提克早已私下吩咐小侍童们去戳赶单峰驼，笼轿里顿时爆出一阵阵大笑——那些可怜牲口笨拙腾跃的姿势，加上上了年纪的骑手们狼狈不堪的情状，给夫人们添了不小的消遣。

===Original===


They descended, however, unhurt into the valley, by the large steps which the emir had cut in the rock; and already the murmuring of streams and the rustling of leaves began to catch their attention. The cavalcade soon entered a path, which was skirted by flowering shrubs, and extended to a vast wood of palm-trees whose branches overspread a building of hewn stone. This edifice was crowned with nine domes, and adorned with as many portals of bronze, on which was engraven the following inscription:

“This is the asylum of pilgrims, the refuge of travellers, and the depository of secrets for all parts of the world.”

===Chinese===


不过，众人安然无恙地循着埃米尔命人在岩石上凿出的宽阔台阶下到谷中；溪水的潺潺、树叶的沙沙已开始引他们注目。队伍很快踏上一条小径，两旁花灌木夹道，径直延伸到一片辽阔的棕榈林；林梢覆罩着一座琢石砌成的大厦。这座建筑顶着九座穹顶，配着同样数目的青铜大门，门上镌着如下铭文：

“此处是香客的庇护所、旅人的避难地，也是天下各地秘密的收存处。”

===Original===


Nine pages beautiful as the day, and clothed in robes of Egyptian linen, very long and very modest, were standing at each door. They received the whole retinue with an easy and inviting air. Four of the most amiable placed the Caliph on a magnificent taktrevan; four others, somewhat less graceful, took charge of Bababalouk, who capered for joy at the snug little cabin that fell to his share; the pages that remained, waited on the rest of the train.

When everything masculine was gone out of sight, the gate of a large enclosure on the right turned on its harmonious hinges, and a young female of a slender form came forth. Her light brown hair floated in the hazy breeze of the twilight. A troop of young maidens, like the Pleiades, attended her on tiptoe. They hastened to the pavilions that contained the sultanas; and the young lady gracefully bending said to them:

===Chinese===


九名侍童俊美如白昼，身着埃及亚麻长袍，袍子又长又端严，分立在每扇门前。他们以从容而殷勤的风姿接待了全体随行人等。其中四位最俊美的把哈里发扶上一乘华丽的驮轿（taktrevan）；另外四位稍欠俊雅的，便去照管巴巴巴鲁克——他分到那么一间舒舒服服的小舱，乐得手舞足蹈；余下的侍童则伺候队伍里的其余人。

待男性人等尽数走出了视线，右手边一座大围场的院门在和谐的铰链声里转开，一位身形窈窕的少女走了出来。她淡褐色的长发在薄暮朦胧的微风里飘拂。一队少女宛如昴星团（Pleiades）的姊妹，踮着脚追随在她身后。她们匆匆来到安置妃嫔的营帐前；那少女优雅地欠身，对她们说道：

===Original===


“Charming princesses, everything is ready; we have prepared beds for your repose, and strewed your apartments with jasmine; no insects will keep off slumber from visiting your eyelids; we will dispel them with a thousand plumes. Come then, amiable ladies! refresh your delicate feet and your ivory limbs in baths of rose water, and by the light of perfumed lamps your servants will amuse you with tales.”

The sultanas accepted with pleasure these obliging offers, and followed the young lady to the emir’s harem, where we must for a moment leave them and return to the Caliph.

===Chinese===


“迷人的公主们，一切都已备好；我们为你们备下了安寝的床榻，你们的居室里撒满了茉莉花；再没有虫豸能拦住睡意降临你们的眼睑；我们会用一千支羽扇把它们驱散。来吧，可爱的夫人们！在玫瑰水浴中滋润你们娇嫩的双足和象牙般的肢体；就着香灯的光亮，你们的仆婢将以故事为你们解闷。”

妃嫔们欣然领受了这番殷勤，跟着少女往埃米尔的后宫去了——我们且把她们撇下片刻，回头再说哈里发。

===Original===


Vathek found himself beneath a vast dome illuminated by a thousand lamps of rock crystal, as many vases of the same material filled with excellent sherbet sparkled on a large table, where a profusion of viands were spread. Amongst others were sweetbreads stewed in milk of almonds, saffron soups, and lamb à la crême, of all of which the Caliph was amazingly fond. He took of each as much as he was able; testified his sense of the emir’s friendship by the gaiety of his heart; and made the dwarfs dance against their will; for these little devotees durst not refuse the commander of the faithful. At last he spread himself on the sofa and slept sounder than he had ever before.

===Chinese===


瓦提克置身于一座巨穹之下，千盏水晶岩灯把它照得通明；同样质地的千百只瓶盏盛着上好的果子露（sherbet），在一张大案上熠熠生辉，案上珍馐罗列。其中有杏仁乳煨的胸腺甜肉、藏红花浓汤、奶油汁烹的羊肉（à la crême）——这几样哈里发都嗜之如命。他每样都放量吃了个够；以心中的欢畅报答埃米尔的情谊；还强拉着两个侏儒跳舞——这两个小信徒不敢违抗信士们的长官。末了，他摊在长榻上，睡得比平生任何时候都沉。

===Original===


Beneath this dome a general silence prevailed, for there was nothing to disturb it but the jaws of Bababalouk, who had untrussed himself to eat with greater advantage, being anxious to make amends for his fast in the mountains. As his spirits were too high to admit of his sleeping, and not loving to be idle, he proposed with himself to visit the harem, and repair to his charge of the ladies, to examine if they had been properly lubricated with the balm of Mecca, if their eyebrows and tresses were in order, and in a word, to perform all the little offices they might need. He sought for a long time together, but without being able to find out the door. He durst not speak aloud for fear of disturbing the Caliph, and not a soul was stirring in the precincts of the palace. He almost despaired of effecting his purpose, when a low whispering just reached his ear: it came from the dwarfs, who were returned to their old occupation, and for the nine hundred and ninety-ninth time in their lives were reading over the Koran. They very politely invited Bababalouk to be of their party, but his head was full of other concerns. The dwarfs, though scandalized at his dissolute morals, directed him to the apartments he wanted to find. His way thither lay through a hundred dark corridors, along which he groped as he went, and at last began to catch, from the extremity of a passage, the charming gossiping of women, which not a little delighted his heart.

===Chinese===


穹顶之下一片岑寂，唯有巴巴巴鲁克的牙关在搅扰它——他解开了腰带，好吃得更加痛快，急着把山里亏欠的斋戒补回来。他兴头太高，睡意全无，又不肯闲着，便暗自打算去逛后宫，回到他看管夫人们的差事上，查一查她们可曾用麦加香膏（balm of Mecca）好好润过肤，眉毛和发辫是否整齐，总而言之，把她们可能需要的种种小差事统统料理一番。他寻了老半天，却怎么也找不着门。他不敢出声，惟恐搅了哈里发；宫殿四下里又不见一个人影走动。他几乎要绝望了，忽然一阵低低的絮语恰好送进他耳朵：是那两个侏儒，他们已回到老营生上，正把《古兰经》平生第九百九十九遍地重读。他们客客气气地邀巴巴巴鲁克入伙，无奈他满脑子都是别的心事。两个侏儒虽为他放浪的行止骇然，还是给他指了路。通往那处的路上要穿过一百条昏暗的回廊，他一路摸索前行，终于从一条过道的尽头，捕捉到女子们叽叽喳喳的悦耳闲谈，这可叫他心花怒放。

===Original===


“Ah, ah! what not yet asleep?” cried he, and taking long strides as he spoke, “did you not suspect me of abjuring my charge? I stayed but to finish what my master had left.”

Two of the black eunuchs on hearing a voice so loud detached a party in haste, sabre in hand, to discover the cause, but presently was repeated on all sides:

“ ’Tis only Bababalouk, no one but Bababalouk!”

===Chinese===


“啊哈！还没睡哪？”他嚷道，说话间迈开大步，“你们莫不是疑心我撂了差事？我只是留下，把我主人剩下的东西收拾完罢了。”

两名黑太监听得这等大嗓门，急忙提刀点起一队人，去查明缘故，但四下里随即响起一片：

“只是巴巴巴鲁克，除了巴巴巴鲁克没别人！”

===Original===


This circumspect guardian having gone up to a thin veil of carnation-colour silk that hung before the doorway, distinguished by means of a softened splendour that shone through it, an oval bath of dark porphyry surrounded by curtains festooned in large folds. Through the apertures between them, as they were not drawn close, groups of young slaves were visible, amongst whom Bababalouk perceived his pupils indulgingly expanding their arms, as if to embrace the perfumed water, and refresh themselves after their fatigues. The looks of tender languor, their confidential whispers, and the enchanting smiles with which they were imparted, the exquisite fragrance of the roses, all combined to inspire a voluptuousness which even Bababalouk himself was scarce able to withstand.

===Chinese===


这位谨小慎微的看守走到门首悬着的一层粉红薄纱跟前，凭那透进来的柔和光华，辨出一座黑斑岩砌成的椭圆形浴池，四周围着大幅垂褶的帏幔。帏幔没有拉严，缝隙间望得见一群群年轻的奴婢；巴巴巴鲁克瞥见他管教的那些学生正忘情地舒展双臂，仿佛要拥抱那香馥馥的泉水，舒解一路的劳乏。她们眼波里慵懒的柔情、咬耳朵的私语、诉说时那迷人的微笑，再加上玫瑰沁人的异香，汇成一股叫人心荡神驰的魔力，连巴巴巴鲁克自己也险些把持不住。

===Original===


He summoned up, however, his usual solemnity, and in the peremptory tone of authority commanded the ladies instantly to leave the bath. Whilst he was issuing these mandates, the young Nouronihar, daughter of the emir, who was sprightly as an antelope, and full of wanton gaiety, beckoned one of her slaves to let down the great swing, which was suspended to the ceiling by cords of silk, and whilst this was doing winked to her companions in the bath, who chagrined to be forced from so soothing a state of indolence, began to twist it round Bababalouk, and tease him with a thousand vagaries.

===Chinese===


不过，他还是强打起平日的威严，用不容分说的口气喝令夫人们即刻出浴。他正发布这些号令，埃米尔的千金、年轻的努罗尼哈（Nouronihar）——她轻捷得像一头羚羊，浑身是俏皮的欢劲儿——招手唤一名奴婢放下那架大秋千；秋千是用丝绳吊在天花板上的。趁这工夫，她朝浴池里的同伴们递眼色；她们正懊恼被从那么受用的慵懒里赶出来，便动手把秋千在巴巴巴鲁克身上绕来绕去，用千百种淘气的花招捉弄他。

===Original===


When Nouronihar perceived that he was exhausted with fatigue, she accosted him with an arch air of respectful concern, and said:

“My lord, it is not by any means decent that the chief eunuch of the Caliph our sovereign should thus continue standing, deign but to recline your graceful person upon this sofa, which will burst with vexation if it have not the honour to receive you.”

Caught by these flattering accents, Bababalouk gallantly replied:

“Delight of the apple of my eye! I accept the invitation of thy honied lips, and to say truth, my senses are dazzled with the radiance that beams from thy charms.”

===Chinese===


努罗尼哈见他累得筋疲力尽，便换上一副又敬又谑的神气搭话道：

“大人，咱们君王哈里发的宦官之首，这般一直站着，实在不成体统；请您屈尊把俊雅的贵体往这张长榻上一倚吧；它若没有福气承接您，准要恼恨得炸裂呢。”

巴巴巴鲁克被这蜜语甜言一恭维，殷勤地答道：

“我眼珠里的欢喜人儿！我领受你蜜嘴唇的邀请了；说实在的，你妩媚的光彩已照得我神迷目眩。”

===Original===


“Repose, then, at your ease,” replied the beauty, and placed him on the pretended sofa, which, quicker than lightning, gave way all at once. The rest of the women having aptly conceived her design, sprang naked from the bath and plied the swing with such unmerciful jerks, that it swept through the whole compass of a very lofty dome, and took from the poor victim all power of respiration. Sometimes his feet rased the surface of the water, and at others the skylight almost flattened his nose. In vain did he pierce the air with the cries of a voice that resembled the ringing of a cracked basin, for their peals of laughter were still more predominant.

===Chinese===


“那么，就请您舒舒服服歇着吧。”美人答道，把他安在那张冒牌的长榻上。那玩意儿比闪电还快，轰隆一下整个塌了下去。其余女子立刻会了她的意，赤条条从浴池里跳将出来，没轻没重地把秋千猛荡猛掼，一路扫过整座高穹的每个角落，荡得那可怜的受难者气也透不出来。他的脚时而擦过水面，时而高处的天窗几乎把他的鼻子压扁。他扯着一副破铜盆似的嗓子在空气里嘶喊，也是枉然——她们一阵阵的哄笑始终压倒一切。

===Original===


Nouronihar in the inebriety of youthful spirits being used only to eunuchs of ordinary harems, and having never seen anything so royal and disgusting, was far more diverted than all of the rest. She began to parody some Persian verses, and sung with an accent most demurely piquant:

> “O gentle white dove as thou soar’st through the air,
 

 Vouchsafe one kind glance on the mate of thy love:
 

 Melodious Philomel I am thy rose;
 

 Warble some couplet to ravish my heart!”

===Chinese===


努罗尼哈正沉在青春的醺然里——她平日见惯的不过是寻常后宫的太监，何曾见过这般既有帝王气派又这般不堪的造物——于是比谁都笑得欢。她开始戏仿几句波斯诗，用一种端庄里带尖俏的腔调唱道：

> “温柔的白鸽啊，当你在空中翱翔，
 

 请眷然一顾你钟爱的侣伴：
 

 歌喉婉转的菲洛墨拉（Philomel），我就是你的玫瑰；
 

 请唱上一联，来迷醉我的心！”

===Original===


The sultanas and their slaves, stimulated by these pleasantries, persevered at the swing with such unremitted assiduity, that at length the cord which had secured it snapped suddenly asunder, and Bababalouk fell floundering like a turtle to the bottom of the bath. This accident occasioned a universal shout. Twelve little doors till now unobserved flew open at once, and the ladies in an instant made their escape, after throwing all the towels on his head, and putting out the lights that remained.

===Chinese===


妃嫔和奴婢们被这些趣话一激，越发不知疲倦地猛荡那架秋千；终于，系着它的绳索啪的一声骤然崩断，巴巴巴鲁克像一只乌龟似的扑腾着栽到浴池底。这场事故引起一片齐声大叫。十二扇先前无人留意的小门同时敞开，妃嫔们转眼间逃得精光，临走把所有巾帕都罩在他头上，又把剩下的灯统统扑灭。

===Original===


The deplorable animal, in water to the chin, overwhelmed with darkness, and unable to extricate himself from the warp that embarrassed him, was still doomed to hear for his further consolation, the fresh bursts of merriment his disaster occasioned. He bustled but in vain to get from the bath, for the margin was become so slippery with the oil spilt in breaking the lamps, that at every effort he slid back with a plunge, which resounded aloud through the hollow of the dome. These cursed peals of laughter at every relapse were redoubled, and he, who thought the place infested rather by devils than women, resolved to cease groping, and abide in the bath, where he amused himself with soliloquies interspersed with imprecations, of which his malicious neighbours, reclining on down, suffered not an accent to escape. In this delectable plight the morning surprised him. The Caliph, wondering at his absence, had caused him to be everywhere sought for. At last he was drawn forth almost smothered from the whisp of linen, and wet even to the marrow. Limping, and chattering his teeth, he appeared before his master, who inquired what was the matter, and how he came soused in so strange a pickle.

===Chinese===


这头可悲的畜生下巴浸在水里，头顶一片漆黑，身子又被缠人的绳索搅得脱不开，偏偏还躲不过耳根——他那场灾祸引来的阵阵新的欢笑，算是给他的额外抚慰。他从池里扑腾着往外爬，只是徒劳：打碎灯时泼洒的油把池沿弄得滑溜溜的，他每使劲爬一次，便扑通一声滑回水里，声响在穹顶的空腔里嗡嗡回荡。他每滑落一回，那些可咒的哄笑便加一回倍。他寻思这地方作祟的不是女人而是魔鬼，索性不再摸索，索性泡在浴池里安顿下来，自言自语地解闷，话里夹着咒骂；他那些促狭的女邻正躺在羽绒褥上，一个音也不肯放过。就在这副美妙的处境里，清晨叫他了个措手不及。哈里发久候他不至，早已传令四处寻找。末了，人们才把他从那一团缠裹的亚麻布里拖出来，几乎闷死，湿得透进骨髓。他一瘸一拐、牙齿打战地站到主人面前；主人问他出了什么事，怎么会泡成这般古怪的一副落汤鸡模样。

===Original===


“And why did you enter this cursed lodge?” answered Bababalouk, gruffly. “Ought a monarch like you to visit with his harem the abode of a grey bearded emir who knows nothing of life? And with what gracious damsels does the place too abound! Fancy to yourself how they have soaked me like a burnt crust, and made me dance like a jack-pudding the livelong night through on their damnable swing. What an excellent lesson for your sultanas to follow, into whom I have instilled such reserve and decorum!”

Vathek, comprehending not a syllable of all this invective, obliged him to relate minutely the transaction; but instead of sympathising with the miserable sufferer, he laughed immoderately at the device of the swing, and the figure of Bababalouk mounting upon it. The stung eunuch could scarcely preserve the semblance of respect.

“Aye, laugh my lord! laugh,” said he, “but I wish this Nouronihar would play some trick on you; she is too wicked to spare even majesty itself.”

===Chinese===


“您干嘛要进这该咒的宅子？”巴巴巴鲁克悻悻地答道，“像您这样一位君王，也配带着妃嫔来登一个白胡子埃米尔的家门？他对生活一窍不通！这地方又盛产些何等‘和蔼可亲’的小娘子！您自己想想吧，她们把我泡得像一块烤糊的面包皮，又叫我在她们那架要命的秋千上，像街头的滑稽丑角一样蹦跳了整整一夜。这可给您的妃嫔们立了个多好的榜样——我往她们心里灌输了那么多的矜持和体统！”

瓦提克对这篇痛骂一个字也没听懂，硬要他把事情原原本本细讲了一遍；可他非但不怜悯这位倒霉的受难者，反而一想到那秋千的机关、巴巴巴鲁克骑上去的那副尊容，便狂笑不止。这位被刺痛的太监险些保不住表面上的那点恭敬。

“唉，笑吧，主公！笑吧，”他说，“我倒巴不得这努罗尼哈也捉弄您一回；她坏得很，连九五之尊也是不肯放过的。”

===Original===


These words made for the present but a slight impression on the Caliph, but they not long after recurred to his mind.

This conversation was cut short by Fakreddin, who came to request that Vathek would join in the prayers and ablutions to be solemnized on a spacious meadow, watered by innumerable streams. The Caliph found the waters refreshing, but the prayers abominably irksome. He diverted himself however with the multitude of Calenders,19 Santons,20 and Dervises21 who were continually coming and going, but especially with the Brahmins,22 Fakirs,23 and other enthusiasts, who had travelled from the heart of India, and halted on their way with the emir. These latter had each of them some mummery peculiar to himself. One dragged a huge chain where ever he went, another an orangutan, whilst a third was furnished with scourges, and all performed to a charm. Some clambered up trees, holding one foot in the air; others poised themselves over a fire, and without mercy fillipped their noses. There were some amongst them that cherished vermin, which were not ungrateful in requiting their caresses. These rambling fanatics revolted the hearts of the Dervises, the Calenders, and Santons; however the vehemence of their aversion soon subsided under the hope that the presence of the Caliph would cure their folly, and convert them to the Mussulman faith. But alas! how great was their disappointment! for Vathek, instead of preaching to them, treated them as buffoons; bade them present his compliments to Visnow and Ixhora, and discovered a predilection for a squat old man from the Isle of Serendib, who was more ridiculous than any of the rest.

===Chinese===


这几句话，当时在哈里发心里只留下浅浅一道印子，可没过多久便又回到了他的心头。

这场谈话被法克雷丁打断——他来请瓦提克参加礼拜和净礼；仪式将在一片受无数溪流滋润的开阔草地上隆重举行。哈里发觉得溪水清爽宜人，那些祷文却烦闷得要命。不过，络绎不绝来来往往的大群卡连德尔游方僧（Calenders）19、苦修圣僧（Santons）20和托钵僧（Dervises）21倒叫他有得消遣；尤其是那些婆罗门（Brahmins）22、苦行僧（Fakirs）23和其他狂信之徒——他们从印度腹地一路行来，中途在埃米尔这里歇脚。这后一班人，个个都有一手自己独有的把戏：一个走到哪儿都拖着一条巨链，另一个牵着一头猩猩，第三个则随身带着鞭子，人人表演得煞是好看。有的攀上树去，一只脚悬在半空；有的悬身在火上，毫不留情地弹自己的鼻子。其中还有豢养虱蚤的，那些小虫倒也不忘恩负义，以爱抚回敬爱抚。这些游方的狂信徒叫托钵僧、卡连德尔僧和圣僧们心里作呕；不过他们厌恶的激烈劲儿很快就平息了，一心指望哈里发的驾临能治好这群痴人的愚妄，把他们感化归顺穆斯林正道。可是天哪！他们的失望何其之大！原来瓦提克非但不向他们布道，反把他们当丑角取乐；还叫他们代他向毗湿奴（Visnow）和伊湿伐罗（Ixhora）问好，并对一个来自细轮迪卜岛（Isle of Serendib）的矮墩墩老头另眼相看——此人比其余哪一个都更滑稽。

===Original===


“Come,” said he, “for the love of your gods, bestow a few slaps on your chops to amuse me.”

The old fellow, offended at such an address, began loudly to weep; but as he betrayed a villainous drivelling in his tears, the Caliph turned his back and listened to Bababalouk, who whispered, whilst he held the umbrella over him:

===Chinese===


“来吧，”他说，“看在你们那些神明的份上，赏自己嘴巴几个耳刮子，给我取个乐。”

老头儿听了这话大为冒犯，放声大哭起来；可他这一哭，涕泪里透出一副下作的淌口水相，哈里发便转过身去，听巴巴巴鲁克低声说话——后者正替他撑着伞：

===Original===


“Your majesty should be cautious of this odd assembly, which hath been collected I know not for what. Is it necessary to exhibit such spectacles to a mighty potentate, with interludes of talapoins more mangy than dogs? Were I you, I would command a fire to be kindled, and at once purge the earth of the emir, his harem, and all his menagery.”

“Tush, dolt,” answered Vathek, “and know that all this infinitely charms me. Nor shall I leave the meadow till I have visited every hive of these pious mendicants.”

===Chinese===


“陛下对这伙七拼八凑的怪聚会可得留神，谁知道他们聚在这儿是要干什么。给一位堂堂大国之君看这些名堂，中间还穿插着比癞皮狗还脏的塔拉波因僧（talapoins），有这个必要吗？我要是您，就下令点一把火，一口气把埃米尔、他的后宫连同他那一园子活物从地面上清除干净。”

“呸，蠢货，”瓦提克答道，“你可知这一切正合我意，妙不可言。不把这群虔诚叫花子的每一个蜂窝都逛上一遍，我决不离开这片草地。”

===Original===


Where ever the Caliph directed his course, objects of pity were sure to swarm round him: the blind, the purblind, smarts without noses, damsels without ears, each to extol the munificence of Fakreddin, who, as well as his attendant greybeards, dealt about gratis plasters and cataplasms to all that applied. At noon a superb corps of cripples made its appearance; and soon after advanced by platoons on the plain the completest association of invalids that had ever been embodied till then. The blind went groping with the blind; the lame limped on together; and the maimed made gestures to each other with the only arm that remained. The sides of a considerable waterfall were crowded by the deaf, amongst whom were some from Pegu, with ears uncommonly handsome and large, but were still less able to hear than the rest. Nor were there wanting others in abundance with hump backs, wenny necks, and even horns of an exquisite polish.

===Chinese===


哈里发无论往哪里走，告可怜的人们的队伍必定蜂拥而至：瞎眼的、半瞎的、没了鼻子的俏郎君、缺了耳朵的少女，人人都要颂扬法克雷丁的慷慨——他和随行的白胡子老人们向来免费施发膏药和糊药，来者不拒。正午时分，一支蔚为壮观的残废大军露了面；不久，一队接一队开上平原的，是有史以来编制最齐全的伤病协会。瞎子同瞎子相互摸索着前行；瘸子与瘸子一齐蹒跚；缺了胳膊的用仅存的那条胳膊彼此打着手势。一道颇为可观的瀑布两旁挤满了聋子，其中有几位勃固（Pegu）来的，耳朵生得异常俊美硕大，却比其余的人更听不见。此外也绝不缺少驼背的、脖子上长瘤的，甚至还有头上生角、磨得油光锃亮的。

===Original===


The emir, to aggrandize the solemnity of the festival in honour of his illustrious visitant, ordered the turf to be spread on all sides with skins and table cloths, upon which were served up for the good mussulmans pilaus of every hue, with other orthodox dishes, and by the express order of Vathek, who was shamefully tolerant, small plates of abominations for regaling the rest. This prince on seeing so many mouths put in motion began to think it time for employing his own. In spite, therefore, of every remonstrance from the chief of his eunuchs, he resolved to have a dinner dressed on the spot. The complaisant emir immediately gave orders for a table to be placed in the shade of the willows. The first service consisted of fish, which they drew from a river flowing over sands of gold, at the foot of a lofty hill: these were broiled as fast as taken, and served up with a sauce of vinegar and small herbs that grew on Mount Sinai; for everything with the emir was excellent and pious.

===Chinese===


埃米尔为了给他显贵的贵宾增光、把节庆办得更隆重，吩咐四面八方的草地上铺满兽皮和桌布，为善良的穆斯林们端上各色抓饭（pilaus）和其他正统菜肴；又遵照瓦提克的明确谕令——他宽容得未免不成体统——另备了一碟碟违禁的荤腥，款待其余的人。这位殿下眼见这么多张嘴巴都动了起来，便觉得也该让自己那张嘴上工了。于是，不顾宦官之首的百般谏阻，他决意就地开一席午饭。殷勤的埃米尔立即吩咐把餐桌安放到柳荫之下。头一道菜是鱼，从一条流过金沙、绕着高山山脚的河里现捞：随捞随烤，浇上用醋和西奈山（Mount Sinai）上所长的小香草调成的汁子上桌；因为在埃米尔这里，样样东西都既精美又虔诚。

===Original===


The dessert was not quite set on when the sound of lutes from the hill was repeated by the echoes of the neighbouring mountains. The Caliph with an emotion of pleasure and surprise, had no sooner raised up his head than a handful of jasmine dropped on his face. An abundance of tittering succeeded this frolic, and instantly appeared through the bushes the elegant forms of several young females, skipping and bounding like roes. The fragrance diffused from their hair struck the sense of Vathek, who in an ecstasy, suspending his repast, said to Bababalouk:

“Are the Peries24 come down from their spheres? Note her in particular whose form is so perfect, venturously running on the brink of the precipice, and turning back her head as regardless of nothing but the graceful flow of her robe. With what captivating impatience doth she contend with the bushes for her veil? Could it be she who threw the jasmine at me?”

===Chinese===


甜食还没摆齐，山上传来的琵琶声已被邻近群山的回声叠叠送来。哈里发又惊又喜，才一抬头，一把茉莉花正落在他脸上。这场顽皮之后紧跟着一片咯咯的窃笑，灌木丛间顿时现出几位少女窈窕的身影，像小鹿般又跳又蹦。她们发间散出的芬芳直沁瓦提克的感官；他心醉神迷，搁下了饭食，对巴巴巴鲁克说：

“是佩丽仙女（Peries）24 从天界降下来了么？尤其留心看那一位，身段何等完美，大胆地沿着悬崖边上奔跑，还回过头来，仿佛除了长裙飘拂的姿致之外，什么都不放在心上。她同灌木丛争抢面纱时那份撩人的急劲儿，你看够不够受？掷茉莉花给我的，会不会就是她？”

===Original===


“Aye, she it was; and you too would she throw from the top of the rock,” answered Bababalouk, “for that is my good friend Nouronihar, who so kindly lent me her swing. My dear lord and master,” added he, twisting a twig that hung by the rind from a willow, “let me correct her for her want of respect: the emir will have no reason to complain, since (bating what I owe to his piety) he is much to be censured for keeping a troop of girls on the mountains, whose sharp air gives their blood too brisk a circulation.”

“Peace, blasphemer!” said the Caliph: “speak not thus of her who over her mountains leads my heart a willing captive. Contrive, rather, that my eyes may be fixed upon hers﻿—that I may respire her sweet breath, as she bounds panting along these delightful wilds!”

===Chinese===


“没错，就是她；连您，她也会从岩石顶上摔下去的，”巴巴巴鲁克答道，“因为那正是我的好朋友努罗尼哈，就是那么好心把秋千借给我的那一位。我亲爱的主公和主子，”他拧着一根还连着树皮垂在柳树上的细枝，又添了一句，“让我去管教管教她的无礼吧：埃米尔也没什么可抱怨的，因为（撇开我欠他的敬虔不谈）他把一大群姑娘养在山上，本就该大受责备——山间凛冽的空气，把她们的血液催得太旺了。”

“住口，你这渎神的东西！”哈里发说，“不许这样议论她——就是她，引着我的心甘愿做俘虏，翻过她的群山。你还是设法让我的双眼同她的双眼锁在一处吧——好让我吸一吸她香甜的气息，她正喘息着、蹦跳着跑过这些可爱的荒野呢！”

===Original===


On saying these words, Vathek extended his arms towards the hill, and directing his eyes with an anxiety unknown to him before, endeavoured to keep within view the object that enthralled his soul; but her course was as difficult to follow as the flight of one of those beautiful blue butterflies of Cachmere, which are at once so volatile and rare.
