I will spring mines of serpents and torpedoes from beneath them, and we shall soon see the stand they will make against such an explosion!”
Having thus spoken, Carathis hasted to her son, who was tranquilly banqueting with Nouronihar in his superb carnation-coloured tent.

===Chinese===


卡拉希丝不惯失掉到口的猎物，把黑女仆们千咒万骂：不去抓那孩子，倒有闲情掐死两个侏儒——从她们身上能捞到什么好处？她一路嘟囔着回到谷里，见儿子还没从努罗尼哈怀里起身，便把一腔恶气尽数撒在这两人身上。不过，一想到明日便可动身去伊斯特卡尔，借吉奥尔的美言引荐，同埃布利斯本人攀上交情，她的恼恨终于得了宽解：怎奈天意另有所安排。

傍晚，卡拉希丝正同迪拉拉闲谈——这女人是她设法安排进队伍里来的，口味又与她相投——巴巴巴鲁克前来禀报，说“萨迈拉那边的天色红得像着了火，只怕有大祸临头”。她立刻搬出星盘和诸般法器，测了行星的高度，一推算，不由得大为懊恼：萨迈拉果然起了大乱子；穆塔瓦基勒（Motavakel）趁着他兄弟素来积聚的民怨，煽动百姓作乱，占了宫城，此刻正围攻那座大塔——莫拉卡纳巴德带着寥寥几个仍旧效忠瓦提克的人，退守在塔中。

“什么！”她嚷道，“这么说，我的塔、我的哑仆、我的黑女奴、我的木乃伊，还有比这一切更要紧的——我熬过多少夜晚的丹房，都要丢了？却连我这个莽撞儿子究竟成不成得了他的大事，也不先让我知道！不成！我岂能做这个冤大头！我这就飞去支援莫拉卡纳巴德。凭我可怕的法术，我要叫乌云把冰雹糊在乱党脸上，把烧红的铁箭射上他们头顶；我要从他们脚底下掘出毒蛇与电鳐的雷穴，一并炸起——且看这般炸裂之下，他们还站不站得住脚！”

说罢，卡拉希丝便赶到儿子那里——他正和努罗尼哈在他那顶华丽的粉红色大帐里安然宴饮。

===Original===


“Glutton that thou art,” cried she, “were it not for me, thou wouldst soon find thyself the commander only of pies. Thy faithful subjects have abjured the faith they swore to thee. Motavakel thy brother now reigns on the hill of pied horses; and had I not some slight resources in the tower, would not be easily persuaded to abdicate. But that time may not be lost, I shall only add four words: strike tent tonight; set forward; and beware how thou loiterest again by the way. Though thou hast forfeited the conditions of the parchment, I am not yet without hope; for it cannot be denied that thou hast violated to admiration the laws of hospitality by seducing the daughter of the emir, after having partaken of his bread and his salt. Such a conduct cannot but be delightful to the Giaour; and if on thy march thou canst signalize thyself by an additional crime, all will still go well, and thou shalt enter the palace of Soliman in triumph. Adieu! Alboufaki and my negresses are waiting.”

===Chinese===


“你这馋痨鬼！”她喊道，“若不是有我，你很快就会发现自己不过是馅饼军的统领。你那些忠心的臣民，已经把向你起的誓统统背弃。你兄弟穆塔瓦基勒如今在杂色马山上登了基；若不是我在塔里还留着几分小小的手段，休想轻易劝他退位。为了不耽误工夫，我只添四个词儿：今夜拔营；即刻前进；路上再敢磨蹭，仔细着。羊皮卷上的条件你虽已误了，我却还不绝望；因为谁也不能否认，你把待客之道的法度亵渎得淋漓尽致——吃了人家的面包和盐，转头便勾引人家埃米尔的女儿。这等行径，吉奥尔见了必定欢喜；行军路上你若能再立一桩新罪的战功，一切依旧顺遂，你将凯旋进入苏莱曼的宫殿。再会！阿尔布法基和我的黑女奴们都等急了。”

===Original===


The Caliph had nothing to offer in reply: he wished his mother a prosperous journey, and eat on till he had finished his supper. At midnight the camp broke up, amidst the flourishing of trumpets and other martial instruments; but loud indeed must have been the sound of the tymbals, to overpower the blubbering of the emir and his long-beards, who by an excessive profusion of tears had so far exhausted the radical moisture, that their eyes shrivelled up in their sockets, and their hairs dropped off by the roots. Nouronihar, to whom such a symphony was painful, did not grieve to get out of hearing. She accompanied the Caliph in the imperial litter, where they amused themselves with imagining the splendour which was soon to surround them. The other women, overcome with dejection, were dolefully rocked in their cages, whilst Dilara consoled herself with anticipating the joy of celebrating the rites of fire on the stately terraces of Istakar.

===Chinese===


哈里发无言可对：祝了母亲一路平安，便接着吃他的，直到用完晚膳。半夜里，营盘在号角和其他军乐的喧阗中拔寨起行；不过那铜鼓之声，也着实得响，才盖得过埃米尔和他那班白胡子老人的嚎啕——他们涕泪滂沱，把身上的根本津液都哭耗干了，眼珠在眼窝里皱缩下去，头发也连根脱落。这样的交响乐叫努罗尼哈听着难受，能走到听不见的地方，她倒也不觉得可惜。她随哈里发同乘御辇，两人一路想象着即将环绕他们的荣华，以此作乐。其余的女人垂头丧气，在各自的笼舆里凄凄惨惨地摇晃；迪拉拉却自宽自解，一心指望不久便能在伊斯特卡尔巍峨的高台上行拜火之礼，先就把那份欢喜受用了个够。

===Original===


In four days they reached the spacious valley of Rocnabad. The season of spring was in all its vigour, and the grotesque branches of the almond trees in full blossom fantastically chequered the clear blue sky. The earth, variegated with hyacinths and jonquils, breathed forth a fragrance which diffused through the soul a divine repose. Myriads of bees, and scarce fewer of Santons had there taken up their abode. On the banks of the stream hives and oratories were alternately ranged, and their neatness and whiteness were set off by the deep green of the cypresses that spired up amongst them. These pious personages amused themselves with cultivating little gardens that abounded with flowers and fruits, especially muskmelons of the best flavour that Persia could boast. Sometimes dispersed over the meadow they entertained themselves with feeding peacocks whiter than snow, and turtles more blue than the sapphire. In this manner were they occupied when the harbingers of the imperial procession began to proclaim:
“Inhabitants of Rocnabad, prostrate yourselves on the brink of your pure waters, and tender your thanksgivings to heaven that vouchsafeth to show you a ray of its glory; for lo! the commander of the faithful draws near.”

===Chinese===


走了四天，他们来到罗克纳巴德宽敞的山谷。时当春光鼎盛，杏树繁花满枝，虬曲的枝干把清朗的蓝天织成奇幻的格子。地上风信子与水仙斑斓相间，吐出的芬芳沁入魂魄，令人顿生神圣的安宁。无数蜜蜂在此安了家，苦修圣僧的数目也几乎不相上下。溪岸上蜂房与小礼拜堂相间排列，那整洁雪白，衬着其间尖耸而起的苍翠柏影，愈见精神。这些虔敬之士以莳弄小园为乐，园中花果繁茂，尤其是波斯最堪夸口的绝品甜瓜。有时他们散在草地上，喂着比雪还白的孔雀、比蓝宝石还蓝的斑鸠，以此自娱。他们正这样消遣着，御驾的前驱官开始高声传谕：

“罗克纳巴德的居民们，俯伏在你们清净的溪水边上，感谢上天垂恩，让你们得见它荣光的一线吧；看哪！信士们的长官驾到了。”

===Original===


The poor Santons, filled with holy energy, having bustled to light up wax torches in their oratories, and expand the Koran on their ebony desks, went forth to meet the Caliph with baskets of honeycomb, dates, and melons. But whilst they were advancing in solemn procession and with measured steps, the horses, camels, and guards wantoned over their tulips and other flowers, and made a terrible havoc amongst them. The Santons could not help casting from one eye a look of pity on the ravages committing around them, whilst the other was fixed upon the Caliph and heaven. Nouronihar, enraptured with the scenery of a place which brought back to her remembrance the pleasing solitudes where her infancy had passed, entreated Vathek to stop, but he, suspecting that each oratory might be deemed by the Giaour a distinct habitation, commanded his pioneers to level them all. The Santons stood motionless with horror at the barbarous mandate, and at last broke out into lamentations, but these were uttered with so ill a grace, that Vathek bade his eunuchs to kick them from his presence. He then descended from the litter with Nouronihar. They sauntered together in the meadow, and amused themselves with culling flowers, and passing a thousand pleasantries on each other. But the bees, who were staunch Mussulmans, thinking it their duty to revenge the insult on their dear masters the Santons, assembled so zealously to do it with effect, that the Caliph and Nouronihar were glad to find their tents prepared to receive them.

===Chinese===


可怜的圣僧们满怀神圣的热忱，忙忙地在小礼拜堂里点起蜡炬，在乌木经架上摊开《古兰经》，便提着一篮篮蜜脾、椰枣和甜瓜，出迎哈里发。然而他们正以庄严的队列、齐整的步武行进时，马匹、骆驼和卫兵已在他们的郁金香和别的花木间肆意践踏，糟蹋得不成样子。圣僧们不由得一只眼睛流露出对四下浩劫的怜惜，另一只眼睛却仍仰望着哈里发和上天。这地方的风光令努罗尼哈心醉神迷——它让她忆起幼年度过的那片惬意的幽境——便恳求瓦提克驻驾；他却疑心每一座小礼拜堂都会被吉奥尔当作一处单独的居所，便吩咐工兵把它们尽数铲平。圣僧们听得这道蛮横的敕令，吓得呆立当场，终于放声哀号起来；只是那哀号发得实在不成体统，瓦提克便叫宦官们把他们踢出跟前。随后他携努罗尼哈下了御辇。两人在草地上闲步，采采花，千百句地互相打趣。怎奈蜜蜂们都是坚贞的穆斯林，认定替亲爱的主人圣僧们报仇雪耻乃是本分，集合得那样踊跃，干得那样见效，害得哈里发和努罗尼哈庆幸营帐早已支好，正好把他们迎了进去。

===Original===


Bababalouk, who in capacity of purveyor, had acquitted himself with applause, as to peacocks and turtles, lost no time in consigning some dozens to the spit, and as many more to be fricasseed. Whilst they were feasting, laughing, carousing, and blaspheming at pleasure on the banquet so liberally furnished, the Moullahs, the Sheiks, the Cadis, and Imans of Shiraz (who seemed not to have met the Santons) arrived, leading by bridles of ribbon, inscribed from the Koran, a train of asses which were loaded with the choicest fruits the country could boast. Having presented their offerings to the Caliph, they petitioned him to honour their city and mosques with his presence.
“Fancy not,” said Vathek, “that you can detain me. Your presents I condescend to accept, but beg you will let me be quiet, for I am not over fond of resisting temptation. Retire then. Yet, as it is not decent for personages so reverend to return on foot, and as you have not the appearance of expert riders, my eunuchs shall tie you on your asses with the precaution that your backs be not turned towards me, for they understand etiquette.”

===Chinese===


巴巴巴鲁克身为采买总管，在孔雀与斑鸠这宗差事上办得人人称许，当下毫不耽搁：几十只上了烤叉，又有几十只送去炖了。正当众人对着这席慷慨备办的酒饭恣情大嚼、狂笑、豪饮、渎神的时候，设拉子的毛拉、谢赫、卡迪和伊玛目们到了（他们似乎没有遇上圣僧那路人），手里牵着缎带缰绳——带子上都织着《古兰经》的经文——牵来一列驮驴，满驮着当地最堪夸耀的时鲜果品。他们向哈里发献了礼物，恳请他驾临他们的城池和清真寺，以光宠之。

“别妄想，”瓦提克说，“你们留得住我。礼物嘛，朕屈尊收下；只是求你们容我清静，因为抗拒诱惑这件事，朕向来不甚在行。退下吧。不过，像诸位这般年尊德劭的人物，步行回去未免不雅；看你们又不像惯于骑术的，朕的宦官们会把你们捆在驴背上——自会留意别叫你们的背朝着朕，他们是懂得礼数的。”

===Original===


In this deputation were some high-stomached Sheiks, who, taking Vathek for a fool, scrupled not to speak their opinion. These Bababalouk girded with double cords; and having well disciplined their asses with nettles behind, they all started with a preternatural alertness, plunging, kicking, and running foul of each other in the most ludicrous manner imaginable.
Nouronihar and the Caliph mutually contended who should most enjoy so degrading a sight. They burst out in volleys of laughter to see the old men and their asses fall into the stream. The leg of one was fractured, the shoulder of another dislocated, the teeth of a third dashed out, and the rest suffered still worse.
Two days more, undisturbed by fresh embassies, having been devoted to the pleasures of Rocnabad, the expedition proceeded, leaving Shiraz on the right, and verging towards a large plain, from whence were discernible on the edge of the horizon the dark summits of the mountains of Istakar.
At this prospect the Caliph and Nouronihar were unable to repress their transports. They bounded from their litter to the ground, and broke forth into such wild exclamations as amazed all within hearing. Interrogating each other, they shouted,
“Are we not approaching the radiant palace of light, or gardens more delightful than those of Sheddad?”
Infatuated mortals! they thus indulged delusive conjecture, unable to fathom the decrees of the Most High!

===Chinese===


这使团里有几位心气高傲的谢赫，把瓦提克当个傻子，毫不忌讳地说出了心里的见解。巴巴巴鲁克给这几位加捆了双道绳索；又用荨麻把他们坐骑的屁股好好管教了一番，驴子们便都拿出超乎天然的敏捷，一齐蹿了出去——又尥蹶子，又乱踢，以想象得到的最为滑稽的模样彼此相撞。

这般有失体统的景象，努罗尼哈和哈里发竟相争着谁看得更受用。见老头儿们连同他们的驴子栽进溪里，两人迸出一阵接一阵的大笑：一个跌断了腿，一个脱了肩胛，一个磕掉了牙齿，其余的遭际更糟。

又过了两天——没有新的使团来搅扰——全用来享受罗克纳巴德的乐趣，队伍方才开拔，把设拉子撇在右边，斜插向一片大平原；从那里望得见地平线边缘伊斯特卡尔群山黛色的峰顶。

一见这景象，哈里发和努罗尼哈便按捺不住心头的狂喜。他们从御辇一跃而下，迸出那般狂野的欢呼，叫听见的人个个吃惊。两人互相追问着，高喊道：

“我们这不是正走向那座光明灿烂的宫殿，或是走向比谢达德（Sheddad）的园子更怡人的花园么？”

痴愚的凡人啊！他们就这样沉溺于虚妄的臆想，参不透至高者的意旨！

===Original===


The good Genii who had not totally relinquished the superintendence of Vathek, repairing to Muhammad in the seventh heaven, said:
“Merciful Prophet! stretch forth thy propitious arms towards thy vicegerent, who is ready to fall irretrievably into the snare which his enemies the Dives have prepared to destroy him. The Giaour is awaiting his arrival in the abominable palace of fire, where if he once set his foot his perdition will be inevitable.”
Muhammad answered with an air of indignation:
“He hath too well deserved to be resigned to himself; but I permit you to try if one effort more will be effectual to divert him from pursuing his ruin.”

===Chinese===


那些尚未完全撒手不管瓦提克的善良精灵，去到第七重天上谒见穆罕默德，说道：

“慈悲的先知啊！向你那位代理人伸出你眷顾的手臂吧——他眼看就要无可挽回地堕入他的仇敌迪夫们为毁灭他而设的圈套。吉奥尔正在那座可憎的火宫里等他到来；他只要一踏进去，沉沦便在所难免。”

穆罕默德面带愠色，答道：

“他实在咎由自取，合该任他自便；不过我准你们再去一试，看最后这一番努力，能否还把他从毁灭的路上拉回来。”

===Original===


One of these beneficent Genii, assuming without delay the exterior of a shepherd, more renowned for his piety than all the Dervises and Santons of the region, took his station near a flock of white sheep on the slope of a hill, and began to pour forth from his flute such airs of pathetic melody, as subdued the very soul; and awakening remorse, drove far from it every frivolous fancy. At these energetic sounds, the sun hid himself beneath a gloomy cloud; and the waters of two little lakes, that were naturally clearer than crystal, became a colour like blood. The whole of this superb assembly was involuntarily drawn towards the declivity of the hill. With downcast eyes, they all stood abashed; each upbraiding himself with the evil he had done. The heart of Dilara palpitated; and the chief of the eunuchs, with a sigh of contrition, implored pardon of the women, whom, for his own satisfaction, he had so often tormented.
Vathek and Nouronihar turned pale in their litter; and, regarding each other with haggard looks, reproached themselves﻿—the one with a thousand of the blackest crimes, a thousand projects of impious ambition; the other, with the desolation of her family, and the perdition of the amiable Gulchenrouz. Nouronihar persuaded herself that she heard in the fatal music the groans of her dying father; and Vathek, the sobs of the fifty children he had sacrificed to the Giaour. Amidst these complicated pangs of anguish, they perceived themselves impelled towards the shepherd, whose countenance was so commanding, that Vathek, for the first time, felt overawed; whilst Nouronihar concealed her face with her hands. The music paused, and the Genius, addressing the Caliph, said:

===Chinese===


这些慈惠的精灵中有一位，当即化身为一个牧羊人——其虔敬之名，压过这一带所有的托钵僧和圣僧——在一面山坡上挨着一群白羊站定，便从笛中吹出如许哀婉动人的曲调，直沁得人魂魄都软了下去，唤醒了悔恨，把一切轻浮的妄念驱得远远的。随着这荡气回肠的声响，太阳躲进了一片阴云；两面小湖——本来就清得过水晶——竟变得殷红如血。这一整个煊赫的队伍，身不由己地被牵向山坡。众人垂着眼，个个羞惭伫立，各自数落着自己造下的孽。迪拉拉的心突突直跳；宦官之首长叹一声悔罪，向那些他曾为一己快意百般折磨过的女人们乞求饶恕。

瓦提克和努罗尼哈在御辇里面无人色，彼此相顾，神色枯槁，各自懊责——一个想着千般最黑重的罪孽、千般渎神的野心图谋；一个想着破败的门庭，和那可爱的古尔申鲁兹的沉沦。努罗尼哈恍惚觉得，在那夺命的乐声里听见了垂死父亲的呻吟；瓦提克则听见了献给吉奥尔的五十个孩子的啜泣。在这般交并的痛楚之中，他们只觉自己被牵向那牧羊人；他的面容威严赫赫，竟叫瓦提克生平头一遭感到了慑服，努罗尼哈则双手掩住了脸。乐声一停，那精灵便对哈里发说道：

===Original===


“Deluded Prince! to whom Providence hath confided the care of innumerable subjects, is it thus that thou fulfillest thy mission? Thy crimes are already completed; and, art thou now hastening towards thy punishment? Thou knowest, that beyond these mountains, Eblis and his accursed Dives hold their infernal empire; and seduced by a malignant phantom, thou art proceeding to surrender thyself to them! This moment is the last of grace allowed thee! Abandon thy atrocious purpose. Return. Give back Nouronihar to her father, who still retains a few sparks of life. Destroy thy tower, with all its abominations. Drive Carathis from thy councils. Be just to thy subjects. Respect the ministers of the Prophet. Compensate for thy impieties by an exemplary life; and, instead of squandering thy days in voluptuous indulgence, lament thy crimes on the sepulchres of thy ancestors. Thou beholdest the clouds that obscure the sun; at the instant he recovers his splendour, if thy heart be not changed, the time of mercy assigned thee will be past forever.”
Vathek, depressed with fear, was on the point of prostrating himself at the feet of the shepherd, whom he perceived to be of a nature superior to man, but his pride prevailing, he audaciously lifted his head, and glancing at him one of his terrible looks, said:
“Whoever thou art, withhold thy useless admonitions. Thou wouldst either delude me, or art thyself deceived. If what I have done be so criminal as thou pretendest, there remains not for me a moment of grace. I have traversed a sea of blood, to acquire a power which will make thy equals tremble; deem not that I shall retire when in view of the port; or that I will relinquish her who is dearer to me than either my life or thy mercy. Let the sun appear! Let him illumine my career! It matters not where it may end.”

===Chinese===


“痴迷的君王！上天赋你照管万民之责，你就这样践你的使命么？你的罪已经作满，如今倒要赶着去领你的刑罚了么？你知道，山那边，埃布利斯和他那些可咒的迪夫们正盘踞着他们的幽冥帝国；你被一个恶毒的幻影诱着，竟要去自投罗网！此刻便是容你的最后恩典！快撇了那凶残的图谋。回去。把努罗尼哈还给她父亲——他还剩着一口游丝之气。拆了你的塔，连同塔里一切可憎之物。把卡拉希丝逐出你的枢机。善待你的臣民。敬重先知的仆役们。以卓立的善行补赎你的渎神；与其把光阴抛掷在纵欲享乐里，不如到你祖先的陵前去哭你的罪。你看见遮蔽太阳的云了；待太阳重放光明的那一刻，你若还不回心转意，指给你的宽宥之期便永远过去了。”

瓦提克被恐惧压住，眼看就要拜倒在牧羊人脚下——他已看出对方非人类之属——怎奈骄心终究占了上风，他悍然昂起头，朝那精灵狠狠盯了一眼，说道：

“不管你是谁，收起你那没用的训诫吧。你若不是想诓我，便是自己受了骗。我做过的事若真如你所说那般罪孽深重，那我连一瞬的宽宥也不剩了。我蹚过了一片血海，来攫取一种能叫你的同类发抖的权能；莫指望我望见了港口还肯折返，莫指望我肯舍下她——她比我的性命贵重，也比你的慈悲贵重。让太阳出来吧！让它照亮我的前程！前程终于何处，又有何妨。”

===Original===


On uttering these words, which made even the Genius shudder, Vathek threw himself into the arms of Nouronihar, and commanded that his horses should be forced back to the road.
There was no difficulty in obeying these orders, for the attraction had ceased, the sun shone forth in all his glory, and the shepherd vanished with a lamentable scream.
The fatal impression of the music of the Genius remained, notwithstanding, in the hearts of Vathek’s attendants. They viewed each other with looks of consternation. At the approach of night, almost all of them escaped; and, of this numerous assemblage, there only remained the chief of the eunuchs, some idolatrous slaves, Dilara, and a few other women, who, like herself, were votaries of the religion of the Magi.

===Chinese===


这话一出，连那精灵也不寒而栗。瓦提克一头扑进努罗尼哈怀里，吩咐人把马硬拉回大路上去。

这道命令执行起来毫无难处：那股摄引力已经止了，太阳大放光明，牧羊人发出一声凄厉的长嚎，踪影全无。

然而，精灵那乐声的夺命印痕，仍旧留在瓦提克随从们的心里。他们面面相觑，个个惊惶失色。夜色将临，这些人几乎逃散一空；那一大队人马里，只剩下宦官之首、几个拜偶像的奴隶、迪拉拉，和另外几个同她一样信奉祆教的女人。

===Original===


The Caliph, fired with the ambition of prescribing laws to the Intelligences of Darkness, was but little embarrassed at this dereliction. The impetuosity of his blood prevented him from sleeping; nor did he encamp any more as before. Nouronihar, whose impatience, if possible, exceeded his own, importuned him to hasten his march, and lavished on him a thousand caresses, to beguile all reflection. She fancied herself already more potent than Balkis;25 and pictured to her imagination the Genii falling prostrate at the foot of her throne. In this manner they advanced by moonlight, till they came within view of the two towering rocks, that form a kind of portal to the valley, at whose extremity rose the vast ruins of Istakar. Aloft on the mountain, glimmered the fronts of various royal mausoleums, the horror of which was deepened by the shadows of night. They passed through two villages, almost deserted; the only inhabitants remaining being a few feeble old men, who at the sight of horses and litters fell upon their knees, and cried out:

===Chinese===


哈里发一心梦想要给黑暗的诸智灵立法，对这场星散并不怎么挂心。他血气沸腾，睡也睡不着，也不像先前那样安营了。努罗尼哈的性急若还能甚于他，便更催他兼程赶路，又把千百种温存倾倒在他身上，好哄得他把一切思量都忘掉。她恍惚觉得自己已经比巴尔吉丝（Balkis）25 还要威风，想象着精灵们纷纷拜倒在她宝座脚下。他们就这样乘着月色前行，直到望见两座高耸的巨岩——它们构成谷口一般的门户——谷的尽头，伊斯特卡尔庞大的废墟巍然而起。高山之上，隐约闪动着几座王家陵墓的正面，夜影沉沉，更添了几分阴森。他们穿过两座几乎荒废的村子；仅剩的居民是几个衰弱的老头儿，一见车马便双膝跪倒，哭喊道：

===Original===


“O heaven! is it then by these phantoms that we have been for six months tormented! Alas! it was from the terror of these spectres, and the noise beneath the mountains, that our people have fled, and left us at the mercy of maleficent spirits!”
The Caliph, to whom these complaints were but unpromising auguries, drove over the bodies of these wretched old men, and at length arrived at the foot of the terrace of black marble. There he descended from his litter, handing down Nouronihar; both, with beating hearts, stared wildly around them, and expected, with an apprehensive shudder, the approach of the Giaour. But nothing as yet announced his appearance.

===Chinese===


“天哪！折磨了我们六个月的，原来就是这些幽灵么！唉！正是被这些鬼影的恐怖，加上山底下的声响，吓得我们的人都逃光了，撇下我们任凭凶神恶煞发落！”

这些怨言在哈里发听来，不过是些不祥的前兆；他驱车从这些可怜老人的身躯上碾了过去，终于到达黑石大台的脚下。他在那里下了御辇，携努罗尼哈下来；两人心头突突乱跳，狂乱地环顾四周，一面打着寒噤，一面等候吉奥尔驾临。可是尚无任何动静宣告他的到来。

===Original===


A deathlike stillness reigned over the mountain, and through the air. The moon dilated, on a vast platform, the shades of the lofty columns, which reached from the terrace almost to the clouds. The gloomy watchtowers, whose number could not be counted, were veiled by no roof: and their capitals, of an architecture unknown in the records of the earth, served as an asylum for the birds of darkness, which, alarmed at the approach of such visitants, fled away croaking.
The chief of the eunuchs, trembling with fear, besought Vathek that a fire might be kindled.
“No!” replied he, “there is no time left to think of such trifles; abide where thou art, and expect my commands.”
Having thus spoken, he presented his hand to Nouronihar, and ascending the steps of a vast staircase, reached the terrace, which was flagged with squares of marble, and resembled a smooth expanse of water, upon whose surface not a leaf ever dared to vegetate. On the right rose the watchtowers, ranged before the ruins of an immense palace, whose walls were embossed with various figures. In front stood forth the colossal forms of four creatures, composed of the leopard and the griffin; and though but of stone, inspired emotions of terror. Near these were distinguished by the splendour of the moon, which streamed full on the place, characters like those on the sabres of the Giaour, that possessed the same virtue of changing every moment. These, after vacillating for some time, at last fixed in Arabic letters, and prescribed to the Caliph the following words:

===Chinese===


一片死一般的寂静笼罩着山峦，弥漫在空气里。月亮把高柱的影子投在一片广阔的平台上——那些柱子从台面几乎直抵云霄。阴森森的望楼，数也数不清，全都不加顶盖；柱头上是一种尘世典籍里见所未见的建筑式样，成了黑暗之鸟的巢窟；那些鸟被这一行来客惊起，呱呱地飞远了。

宦官之首吓得发抖，恳求瓦提克生一堆火。

“不！”他答道，“没工夫想这些琐事了；你待在原地，听候我的吩咐。”

说罢，他向努罗尼哈伸出手，登上一道巨大台阶的级级石级，到了台面上。台面用一方方大理石铺就，宛如一泓平滑的水面，从来不敢有一片叶芽在它上面生发。右边耸起那些望楼，排列在一座巨大宫殿的废墟之前，宫墙上浮雕着各色形象。正面立着四尊庞然的巨兽，由豹与狮鹫拼合而成，虽是石头凿成，也令人望而生畏。月光正满泻在这地方，照出巨兽近旁的文字，同吉奥尔刀剑上的那些如出一辙，也有一刻一变的神通。这些字游移了半晌，终于定成阿拉伯字母，向哈里发宣示了下面的话：

===Original===


“Vathek! thou hast violated the conditions of my parchment, and deservest to be sent back; but in favour to thy companion, and as the meed for what thou hast done to obtain it, Eblis permitteth that the portal of his palace shall be opened, and the subterranean fire will receive thee into the number of its adorers.”
He scarcely had read these words before the mountain, against which the terrace was reared, trembled; and the watchtowers were ready to topple headlong upon them. The rock yawned, and disclosed within it a staircase of polished marble, that seemed to approach the abyss. Upon each stair were planted two large torches, like those Nouronihar had seen in her vision, the camphorated vapour ascending from which gathered into a cloud under the hollow of the vault.

===Chinese===


“瓦提克！你已违了我羊皮卷上的条件，本该把你打发回去；但念在你的同伴份上，也念你为得到她所下的本钱，埃布利斯恩准开启他宫殿的门扉，地下之火将收你入它的礼拜者之列。”

字才读完，那座高台倚着的山便震颤起来，望楼险些儿迎头砸在他们身上。岩石豁然张开，露出里面一道磨光大理石的阶梯，仿佛直通深渊。每级石阶上立着两支大火炬，正同努罗尼哈在异象中所见的一样，樟脑的烟气袅袅上升，在穹窿底下聚成一团云。

===Original===


This appearance, instead of terrifying, gave new courage to the daughter of Fakreddin. Scarcely deigning to bid adieu to the moon and the firmament, she abandoned without hesitation the pure atmosphere, to plunge into these infernal exhalations. The gait of those impious personages was haughty and determined. As they descended, by the effulgence of the torches, they gazed on each other with mutual admiration, and both appeared so resplendent, that they already esteemed themselves spiritual intelligences. The only circumstance that perplexed them, was their not arriving at the bottom of the stairs. On hastening their descent, with an ardent impetuosity, they felt their steps accelerated to such a degree, that they seemed not walking, but falling from a precipice. Their progress, however, was at length impeded by a vast portal of ebony, which the Caliph without difficulty recognized. Here the Giaour awaited them, with the key in his hand,
“Ye are welcome!” said he to them, with a ghastly smile, “in spite of Muhammad, and all his dependents. I will now admit you into that palace, where you have so highly merited a place.”
Whilst he was uttering these words, he touched the enamelled lock with his key, and the doors at once expanded with a noise still louder than the thunder of mountains, and as suddenly recoiled the moment they had entered.

===Chinese===


这番景象非但没有吓住法克雷丁的女儿，反倒给她添了新的胆气。她几乎不屑向月亮和苍穹道一声别，便毫不犹豫地抛却清朗的大气，一头扎进这地狱的瘴雾里。这两个渎神人物的步态，高傲而决绝。下阶时，凭着火炬的辉光，他们彼此对望，互相叹赏；两人都显得那般光彩焕发，竟已自视为灵界之神明了。唯一叫他们纳闷的是，老也走不到阶梯的尽头。两人性急火燎地加快脚步，只觉步子快得出奇，仿佛不是在行走，而是从悬崖上坠落。他们的行进终于被一座巨大的乌木门拦住，哈里发毫不费力便认出了它。吉奥尔手执钥匙，正在这里候着他们，

“欢迎之至！”他面带狞笑，对他们说道，“到底是逆着穆罕默德和他那一干仆从来了。我现在就引你们进那座宫殿，你们在那里的席位，可是挣得再体面不过了。”

话音未落，他用钥匙一触那珐琅锁，两扇门轰然洞开，声响比山中的雷霆还猛；待他们进去，门又倏然合拢。

===Original===


The Caliph and Nouronihar beheld each other with amazement, at finding themselves in a place which, though roofed with a vaulted ceiling, was so spacious and lofty, that at first they took it for an immeasurable plain. But their eyes at length growing to the grandeur of the objects at hand, they extended their view to those at a distance, and discovered rows of columns and arcades, which gradually diminished, till they terminated in a point, radiant as the sun, when he darts his last beams athwart the ocean. The pavement, strewed over with gold dust and saffron, exhaled so subtle an odour, as almost overpowered them. They, however, went on, and observed an infinity of censers, in which ambergris and the wood of aloes were continually burning. Between the several columns were placed tables, each spread with a profusion of viands, and wines of every species, sparkling in vases of crystal. A throng of Genii, and other fantastic spirits, of each sex, danced lasciviously in troops, at the sound of music which issued from beneath.
In the midst of this immense hall, a vast multitude was incessantly passing, who severally kept their right hands on their hearts, without once regarding anything around them. They had all the livid paleness of death. Their eyes, deep sank in their sockets, resembled those phosphoric meteors, that glimmer by night in places of interment. Some stalked slowly on, absorbed in profound reverie; some shrieking with agony, ran furiously about, like tigers wounded with poisoned arrows; whilst others, grinding their teeth in rage, foamed along, more frantic than the wildest maniac. They all avoided each other, and though surrounded by a multitude that no one could number, each wandered at random unheedful of the rest, as if alone on a desert which no foot had trodden.

===Chinese===


哈里发和努罗尼哈发现置身于这样一个所在，不禁彼此愕然相顾：这里虽有穹顶为盖，却广阔高旷得叫两人起初当它是一片无垠的平野。待眼睛渐渐习惯了眼前事物的宏大，他们又把目光放远，看出一列列的柱廊与拱廊，渐远渐细，末了收束成一点，辉煌如夕阳把最后一抹余晖横掷过海面时那样。地面上铺洒着金粉与番红花，蒸起一股异香，馥郁得几乎叫人站立不住。他们继续前行，只见无数香炉，炉中龙涎香与沉香木长燃不熄。一根根柱子之间安放着桌案，每张都堆满了珍馐，各色美酒在水晶瓶中熠熠生光。一大群精灵，还有男女兼备的各色奇幻神魔，成群结队，合着从地底涌出的乐声，妖冶地舞蹈。

这座巨厅中央，有一大群川流不息的人经过，个个把右手按在心口，对周围的一切连望也不望。他们个个带着死一般的青灰之色。一双双眼睛深深陷在眼窝里，恰似坟茔之地夜间的磷火，幽幽闪烁。有的踽踽缓行，沉在深深的迷惘里；有的痛得尖声长嚎，疯狂乱窜，像中了毒箭的老虎；还有的咬牙切齿，口喷白沫，比最狂的疯子还要癫狂。他们彼此避让，虽身处数不清的人群之中，却各自茫然游荡，对其余的人浑不理会，仿佛独自在一片人迹未至的荒漠上。

===Original===


Vathek and Nouronihar, frozen with terror at a sight so baleful, demanded of the Giaour what these appearances might mean, and why these ambulating spectres never withdrew their hands from their hearts.
“Perplex not yourselves,” replied he bluntly, “with so much at once, you will soon be acquainted with all; let us haste and present you to Eblis.”
They continued their way through the multitude, but notwithstanding their confidence at first, they were not sufficiently composed to examine with attention the various perspectives of halls, and of galleries, that opened on the right hand and left, which were all illuminated by torches and braziers, whose flames rose in pyramids, to the centre of the vault. At length they came to a place where long curtains, brocaded with crimson and gold, fell from all parts, in striking confusion. Here the choirs and dances were heard no longer. The light which glimmered came from afar.

===Chinese===


瓦提克和努罗尼哈见了这等凶象，吓得浑身冰凉，忙问吉奥尔这些光景是何意思，这些游荡的鬼影为何总把手按在心口，从不放下。

“别一口气操这么多闲心，”他没好气地答道，“你们很快就全明白了；快走，让我引你们去见埃布利斯。”

他们从人群中穿行而过；虽然起初满有把握，此时却心神不定，无暇细看左右两面展开的一重重厅堂与回廊——那里全靠火炬与火盆照着，火焰层层叠起如金字塔，直抵穹顶中央。

===Original===


After some time Vathek and Nouronihar perceived a gleam brightening through the drapery, and entered a vast tabernacle, carpeted with the skins of leopards. An infinity of elders, with streaming beards, and afreets, in complete armour, had prostrated themselves before the ascent of a lofty eminence, on the top of which, upon a globe of fire, sat the formidable Eblis. His person was that of a young man, whose noble and regular features seemed to have been tarnished by malignant vapours. In his large eyes appeared both pride and despair; his flowing hair retained some resemblance to that of an angel of light. In his hand, which thunder had blasted, he swayed the iron sceptre, that causes the monster Ouranabad,26 the afreets, and all the powers of the abyss to tremble. At his presence the heart of the Caliph sank within him, and, for the first time, he fell prostrate on his face. Nouronihar, however, though greatly dismayed, could not help admiring the person of Eblis, for she expected to have seen some stupendous giant. Eblis, with a voice more mild than might be imagined, but such as transfused through the soul the deepest melancholy, said:

===Chinese===


过了一阵，瓦提克和努罗尼哈望见帷幔后透出一团渐亮的光，便走进一座巨大的圣殿，地上铺满豹皮。无数长髯垂胸的长老，和披挂齐整的阿弗里特，都匍匐在一座高台的阶前；台顶上一团火球，火球上端坐着威严的埃布利斯。他的身形是个青年，眉宇高贵端正，却像被恶毒的瘴气熏得黯淡了。一双大眼里骄傲与绝望并现；披散的长发还留着几分光明天使的模样。他那只为雷霆击过的手，握着铁杖——这一杖能叫怪兽乌拉纳巴德（Ouranabad）26、阿弗里特们和深渊的一切权能发抖。一见他的面，哈里发的心便沉了下去，生平头一遭，他伏倒在地。努罗尼哈虽也大为惊惶，却禁不住暗暗赞叹埃布利斯的仪表——她还当要见到什么顶天立地的巨人呢。埃布利斯开口了，声音比想象中温和，却把一种最深切的忧郁沁进人的魂灵。他说：

===Original===


“Creatures of clay, I receive you into mine empire. Ye are numbered amongst my adorers. Enjoy whatever this palace affords﻿—the treasures of the preadamite sultans, their bickering sabres, and those talismans that compel the Dives to open the subterranean expanses of the mountain of Kaf, which communicate with these. There, insatiable as your curiosity may be, shall you find sufficient to gratify it. You shall possess the exclusive privilege of entering the fortress of Aherman, and the halls of Argenk, where are portrayed all creatures endowed with intelligence, and the various animals that inhabited the earth prior to the creation of that contemptible being, whom ye denominate the Father of Mankind.”
Vathek and Nouronihar feeling themselves revived and encouraged by this harangue, eagerly said to the Giaour:
“Bring us instantly to the place which contains these precious talismans.”
“Come,” answered this wicked Dive, with his malignant grin, “come, and possess all that my sovereign hath promised, and more.”

===Chinese===


“泥土造的生灵，我收你们进我的帝国。你们已列于我的礼拜者之数。这宫殿里有什么，尽管享用——洪荒前的众苏丹的财宝、他们寒光闪闪的弯刀，还有那些护符：凭着它们，迪夫们便不得不打开卡夫山底下四通八达的洞府，与这里处处相通。到了那里，任凭你们的好奇心再不知足，也尽够你们餍足。你们还将独享特权，进入阿赫曼（Aherman）的堡垒和阿尔根克（Argenk）的殿庑——那里绘着一切有灵之受造物，和你们称作‘人类始祖’的那个可鄙东西受造之前栖居世上的种种禽兽。”

这一篇训示叫瓦提克和努罗尼哈精神重振、胆气顿生，两人急切地对吉奥尔说：

“快带我们到收藏这些宝符的地方去。”

“来吧，”这个凶恶的迪夫狞笑着答道，“来吧，我君上许给你们的一切，你们尽去占有，还有加倍的。”

===Original===


He then conducted them into a long aisle adjoining the tabernacle, preceding them with hasty steps, and followed by his disciples with the utmost alacrity. They reached at length a hall of great extent, and covered with a lofty dome, around which appeared fifty portals of bronze, secured with as many fastenings of iron. A funereal gloom prevailed over the whole scene. Here, upon two beds of incorruptible cedar, lay recumbent the fleshless forms of the preadamite kings, who had been monarchs of the whole earth. They still possessed enough of life to be conscious of their deplorable condition. Their eyes retained a melancholy motion; they regarded each other with looks of the deepest dejection, each holding his right hand motionless on his heart. At their feet were inscribed the events of their several reigns, their power, their pride, and their crimes. Soliman Raad, Soliman Daki, and Soliman Di Gian Ben Gian, who, after having chained up the Dives in the dark caverns of Kaf, became so presumptuous, as to doubt of the Supreme Power. All these maintained great state, though not to be compared with the eminence of Soliman Ben Daoud.

===Chinese===


于是他把他们引进圣殿旁一条长长的甬道，自己脚步匆匆在前引路，他的门徒们兴致勃勃地跟在后面。他们终于来到一座极宽广的大厅，上覆高穹，四围开着五十道铜门，门门都用铁闩锁着。一片丧葬似的阴郁笼罩着整个景象。这里，两张不朽雪松榻上，直挺挺卧着洪荒前众王的枯骨之躯——他们都曾君临过整个大地。他们尚存的一点生气，还够他们意识到自己可悲的处境。一双双眼睛还带着忧郁的转动；他们彼此对望，神色颓丧到极点，个个右手一动不动地按在心口。他们脚边铭刻着各自在位的业绩：他们的权势、他们的骄横、他们的罪愆。苏莱曼·拉德（Soliman Raad）、苏莱曼·达基（Soliman Daki）、苏莱曼·迪·吉安·本·吉安（Soliman Di Gian Ben Gian）——这一位把迪夫们锁进卡夫山黑窟之后，竟狂妄到怀疑起至高的权能。这些君主个个维持着偌大的排场，只是比不得苏莱曼·本·达乌德那样尊崇。

===Original===


This king, so renowned for his wisdom, was on the loftiest elevation, and placed immediately under the dome. He appeared to possess more animation than the rest, though, from time to time, he laboured with profound sighs, and, like his companions, kept his right hand on his heart; yet his countenance was more composed, and he seemed to be listening to the sullen roar of a vast cataract, visible in part through the grated portals. This was the only sound that intruded on the silence of these doleful mansions. A range of brazen vases surrounded the elevation.
“Remove the covers from these cabalistic depositaries,” said the Giaour to Vathek, “and avail thyself of the talismans, which will break asunder all these gates of bronze, and not only render thee master of the treasures contained within them, but also of the spirits by which they are guarded.”
The Caliph, whom this ominous preliminary had entirely disconcerted, approached the vases with faltering footsteps, and was ready to sink with terror, when he heard the groans of Soliman. As he proceeded, a voice from the livid lips of the prophet articulated these words:

===Chinese===


这位以智慧著称的君王，卧在最高的位置上，正对着穹顶之下。他仿佛比其余的更有生气，虽不时憋出深沉的叹息，也像同伴们一样右手按着心口；但他的面容较为安静，仿佛在侧耳听一道巨瀑沉闷的吼声——隔着铁栅门望得见它的一部分。这是唯一搅扰这凄凉府邸之寂静的声响。高台四围环列着一圈铜瓮。

“揭开这些秘藏瓮的盖子，”吉奥尔对瓦提克说，“那些护符自会供你驱使：它们能劈开所有这些铜门，不但叫门里的财宝尽归你，连看守财宝的众精灵也归你掌管。”

这凶兆的前奏吓得哈里发方寸大乱，他脚步虚浮地挨近那些铜瓮，一听见苏莱曼的呻吟，险些瘫倒。他往前走时，先知青灰的唇间发出声音，一字一字说道：

===Original===


“In my lifetime, I filled a magnificent throne, having on my right hand twelve thousand seats of gold, where the patriarchs and prophets heard my doctrines; on my left the sages and doctors, upon as many thrones of silver, were present at all my decisions. Whilst I thus administered justice to innumerable multitudes, the birds of the air librating over me, served as a canopy from the rays of the sun. My people flourished, and my palace rose to the clouds. I erected a temple to the Most High, which was the wonder of the universe; but I basely suffered myself to be seduced by the love of women, and a curiosity that could not be restrained by sublunary things. I listened to the counsels of Aherman, and the daughter of Pharaoh; and adored fire, and the host of heaven. I forsook the holy city, and commanded the Genii to rear the stupendous palace of Istakar, and the terrace of the watchtowers, each of which was consecrated to a star. There for a while I enjoyed myself in the zenith of glory and pleasure. Not only men, but supernatural existences were subject also to my will. I began to think, as these unhappy monarchs around had already thought, that the vengeance of heaven was asleep, when at once the thunder burst my structures asunder, and precipitated me hither; where, however, I do not remain like the other inhabitants totally destitute of hope, for an angel of light hath revealed, that in consideration of the piety of my early youth, my woes shall come to an end when this cataract shall forever cease to flow. Till then I am in torments, ineffable torments, an unrelenting fire preys on my heart.”

===Chinese===


“我在世之时，坐的是赫赫宝座：右手边一万二千张金座，众族长与先知在上面听我讲道；左手边众贤士与博士，坐在同样数目的银座上，出席我一切的裁断。我这样为无数兆民执掌公义时，空中的飞鸟翔集在我头顶，替我张成一顶华盖，遮住日光。我的百姓昌盛，我的宫殿高耸入云。我为至高者建了一座圣殿，堪称宇宙奇观；但我卑劣地任凭自己被对女色的贪恋迷住，还有一种尘世之物拘不住的好奇心。我听信了阿赫曼和法老之女的谋划，敬拜了火与天上的万象。我抛下圣城，命精灵们造起伊斯特卡尔那座惊人的宫殿，和望楼的高台——每一座都奉献给一颗星。在那里，我一时也真享尽了荣华与欢乐的极盛。不但世人，连超凡的存在也臣服于我的意志。我开始想——正如四周这些不幸的君王早已想过的那样——上天的震怒睡着了；霎时间雷霆炸碎了我的殿宇，把我贬到这里。不过在这里，我不像别的居人那样全然无望，因为一位光明的天使曾启示：念我少年时的虔敬，待这道瀑布永远断流，我的苦难便有尽头。在那之前，我受着煎熬，难以言喻的煎熬，一团不熄的火啃噬着我的心。”

===Original===


Having uttered this exclamation, Soliman raised his hands towards heaven, in token of supplication, and the Caliph discerned through his bosom, which was transparent as crystal, his heart enveloped in flames. At a sight so full of horror, Nouronihar fell back like one petrified, into the arms of Vathek, who cried out with a convulsive sob:
“O Giaour! whither hast thou brought us! Allow us to depart, and I will relinquish all thou hast promised. O Muhammad! remains there no more mercy!”

===Chinese===


这声哀诉出口，苏莱曼便双手举向天空，作恳求之状；哈里发透过他那水晶般透明的胸膛，看见他的心裹在火焰之中。一见这等骇人的光景，努罗尼哈像石头人一样向后倒入瓦提克怀中，瓦提克抽咽着喊道：

“吉奥尔啊！你把我们带到什么鬼地方来了！放我们走吧，你许下的一切我都不要了。穆罕默德啊！再没有一丝怜悯了么！”

===Original===


“None! none!” replied the malicious Dive. “Know, miserable prince, thou art now in the abode of vengeance, and despair. Thy heart, also, will be kindled, like those of the other votaries of Eblis. A few days are allotted thee previous to this fatal period: employ them as thou wilt. Recline on these heaps of gold: command the Infernal Potentates: range at thy pleasure through these immense subterranean domains. No barrier shall be shut against thee. As for me, I have fulfilled my mission. I now leave thee to thyself.”
At these words he vanished.

===Chinese===


“没有了！没有了！”那凶恶的迪夫答道，“要知道，不幸的君王，你如今身在复仇与绝望的居所。你的心，也终将像埃布利斯的其他信徒一样被点燃。那命定的时辰到来之前，还准你苟活几日：随你怎么用吧。躺到这些金山上：去驱遣地狱的诸权贵：在这些浩大的地下疆域里随意游荡。任何一道门都不会对你关闭。至于我，使命已了。现在，我把你交还给你自己。”

话音一落，他便消失了。

===Original===


The Caliph and Nouronihar remained in the most abject affliction. Their tears unable to flow, scarcely could they support themselves. At length, taking each other despondingly by the hand, they went faltering from this fatal hall, indifferent which way they turned their steps. Every portal opened at their approach. The Dives fell prostrate before them. Every reservoir of riches was disclosed to their view, but they no longer felt the incentives of curiosity, pride, or avarice. With like apathy they heard the chorus of Genii, and saw the stately banquets prepared to regale them. They went wandering on from chamber to chamber, hall to hall, and gallery to gallery; all without bounds or limit; all distinguishable by the same lowering gloom; all adorned with the same awful grandeur; all traversed by persons in search of repose and consolation, but who sought them in vain, for everyone carried within him a heart tormented in flames. Shunned by these various sufferers, who seemed by their looks to be upbraiding the partners of their guilt, they withdrew from them, to wait in direful suspense the moment which should render them to each other the like objects of terror.

===Chinese===


哈里发和努罗尼哈陷入最卑屈的哀恸。眼泪流不出来，身子几乎撑持不住。末了，两人灰心丧气地执手相携，踉踉跄跄走出这座夺命的大厅，脚步迈向哪边也全不在意。他们一路走去，门户洞开，迪夫们纷纷拜倒，一座座藏宝之所尽陈眼前，可是好奇、骄矜与贪欲的冲动，都已不复唤得起了。精灵们的合唱、为他们盛设的华筵，他们也一概漠然听着看着。他们从一间室到一间室、一座厅到一座厅、一条廊到一条廊地游荡；处处无边无际；处处笼着同样沉沉的阴翳；处处妆点着同样可畏的壮丽；处处都是寻觅安息与慰藉的人穿行其间——却个个寻而不得，因为人人胸中都揣着一颗受火煎熬的心。这些受苦的人个个避着他们，神色间仿佛在责骂这对同罪的伙伴；他们便从人前退开，在可怖的悬揣中，等候那一刻的到来——到那时，他们也将彼此成为同样可怖之物。

===Original===


“What,” exclaimed Nouronihar, “will the time come, when I shall snatch my hand from thine!”
“Ah!” said Vathek, “and shall my eyes ever cease to drink from thine long draughts of enjoyment! Shall the moments of our reciprocal ecstasies be reflected on with horror! It was not thou that broughtest me hither; the principles by which Carathis perverted my youth have been the sole cause of my perdition!”
Having given vent to these painful expressions, he called to an Afreet, who was stirring up one of the braziers, and bade him fetch the Princess Carathis from the palace of Samarah.

===Chinese===


“什么！”努罗尼哈喊道，“难道终有一天，我要把自己的手从你手里抽走么！”

“唉！”瓦提克说，“难道我的眼睛，终有不再从你眼中畅饮欢愉的一天！难道我们彼此销魂的时光，回想起来竟只剩恐怖！诱我到此地来的并不是你；是卡拉希丝败坏我少年时的那套道理，把我独自送上了毁灭！”

这一番痛语倾吐之后，他唤过一个正在拨火的阿弗里特，吩咐他去萨迈拉宫中把卡拉希丝公主接来。

===Original===


After issuing these orders, the Caliph and Nouronihar continued walking amidst the silent crowd, till they heard voices at the end of the gallery. Presuming them to proceed from some unhappy beings, who like themselves were awaiting their final doom, they followed the sound, and found it to come from a small square chamber, where they discovered sitting on sofas, five young men of goodly figure, and a lovely female, who were all holding a melancholy conversation, by the glimmering of a lonely lamp. Each had a gloomy and forlorn air, and two of them were embracing each other with great tenderness. On seeing the Caliph and the daughter of Fakreddin enter they arose, saluted, and gave them place. Then he who had appeared the most considerable of the group, addressed himself thus to Vathek:
“Strangers! who doubtless are in the same state of suspense as ourselves, as you do not yet bear your hand on your heart, if you are come hither to pass the interval allotted previous to the infliction of our common punishment, condescend to relate the adventures that have brought you to this fatal place; and we in return will acquaint you with ours; which deserves but too well to be heard. We will trace back our crimes to their source, though we are not permitted to repent. This is the only employment suited to wretches like us.”

===Chinese===


吩咐既毕，哈里发和努罗尼哈继续在沉默的人群中信步走着，忽听得回廊尽头有人声。他们料想那也是些同他们一样等候最终判决的不幸者，便循声而去，发现声音出自一间四方的小室：五位仪表堂堂的青年和一位可爱的女子，正就着一盏孤灯的微光，坐在长沙发上凄然交谈。个个神色阴郁凄惶，其中两个还满怀柔情地彼此搂抱着。见哈里发和法克雷丁的女儿进来，众人起身见礼，让出座位。于是那位看起来最居首的青年，对瓦提克这样说道：

“远来的客人！看你们还不曾把手按在心口，想必也同我们一样悬着心。你们若也是来度过共同刑罚降临前这段期限的，就请屈尊讲讲是怎样的奇遇把你们引到这夺命之地；我们也把自己的经历奉告——那可是太值得一听了。我们要把各自的罪追溯到源头，虽然上头不许我们悔改。这乃是适合我们这等可怜虫的唯一营生。”

===Original===


The Caliph and Nouronihar assented to the proposal, and Vathek began, not without tears and lamentations, a sincere recital of every circumstance that had passed. When the afflicting narrative was closed, the young man entered on his own. Each person proceeded in order, and when the fourth prince had reached the midst of his adventures, a sudden noise interrupted him, which caused the vault to tremble, and to open.
Immediately a cloud descended, which gradually dissipating, discovered Carathis, on the back of an Afreet, who grievously complained of his burden. She, instantly springing to the ground, advanced towards her son, and said:
“What dost thou here, in this little square chamber? As the Dives are become subject to thy beck, I expected to have found thee on the throne of the preadamite kings.”
“Execrable woman!” answered the Caliph; “cursed be the day thou gavest me birth! Go! follow this Afreet; let him conduct thee to the hall of the Prophet Soliman; there thou wilt learn to what these palaces are destined, and how much I ought to abhor the impious knowledge thou hast taught me.”

===Chinese===


哈里发和努罗尼哈应允了，瓦提克便含着眼泪、带着哀叹，把前后种种情由老老实实讲了一遍。这段伤心的叙述完结后，那青年也讲起自己的来历。众人依次讲下去；待第四位王子讲到他的奇遇中段，忽有一阵巨响打断了他，震得穹顶簌簌发抖，随即豁然洞开。

霎时一朵云降了下来，渐渐散开，露出骑在一名阿弗里特背上的卡拉希丝——那阿弗里特被这份重负压得叫苦不迭。她一跃下地，径直走向儿子，说道：

“你在这四方小屋里做什么？迪夫们既已听你指使，我还道你已坐上洪荒前众王的宝座了呢。”

“可咒的女人！”哈里发答道，“你生我的那一天就该受诅咒！去！跟着这个阿弗里特；让他领你到先知苏莱曼的厅里去；到那里你就知道这些宫殿是派什么用场的，也知道我该多么痛恨你教给我的那套渎神的学问。”

===Original===


“The height of power to which thou art arrived, has certainly turned thy brain,” answered Carathis; “but I ask no more, than permission to show my respect for the prophet. It is, however, proper thou shouldst know, that, as the Afreet has informed me neither of us shall return to Samarah, I requested his permission to arrange my affairs, and he politely consented. Availing myself, therefore, of the few moments allowed me, I set fire to the tower, and consumed in it the mutes, negresses, and serpents, which have rendered me so much good service; nor should I have been less kind to Morakanabad, had he not prevented me, by deserting at last to thy brother. As for Bababalouk, who had the folly to return to Samarah, and all the good brotherhood to provide husbands for thy wives, I undoubtedly would have put them to the torture, could I but have allowed them the time. Being, however, in a hurry, I only hung him, after having caught him in a snare with thy wives; whilst them I buried alive by the help of my negresses, who thus spent their last moments, greatly to their satisfaction. With respect to Dilara, who ever stood high in my favour, she hath evinced the greatness of her mind, by fixing herself near, in the service of one of the Magi, and, I think, will soon be our own.”

===Chinese===


“你爬到的这点权位，果真叫你昏了头，”卡拉希丝答道，“不过我只求一件事：容我向先知表一表敬意。只是有件事你理当知道：那阿弗里特告诉我，你我谁都回不去萨迈拉了，我便求他容我料理料理后事，他客客气气地应允了。于是，趁着准我的这点工夫，我一把火烧了塔，连同塔里那些一直尽心竭力伺候我的哑仆、黑女奴和毒蛇，一并送了进去；莫拉卡纳巴德若不是临了投奔你兄弟去，我也少不了照样成全他。至于巴巴巴鲁克——那个蠢得回萨迈拉去、还领着那班好弟兄给你的妻妾们张罗改嫁的——只要时间容得，我必定好好让他们吃一顿刑。只是我赶时候，只把他吊死了，那还是先把他同你的妻妾们一网捉住之后；那些妻妾呢，我让黑女奴们帮我活埋了——她们自己最后这点光阴也这样打发掉，个个心满意足。说到迪拉拉，她一向得我的欢心，也真显出了她的气魄：就近安顿下来，去伺候一位祆教祭司了；我想，她不久也快归我们这一伙了。”

===Original===


Vathek, too much cast down to express the indignation excited by such a discourse, ordered the Afreet to remove Carathis from his presence, and continued immersed in thought, which his companions durst not disturb.
Carathis, however, eagerly entered the dome of Soliman, and, without regarding in the least the groans of the Prophet, undauntedly removed the covers of the vases, and violently seized on the talismans. Then, with a voice more loud than had hitherto been heard in these mansions, she compelled the Dives to disclose to her the most secret treasures, the most profound stores, which the Afreet himself had not seen. She passed by rapid descents known only to Eblis and his most favoured Potentates, and thus penetrated the very entrails of the earth, where breathes the Sansar, or icy wind of death. Nothing appalled her dauntless soul. She perceived, however, in all the inmates who bore their hands on their heart, a little singularity not much to her taste. As she was emerging from one of the abysses, Eblis stood forth to her view, but, notwithstanding he displayed the full effulgence of his infernal majesty, she preserved her countenance unaltered, and even paid her compliments with considerable firmness.

===Chinese===


这样一篇话激起的义愤，瓦提克沮丧得竟无从发作，只吩咐那阿弗里特把卡拉希丝带离自己眼前，随即又沉入深思，同伴们也不敢惊动他。

卡拉希丝却一头热地进了苏莱曼的穹殿，全不理会先知的呻吟，毫无惧色地揭开铜瓮的盖子，一把抢过那些护符。接着，她用这些府邸里从未听过的响亮嗓音，逼着迪夫们向她献出最隐秘的宝藏、最深藏的库府——连那阿弗里特自己都未曾见过。她又循着唯有埃布利斯和他的宠臣权贵才知晓的捷途疾降而下，直贯大地的脏腑——那里吹着桑萨尔（Sansar），又名死亡阴风。什么都吓不住她那剽悍的灵魂。只是她看出，凡按着心口的居人身上都有一样小小的不寻常之处，很不对她的口味。她正从一个深渊里冒出头来，埃布利斯现身在她眼前；他尽管使出了地狱威严的全部光焰，她却面不改色，甚至还相当镇定地向他寒暄致意。

===Original===


This superb monarch thus answered:
“**Princess**, whose knowledge and whose crimes have merited a conspicuous rank in my empire, thou doest well to employ the leisure that remains, for the flames and torments which are ready to seize on thy heart, will not fail to provide thee with full employment.”
He said this, and was lost in the curtains of his tabernacle.

===Chinese===


这位煊赫的君王这样答道：

“公主，你的学问和你的罪孽，已挣得我帝国里一个显赫的席位；你把剩下的闲工夫好好利用起来吧——那即将攫住你心的烈焰与酷刑，自会给你派足够多的活计。”

说罢这话，他便隐入圣殿的帷幔深处。

===Original===


Carathis paused for a moment with surprise, but, resolved to follow the advice of Eblis, she assembled all the choirs of Genii, and all the Dives, to pay her homage. Thus marched she in triumph through a vapour of perfumes, amidst the acclamations of all the malignant spirits; with most of whom she had formed a previous acquaintance. She even attempted to dethrone one of the Solimans, for the purpose of usurping his place, when a voice, proceeding from the Abyss of Death, proclaimed:
“**All is accomplished!**”
Instantaneously, the haughty forehead of the intrepid princess became corrugated with agony; she uttered a tremendous yell, and fixed﻿—no more to be withdrawn﻿—her right hand upon her heart, which was become a receptacle of eternal fire.
In this delirium, forgetting all ambitious projects, and her thirst for that knowledge which should ever be hidden from mortals, she overturned the offerings of the Genii; and, having execrated the hour she was begotten, and the womb that had borne her, glanced off in a whirl that rendered her invisible, and continued to revolve without intermission.

===Chinese===


卡拉希丝怔了片刻，但既已打定主意听从埃布利斯的指教，她便召集起精灵的歌队和所有的迪夫，来向她致敬。她就这样凯旋而行，穿过香雾氤氲，在一切凶神的欢呼声中——其中大半她早就是旧相识了。她甚至想把某一位苏莱曼拽下宝座，自己取而代之；正在此时，一个出自死亡深渊的声音宣告：

“一切都成了！”

霎时间，这无畏公主那高傲的额角因剧痛而扭作一团；她发出一声骇人的长嚎，右手死死按上自己的心口——再也拿不下来了——那颗心已成了一座永火的牢狱。

在这癫狂之中，她忘却了一切野心的图谋，也忘却了那永远该对凡人掩藏的知识的渴念；她掀翻了精灵们的供献，咒罢生她的时辰、咒罢生她的母腹，便化作一阵旋风倏然而逝，再不见踪影，只在那里不住地旋转，永不停歇。

===Original===


At almost the same instant, the same voice announced to the Caliph, Nouronihar, the five princes, and the princess, the awful and irrevocable decree. Their hearts immediately took fire, and they at once lost the most precious of the gifts of heaven﻿—hope. These unhappy beings recoiled, with looks of the most furious distraction. Vathek beheld in the eyes of Nouronihar nothing but rage and vengeance; nor could she discern aught in his but aversion and despair. The two princes who were friends, and till that moment had preserved their attachment, shrunk back, gnashing their teeth with mutual and unchangeable hatred. Kalilah and his sister made reciprocal gestures of imprecation; whilst the two other princes testified their horror for each other by the most ghastly convulsions, and screams that could not be smothered. All severally plunged themselves into the accursed multitude, there to wander in an eternity of unabating anguish.
Such was, and such should be, the punishment of unrestrained passions, and atrocious actions. Such is, and such should be, the chastisement of blind ambition, that would transgress those bounds which the Creator hath prescribed to human knowledge, and by aiming at discoveries reserved for pure intelligence, acquire that infatuated pride, which perceives not the condition appointed to man is, **to be ignorant and humble**.
Thus the **Caliph Vathek** who, for the sake of empty pomp and forbidden power, hath sullied himself with a thousand crimes, became a prey to grief without end, and remorse without mitigation; whilst the humble and despised **Gulchenrouz** passed whole ages in undisturbed tranquillity, and the pure happiness of childhood.

===Chinese===


几乎在同一瞬间，那同一个声音向哈里发、努罗尼哈、五位王子和那位公主，宣布了可畏而不可挽回的敕令。他们的心顿时燃起火来，登时便失去了上天最珍贵的恩赐——希望。这些不幸的人各自退缩，面上是狂乱到极点的神情。瓦提克在努罗尼哈眼中只看见狂怒与复仇；她在他眼中也只看得出憎嫌与绝望。那两位素来交好、直到此刻还维系着情谊的王子，退缩开去，咬牙切齿，怀着彼此永不改变的仇恨。卡利拉（Kalilah）和他的妹妹互相做出咒诅的手势；另外两位王子则用最狰狞的抽搐和压抑不住的尖叫，表达彼此的憎怖。众人各自一头扎进那受诅的人群，在那里游荡，忍受永无休止、永不清减的痛楚。

放纵的情欲与凶残的行为，过去受到、今后也该受到这样的惩罚。盲目的野心——那要僭越造物主为人类知识划定的界限、妄图染指只留给纯粹神明的奥秘、从而染上狂悖骄矜的野心——如今正受着、今后也该受着这样的惩戒；那骄矜使人再看不见，人被指定的本分原是：无知而谦卑。

于是，哈里发瓦提克——为了虚浮的排场和违禁的权能，以千般罪孽玷污了自己——沦为无尽悲愁、无可宽解之悔恨的猎物；而谦卑的、被人轻视的古尔申鲁兹，却在不受搅扰的安宁中、在童年纯净的幸福里，度过了整整一个个世代。
